# Changelog

All notable changes to trac-mcp-server will be documented in this file.

Format based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/).

## [Unreleased]

### Added
- **`wiki_file_push` takes an `encoding` for a server-side `file_path`, `trac-mcp wiki-push` an `--encoding` flag, and a leading UTF-8 BOM is no longer taken from a file into a page (Trac /trac_mcp_server #245).** A file saved with a BOM kept a leading U+FEFF in the stored page, and a caller who knew the file's codec could not say so (detection guessed). The BOM is removed when a FILE is decoded: the tool's `file_path`, and the CLI's local file or stdin. Inline `content` is stored as sent (inline writes are byte-for-byte by design), so an older CLI, or another client that decoded a BOM'd file itself, still stores the U+FEFF. One decoder, `decode_bytes` (`read_file_with_encoding(path, encoding=None)` reads the bytes and calls it), removes exactly one leading U+FEFF on every path (strict UTF-8, charset-normalizer, an explicit codec) and never a second one: a decoder that already consumed the BOM (`utf-8-sig`, `utf-16`, `utf-32`, a charset-normalizer match that reports a BOM) is not stripped again. The reported encoding of a BOM'd UTF-8 file stays `utf-8`, so no "not UTF-8" warning fires, and an interior U+FEFF is left alone. With `encoding` given it is probed with `"".encode(name)` (unknown and non-text codecs such as `base64` and `rot13`, and names such as `undefined`, raise; `b"".decode(name)` does not, CPython returns early on empty input), the bytes are decoded strictly with it and never fall back to detection, and the canonical codec name is returned (`latin-1` is `iso8859-1`). The tool refuses `encoding` with inline `content` and answers an unknown codec or a decode failure with a `validation_error` naming the codec and byte offset, never the content, before any Trac call; an explicit encoding adds no "not UTF-8" warning. `trac-mcp wiki-push --encoding CODEC` decodes the local file (or stdin) the same way, exits 2 on a bad codec or decode failure before any network call, and still sends the text as `content`, with no `encoding` key, so it works against an older server. The CLI has one local text read for files and stdin: stdin to `wiki-push` and `detect-format` (and the `ticket-*` body commands) is now read as bytes, so CRLF and lone CR are kept exactly like a file's (`printf 'a\r\nb' | trac-mcp wiki-push P -` sends `a\r\nb`, with or without `--encoding`), and a stdin body that is not UTF-8 is now detected with the "decoded as ..., not UTF-8" warning like a file instead of refused as `stdin is not valid UTF-8`. `ticket-comment-edit --expect-file` is the exception to the BOM strip: it is compared with the stored comment verbatim, so a legacy comment that begins with U+FEFF still matches its own `ticket-pull`. Connected MCP sessions keep the old schema until they reconnect. Tests: `tests/test_file_handler.py` (`TestReadFileBom`, `TestReadFileExplicitEncoding`, `TestExactlyOneBom`, `TestCodecValidationCatchesValueErrors`), `tests/test_mcp/tools/test_wiki_file.py` (`TestPushEncoding`), `tests/test_cli_client.py` (`TestWikiPushEncoding`, `TestTicketCommentEdit`).
- **Quoted-target bracket shapes in prose (`x['KEY']`, `d["key"]`) now get Trac's `!` escape, so they stay text instead of becoming wiki links (Trac /trac_mcp_server #241).** Trac reads `['Page']` and `["Page"]`, with an optional label, as a link to a wiki page, so Markdown such as `app.config['SERVER_NAME']` or `d["key"]` rendered a missing-page link and the write gate refused the push. A mistune inline rule (`_parse_quoted_bracket`, registered before `link` in `_MISTUNE_PLUGINS`, so the converter and `find_list_item_splits` parse alike) turns the shape into a `quoted_bracket` token, and the renderer writes it as `app.config!['SERVER_NAME']`; chained subscripts (`x['a']['b']`) are each escaped, and so is a call on one (`handlers['get'](req, resp)` is `handlers!['get'](req, resp)`, which Trac shows as text). Real links first: the rule hands the position to mistune's own link parser, so an inline link (`["T"](url)`) and a reference link the document defines (`["T"][id]`, `["T"]`, `["T"][]`) convert as before. The label follows Trac's grammar (whitespace other than a newline, then at least one non-`]` character: `x['a' ] y` is text, `x['a'  ] y` a link with an empty label). The rule fires only where mistune sees prose, so code spans and code blocks, link and image destinations, link titles, autolinks, reference definitions and a backslash-escaped bracket are untouched, and so is a shape right after a `scheme:...` run that nothing closes (`https://e.com/a['b']`, `HTTPS://E.COM/['a']`; Trac never forms a bracket link there, so an escape would only add a visible `!`). A `!` typed in Markdown is a literal character, not an escape, so `Wow!['a']` is written `Wow!!['a']`. A first version stashed the shape on the raw source and corrupted link destinations, titles, autolinks and reference links; the rule replaced it. `tests/test_converter_quoted_bracket_links.py` covers each case against master's output, with one `--run-live` row on `/trac_test`.
- **A code fence whose language Trac cannot highlight is now written as `{{{#!text lang=<name>}}}`, and `html` fences show as code (Trac /trac_mcp_server #240).** A Markdown fence naming a language Trac has no processor for (`pf`, `graphql`, `mermaid`, `vue`, `jsonc`, ...) used to become `{{{#!NAME}}}`, which Trac renders as an error box. A name Trac runs as a processor (`div`, `span`, `comment`, `default`) ran that processor instead of showing the code, so a ` ```div ` or ` ```comment ` fence changed what the block meant. Now `#!NAME` is written only for a language Trac highlights (after the existing `bash` -> `sh` style mapping, compared lowercased and written lowercased); any other name becomes a plain `{{{#!text lang=<name>}}}` block, with the name kept for a reader, and `convert_with_warnings` (so `convert_preview`) adds one `conversion_warning` per distinct such name, in order of appearance. A name with characters that could break the opener is written as `{{{#!text}}}`; only the first word of the info string counts, and only its leading run of Trac's processor-name characters (`[\w+-][\w+/-]*`, what Trac reads after `{{{#!`) is the language, so a suffix such as `python{1,3}` or `python:file.py` is dropped (`{{{#!python`), and `c#` and `f#`, which Trac would read as `c` and `f`, are written as `csharp` and `fsharp`. The warning names the opener it wrote. Reading back, `{{{#!text lang=pf` exports as a `pf` fence and `{{{#!text/html` as an `html` fence. `html` fences are mapped to `text/html`: shown as code, not rendered (`#!html` is never emitted from Markdown). The allow-list is `converters/fence_languages.py`, generated by `scripts/probe_fence_languages.py`, which renders each Pygments lexer alias (plus `text/plain`, `text/html` and `svg`) through the instance's `wiki.wikiToHtml` and keeps the names that give highlighted code and no error box; rerun it after a Trac or Pygments upgrade on the host. The new public `fence_processor` in `converters.common` holds the rule. `tests/test_converter_fence_languages.py` covers it, with one `--run-live` row on `/trac_test`. Three existing rows that pinned pass-through of a name the list rejects (`foo`, `tracwiki`, a bare `div` fence in a list item) now expect the `#!text lang=` form, and the `html` identity row of `markdown_to_tracwiki_lang` moved to its own `text/html` row.
- **`markdown_to_tracwiki` now decodes CommonMark entity and numeric character references in text (Trac /trac_mcp_server #239).** `&amp;`, `&copy;`, `&#x20;` and `&#87;` in Markdown prose used to pass through unchanged, so Trac showed them literally; they are now decoded to the characters they name, before the CamelCase escape (so a reference that spells a CamelCase word still gets its `!`). A reference is decoded only when the result cannot form TracWiki markup: letters, digits, space, `&`, `<`, `>`, `"` and characters from U+00A0 up (except U+2028 and U+2029) are decoded; markup-significant ASCII (`&#35;`, `&#124;`, `&#123;`, `&#33;` and the like), control characters and line-breaking characters stay as typed, as they rendered before. A named reference is decoded only when `name;` is in HTML5's entity table, and a number follows CommonMark (0, above U+10FFFF or a surrogate gives U+FFFD). A reference at the start of a line has its leading spaces stripped after decoding, because two would make Trac render a blockquote. Also left literal, as in CommonMark: semicolon-less legacy names (`&amp`), unknown names (`&notanentity;`), anything inside code spans and code blocks (which never reach the text renderer), and a backslash-escaped `\&`. `tests/test_converter_char_refs.py` covers each case.
- **`pyright src/` is now clean and gates CI at zero errors (Trac /trac_mcp_server #236).**
  - **Install**: `pyright[nodejs]==1.1.408` is in the `dev` extra, pinned exactly like ruff for the reason in #184 (a newer pyright adds checks and turns a green gate red). The wheel bundles pyright's JavaScript and `nodejs-wheel-binaries` supplies node, so it runs offline once installed; `PYRIGHT_PYTHON_IGNORE_WARNINGS=1` stops the wrapper's newer-version check calling PyPI.
  - **Gate**: `ci.sh` has a `=== Type check ===` section after Lint, and `.github/workflows/ci.yml` a `typecheck` job (same checkout, setup-python 3.12 and `pip install -e ".[dev]"` steps as the others). Both run `pyright --pythonpath "$(command -v python)" src/`; pyright exits non-zero on any error, so the gate is zero errors with no script. `src/` only.
  - **Config**: `pyrightconfig.json` gained `"include": ["src"]`, so a bare `pyright` checks `src/` only. It keeps `venvPath` and `venv` (`.venv`) so a bare `pyright` or an editor in the main checkout finds the dependencies; `.venv` exists in neither Actions nor a worktree, so there callers pass `--pythonpath`, which wins (pyright prints a one-line `venv .venv subdirectory not found` notice and still exits 0 at zero errors). `pythonVersion`, `extraPaths` and `reportMissingModuleSource` are unchanged, and no rule was turned off.
  - **The 81 errors**: 26 real fixes (narrowing, `isinstance`, return and parameter annotations, `from bs4.element import NavigableString`, `from lxml.etree import strip_elements`, `getattr(sys, "_MEIPASS", None)`, mistune's `Plugin` protocol for `flavor_plugins`, `_require` returning a tuple type); 35 in `core/client.py` fixed by typing `_rpc_request` and `_parse_xmlrpc_value` as returning `Any`, the truthful type of an XML-RPC value, instead of the inferred union of every wire type; 14 in `html_to_markdown.py` fixed by `converters/_markdownify_typing.py`, which declares what markdownify's bundled `.pyi` omits (`options`, `DefaultOptions`, the `convert_*` methods, `chomp`, `abstract_inline_conversion`) under `TYPE_CHECKING` and is a plain re-export at runtime; 3 `typing.cast`s where the runtime type is known (the SDK's request, mistune's AST with `renderer=None`, a bs4 class list); 3 errors suppressed by 2 rule-scoped `# pyright: ignore[...]` comments with the reason on the line (`link_targets.py`: `html.parser.attrfind_tolerant` and `tagfind_tolerant`, stdlib privates with no stub; `version.py`: the build-generated `._release`). No behaviour changed: the full suite passes unchanged.
  - **Real bugs found**: none. The "None is not iterable" and "folded"/"strip" on None errors were all guarded paths pyright could not follow, so none needed a regression test.
  - **Seeded**: with `x: int = "s"` added to `version.py`, the `ci.sh` type-check line printed the `reportAssignmentType` error, `1 error`, and exited 1; reverted.
  - **Out of scope**: `tests/` and `scripts/` (2861 errors, mostly mocks) are not checked and not part of the gate.
  - **Review fixes**: (1) `converters/flavors.py` imported `mistune.plugins.Plugin` at runtime, but `Plugin` exists only from mistune 3.1.0 and pyproject allows `mistune>=3.0.0`; the import is now under `TYPE_CHECKING` (with `from __future__ import annotations`), and `test_the_module_imports_on_a_mistune_without_plugin` loads the module against a `mistune.plugins` lacking `Plugin` (it failed with `ImportError: cannot import name 'Plugin'` before). (2) The `typecheck` job installs with `-c constraints/deploy.txt`, so pyright reads the stubs of the shipped dependency versions (the #184 drift); the combination resolves (`pip check` clean) and gives 0 errors. (3) `venvPath`/`venv` are back in `pyrightconfig.json` (see Config). Also: `_ANY` removed from `html_to_markdown.py` (`None` in the table means any class is allowed); `assert filename` in `wiki_attachment_put` is an explicit `validation_error` that survives `python -O`; dead `AttributeError` dropped from `version.py`'s except; `Request` in `mcp/server.py` imported under `TYPE_CHECKING`. The `posted is None` half of the `comment_folded` condition in `ticket_write.py` is redundant at runtime but kept, because pyright needs it to narrow `posted`.
- **A token Trac rejects gets a 401 from the MCP endpoint on its next request (Trac /trac_mcp_server #205).** #202 answered 401 only for a missing bearer token or a JWT past its `exp`. Any other token Trac refuses (a revoked JWT, an expired opaque token, a token that lapses during a tool call) still reached the client as HTTP 200 with Trac's 401 as tool-result text, so a client that refreshes its token only on a `401` from the endpoint (LibreChat) never did. That request cannot be answered 401: the daemon uses `StreamableHTTPSessionManager(json_response=False)`, and the SDK sends `200` and the SSE headers before it dispatches to the tool. So `TracClient` takes an `on_unauthorized` callback, called by `_rpc_call`, before it raises, on a `401` that came straight from the RPC URL for a bearer-token client (not a `401` on a page the link probes or the render check fetch, not one after a redirect, never for Basic auth; an exception in it is logged at DEBUG and dropped). `OidcClientCache` passes one that records the token in a reject list: SHA-256 digests only, at most 1024 in FIFO order, each for 300 s, with an injectable clock, and the token's cached client is dropped without being closed. The TTL is short so that a transient `401` from the identity provider or a proxy locks a token out for at most five minutes, while a token that really is revoked is marked again on its next refused call. `OidcTokenRequiredMiddleware` then answers the next request carrying that token with `401` and `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer error="invalid_token"`, logged at INFO without the token. The refused call itself still returns the error as tool-result text, and tool-result text is unchanged. `/healthz` stays exempt
  - **Seeded**: with the middleware's rejected-token check disabled, `TestRejectedTokenGetsA401OnTheNextRequest::test_the_refused_call_is_200_and_the_next_request_is_401` and `test_the_rejection_is_logged_without_the_token` fail (200 instead of 401); with the callback also reported from a session-wide `requests` response hook, `test_a_401_on_a_non_rpc_get_does_not_fire_the_callback` and both `test_a_401_after_a_redirect_does_not_fire_the_callback` cases fail
- **Every tracked file is now checked for internal addresses and names, and the existing ones were scrubbed (Trac /trac_mcp_server #217).** The internal Trac and daemon addresses in the test constants, the `convert_preview` and `render_check` fixtures and their manifests, `ci-live.sh` and this file became `trac.example.org` / `mcp.example.org` placeholders, the private-range test address became the documentation address `192.0.2.5`, and two home-directory examples became `/home/USER`. `tests/test_no_internal_names.py` walks `git ls-files` and holds files under `tests/fixtures/` to `FIXTURE_RE` and every other file to the narrower `CORE_RE` (both in `tests/internal_names.py`, shared with the #208 fixture check). An explicit allowlist, keyed on path and the whole matched token (not just the matched prefix) with a reason each, names the few legitimate hits, and a second test fails on an allowlist entry that matches nothing.
- **`--version` of `trac-mcp`, `trac-convert` and `trac-mcp-server` now names the commit the build was made from (Trac /trac_mcp_server #230).**
  - **The three CLIs**: each prints its existing prefix and then `<version>+g<12 hex>` (`.dirty` appended when the build tree differed from the commit), or `<version> (commit unknown)` when there is no stamp (an editable install, a source run, a build from an sdist). Before, a PyInstaller binary printed a bare `3.0.0` whatever it was built from, so a stale binary and a fresh one could not be told apart. The new `format_cli_version` in `version.py` reuses `format_server_version`, so the form matches `serverInfo.version` and `/healthz`.
  - **Frozen builds**: a PyInstaller binary never runs `setup.py`'s `build_py`, so it has no `_release.py`. `build.sh` now computes the sha with `setup.release_sha` (the function the wheel stamp uses) and bundles it as the data file `trac_mcp_server_RELEASE_SHA` through `--add-data` in both PyInstaller calls; `release_sha()` reads it from `sys._MEIPASS` when the `_release` import fails and the process is frozen, and accepts only 40 hex with an optional `-dirty`. The file is written to a `mktemp -d` directory removed on exit, never into `src/`: a file there would be picked up by editable installs and wheels as a stamp that nothing refreshes. The `_release` path is tried first and is unchanged.
  - **`build.sh` fails on a missing stamp**: when a sha was bundled and a binary's `--version` still says `(commit unknown)`, `verify_binary` prints an `ERROR` and fails the build. Outside a git checkout `build.sh` prints one line saying the binaries will report "commit unknown" and bundles nothing. The check is for the exact commit (`+g` and the first 12 hex of the stamped sha), not just the absence of "commit unknown", and the "Build successful!" banner now prints only after both binaries pass. Windows builds are not exercised (CI is Linux-only): on a `.exe` a stamp that does not land warns instead of failing.
  - **`cli_version()` and validation**: the three parsers build their string through one `version.cli_version()`, so tests patch `release_sha` in one place. `release_sha()` holds the `_release` value to the same `40 hex[-dirty]` shape as the data file (`"unknown"` or a short value gives None), and reads the data file as ASCII so an undecodable file gives None instead of crashing every CLI at parser build.
  - **Tests**: `tests/test_cli_version.py` runs each parser's `--version` with `release_sha` patched to a sha, a dirty sha and None; `tests/test_version.py` covers `format_cli_version` and the frozen fallback (valid, dirty, malformed, missing, unreadable, not frozen, `_release` first). Seen failing first: the CLI tests failed with the old bare version (`trac-mcp-server version 3.0.0` against `...+g0123456789ab`) and the formatter and frozen tests with `assert '3.0.0' == '3.0.0+g0123456789ab'` and `assert None == '0123...'`; the build check was seen failing with `--add-data` removed while the sha was set, and again with a different sha in `RELEASE_SHA` after the data file was written.

### Changed
- **Batch handler tests no longer depend on the order concurrent items reach the mocked `run_sync_limited` (Trac /trac_mcp_server #247).** `tests/test_mcp/tools/test_ticket_batch.py` fed the mock a positional `side_effect` list and then asserted which ticket got which result, but the batch handlers run items concurrently through `gather_limited`. In practice only create could reorder: with a non-empty description each item first awaited `check_write`, whose render hops through a thread. Delete has no await before the call, and the only update test that passes a comment (`test_batch_update_stores_comment_as_written`) is a single item, so in the multi-item delete and update tests nothing suspended before the call (only create's non-empty description reached the thread hop); those tests were order-safe by accident and are keyed the same way so a future await cannot break them. The mocks now answer by argument (summary for create, ticket id for delete and update), and the description-storage test compares the set of `(summary, description)` pairs instead of call positions. The create and update test classes also set `config.write_gate = False`: on the `MagicMock` config the gate defaulted on, every render failed on the mock, and `check_write` turned the failure into an UNCHECKED note, so the tests relied on that error being swallowed. Test-only; `src/` is unchanged. Seen failing first: under a scratch conftest that delays `check_write` so items reach the mock in reverse order, the old file failed `test_batch_create_success`, `test_batch_create_stores_each_description_as_written` and `test_batch_update_partial_failure` (`5 not found in [6]`). That conftest delayed `check_write`, which the gate-off setUp no longer reaches, so it does not prove the final file; the evidence for that is the new `test_batch_create_pairs_ids_when_completions_reverse`, which delays the keyed mock itself so the calls finish in reverse input order (asserted, so the test shows it reordered) and checks each created entry pairs its own id and summary: seen failing with a call-order mock (`[('Ticket A', 3), ('Ticket B', 2), ('Ticket C', 1)] != [('Ticket A', 1), ('Ticket B', 2), ('Ticket C', 3)]`), passing with the keyed one.
- **`docs/reference/mcp-tools.md` now has a section for every registered tool, 54 of 54 (Trac /trac_mcp_server #242).**
  - **Sections added**: 22, each with the spec's description, a parameter table from its `inputSchema`, the permission it needs, success and error responses taken from the handler, and an example call. `ticket_attachment_put`/`get`/`list`/`delete`, `ticket_comment_reply`/`edit`/`delete`/`history` and `ticket_component_*` / `ticket_enum_*` (create, list, delete) sit after `ticket_batch_update`; `wiki_get_history`, `wiki_diff` and `wiki_conventions` after `wiki_recent_changes`; `wiki_attachment_put`/`get`/`list`/`delete` after `wiki_file_detect_format`; `timeline_search` after `milestone_delete`. The four `ticket_comment_*` tools and `timeline_search` say they need the `tracrpc_comment` Trac plugin and that without it the call answers `method_not_available` with an install hint. The page intro no longer calls the page a subset.
  - **Test**: `tests/test_readme_tool_count.py` also checks the page against the registry: every registered name has a `## <name>` heading line, and every `## ` heading is either exactly a registered tool name or one of an explicit allowlist of prose headings ("Instance Routing", "Write Gate Status"), so `## old_tool (removed)` and ``## `old_tool` `` fail and are listed (a heading is not judged by its shape). Lines inside fenced code blocks are ignored, so a fenced `## x` neither hides a missing section nor counts as an extra one. Seen failing first: 22 names missing (the list above); after review, each check was seen failing on a seeded copy of the page (a real heading replaced by a fenced one, `## old_tool (removed)`, ``## `old_tool` ``, an unlisted prose heading).
  - **Known failures, documented not fixed**: `ticket_enum_create` with `enum_type=version` fails with a server error (`create_enum` sends `(name, 0)`; TracXMLRPC's Version create takes an attributes dict), Trac /trac_mcp_server #251. `ticket_attachment_list` and `wiki_attachment_list` answer "has no attachments" for a missing ticket or page instead of `not_found`, Trac /trac_mcp_server #253. Each section says so.
  - **Review fixes**: `ticket_comment_history` documents the `not_found` error a missing ticket gets (fault code 404 from `Ticket(env, id)`); `timeline_search` examples show the absolute `url` values the plugin renders; the attachment examples' `bytes` equal the decoded length of the base64 shown; the `wiki_diff` example hunk is `@@ -1,7 +1,8 @@`, three context lines each side as the default `context` gives.
  - **Instance Routing**: the section said every tool accepts `instance`. It now carries the caveat the README already had: over HTTP with OIDC per-user auth (`TRAC_MCP_OIDC_RPC_URL`) the argument is not offered and calls go only to the configured endpoint (`server.py` skips `with_instance_param` there), and results carry no instance echo.
- **The README now lists all 54 registered tools, a test holds it to the registry, and two stale files are deleted (Trac /trac_mcp_server #226).**
  - **Deleted**: `docs/project/PROJECT.md` and `test-report.md`; nothing linked to either. `test-report.md` is a generated artifact (`scripts/test_trac.py --output ./test-report.md`), so `/test-report.md` is now in `.gitignore`.
  - **README**: `## Available Tools` said 46 while the registry (`[PING_SPEC] + ALL_SPECS`) holds 54. Added the eight missing: `ticket_comment_reply`, `ticket_comment_edit`, `ticket_comment_delete`, `ticket_comment_history` (new `### Ticket Comments (4)`), `timeline_search` (`### Timeline (1)`), and `convert_preview`, `ticket_render_check`, `wiki_render_check` (`### Previews and Render Checks (3)`). The comment and timeline tools carry a note that they need the `tracrpc_comment` Trac plugin (`plugins/README.md`) and return a `method_not_available` error with an install-the-plugin hint without it.
  - **Test**: `tests/test_readme_tool_count.py` reads the names from the built registry (`build_registry` over `[PING_SPEC] + ALL_SPECS`, as a list) and parses only the `## Available Tools (N)` section, once. It checks that the registered names have no duplicates, that N equals the registered count, that the backticked names in the section's table rows are exactly the registered names (a failure lists what is missing and what is extra), that no row is listed twice and the row total equals N, that every `### ` line in the section reads `### Title (k)`, that each k equals that subsection's row count, and that the k values sum to N. Seen failing first: against the old README the count and name checks failed (46 vs 54, eight names missing), and a temporarily wrong subsection count made the third fail; after the review fixes, each added check was seen failing on a seeded input (a duplicated row, a heading of 55, a stray `### Extras` line, a duplicate registered name) and passes restored.
  - **Review fixes**: the README no longer says the comment and timeline tools answer MethodNotFound without the plugin (they return a `method_not_available` error with an install-the-plugin hint). The sentence that every tool accepts an `instance` argument now carries the exception for HTTP with OIDC per-user auth, where the argument is not offered. The `docs/reference/mcp-tools.md` intro no longer claims 28 tools in six categories with a per-category count list; it says the page documents a subset and points to the README list and to Trac /trac_mcp_server #242.
  - **overview.md**: the `mcp-tools.md` row no longer claims "All 27 MCP tools". `mcp-tools.md` itself is incomplete; those gaps are filed as Trac /trac_mcp_server #242.

### Fixed
- **`literal_markup_in_render` no longer refuses code-like prose such as `handlers['get'](req, resp)` that the source escaped with Trac's `!` (Trac /trac_mcp_server #250).**
  - **The rule**: the converter writes `handlers['get'](req, resp)` as `handlers!['get'](req, resp)` (#241; #249 does the same for `x[0](a, b)`), Trac consumes the `!` and renders plain text, but the Markdown-link pattern matched `['get'](req, resp)` in the rendered prose, so the write gate blocked correct text. The check now takes the stored TracWiki (`_check_literal_markup_in_render(facts, tracwiki)`; every caller of `build_warnings` already passed it, and `""` skips nothing) and counts the `[...](...)` spans the source escapes with `!`, after blanking code fences and inline code spans (a `!` in code is not an escape). A render match equal to an escaped span is skipped; a Markdown link that failed to convert never carries the `!`. A hand-written TracWiki write that escapes the bracket gets the same treatment.
  - **One escaped occurrence skips one render match**: the spans are a `Counter`, so `a![t](u v) b [t](u v)` rendered as `a [t](u v) b [t](u v)` still blocks on the second copy.
  - **The scan resumes one character after a skipped match**, not at its end: the label class accepts `[`, so a skipped `[i and [docs](x y)` can contain a real link, and that link is still tested. Only the Markdown-link pattern changed; the other four are untouched, and the evidence and suggestion are those of the reported match.
  - **Replaces a first attempt** that skipped a match glued to an identifier character, `]` or `)`: review showed it hid a real link after `arr[i and ...`, after an element boundary (`prose_text` joins text across elements with no separator: `<a>docs</a>[spec](my file.md)`, `<br />`, table cells) and for any TracWiki-source write, which has no converter behind it. Keying on the source's `!` has none of those holes.
  - **Recall measurement**: over 3,158 documents of both stores the rule skips 0 matches, and the same 5 documents fire before and after. Known remaining false positive, filed separately: the `Project` page, where `handlers['get'](req, resp)` is a TicketQuery macro's output (a ticket summary) and is not in the page source. `tests/test_preview_checks.py` covers the skipped and still-blocking shapes (including each hole above) and one end-to-end row (convert, recorded render, gate).
- **Numeric bracket shapes in Markdown prose (`x[0]`, `rows[12]`, `x[1:3]`, `x[deadbeef]`) now get Trac's `!` escape, so they stay text instead of becoming changeset or log links (Trac /trac_mcp_server #249).**
  - **What is escaped**: Trac reads a bracket holding a number as a changeset or log link (`trac/versioncontrol/web_ui/changeset.py` and `log.py`), so `x[0]` in Markdown rendered a link and the write gate refused the push. `_NUMERIC_BRACKET_LINK_RE` mirrors both patterns as one regex (an optional InterTrac-shaped prefix, then a number, a long hex id, a range `1:3`, `1-3`, `0,1`, or an id with a `/path`, `?query` or `#fragment`), with two line-local changes: the prefix whitespace and every `[^\]]*` stop at a newline. The inline rule `_parse_numeric_bracket` writes each match as `x![0]`. It is the #241 mechanism extended: both rules now come from one `_bracket_rule`, registered before `link` in `_MISTUNE_PLUGINS`, and `_parse_quoted_bracket` behaves as before. Real links first: an inline link `[1](url)`, a reference link `[1][r]` and a shortcut reference `[1]` that the document defines convert as before.
  - **Every match is escaped, including shapes Trac resolves to text**: `x[-1]`, `x[i+1]`, `x[.5]` and an unknown prefix (`x[abc 12]`, `x[ 12]`, `x[abc12]`) match Trac's pattern but its link resolver yields text. Measured on Trac's renderer, the `!` form of every matching shape renders plain text with no visible `!`, so the escape there is harmless noise in the stored text, and the regex stays an exact mirror with no exclusion rule to justify.
  - **Deliberately left**: shapes Trac's pattern does not match and does not link (`x[0, 1]`, `x[1.5]`, `x[0:]`, `x[:3]`, `x[n-1:n]`, `x[cafe]`); a shape right after a `scheme:...` run (`http://e.com/a[0]`, which Trac's URL pattern takes); code spans and blocks, link and image destinations, titles, autolinks and reference definitions (the rule fires only where mistune sees prose). A shape inside a link label (`[a [0] b](http://x)`) gets NO escape, unlike #241: Trac does not consume the `!` inside a bracket-link label (`[http://x a ![0] b]` renders the label `a ![0`), and the bare form links nothing there.
  - **Linear time**: `_NUMERIC_BRACKET_LINK_RE` is written so a bracket with no closing `]` costs a constant times its length (a first version took 49 s on `x[1/` + `?#` * 2000 and 32 s on `x[` + `a` * 20000). The changeset alternative's three optional tails (`/..`, `?..`, `#..`) are one `(?:[/?#][^\]\n]*)?`, which accepts the same strings because each tail runs to the first `]`; the log alternative keeps its own `[/?]` tail. The InterTrac-shaped prefix is bounded to 32 characters: only a known InterTrac prefix makes a link (an unknown one renders as text on Trac), and known prefixes are short, so a longer run left bare still renders as text. The zero-width space of Trac's range grammar is written `\u200b`. `tests/test_converter_numeric_bracket_links.py` converts five pathological inputs under a 2 s bound.
  - **Real links before the scheme-run guard**: both the quoted (#241) and numeric rules used to return None for a shape right after a `scheme:...` run before trying mistune's link parser, so `Note:[1](http://x)`, `see:[1].` with `[1]: http://x` defined, and (a #241 regression) `Note:['a'](http://x)` lost their link. The link parser now runs first; the scheme-run check applies only to the escape fallback, so `http://e.com/a[0] z` is still left as text.
  - **Separate ticket**: Trac's `r12` revision-log and `{1}` report shapes in prose are not handled here.
  - **Tests**: `tests/test_converter_numeric_bracket_links.py`; the `x[0]` row that pinned this defect moved out of `tests/test_converter_quoted_bracket_links.py`, and the default-flavour row of `test_a_spaced_citation_becomes_a_link` now expects `see ![1] (https://example.com/a) now`. Seen failing first: 22 escaped rows against the unmodified converter.
- **The standalone GET SSE stream is now completed when the daemon shuts down, so uvicorn no longer logs `ASGI callable returned without completing response.` for it on every deploy (Trac /trac_mcp_server #248).** `sse-starlette` 3.2.0, which the MCP SDK serves SSE through, patches `uvicorn.Server.handle_exit`, so on SIGTERM `sse_starlette.sse.AppStatus.should_exit` becomes True. Every open `EventSourceResponse` then returns from `_listen_for_exit_signal`, which cancels its task group (including `_stream_response`) before it sends the final empty `more_body: False` message, so `__call__` returns with the response started and not complete and uvicorn logs the ERROR on `uvicorn.error`, and the client sees an incomplete chunked body. `_SessionManagerASGIApp.__call__` (`mcp/http_app.py`) now wraps `send` to note whether `http.response.start` and a final `http.response.body` (falsy or absent `more_body`) went out, and sends `{"type": "http.response.body", "body": b"", "more_body": False}` through the original `send` (DEBUG log) only when ALL of these hold: the request is a GET (the standalone notification stream, where a clean end loses nothing); `handle_request` returned normally; a response started and did not complete; `AppStatus.should_exit` is True. The send is bounded by `_SHUTDOWN_CLOSE_TIMEOUT` (1.0 s, `anyio.move_on_after`): it runs after sse-starlette's task group has finished, so nothing else cancels it, and a stalled client would block uvicorn's `send` in its flow-control drain until TCP gave up; on timeout the stream is left incomplete (DEBUG log), uvicorn logs its ERROR for that one stuck stream and closes the transport, as before the fix.
  - **Not completed**: a POST. Its SSE stream carries a tool call's result and the SDK returns normally after the cut, so completing it would end an aborted `tools/call` as a clean 200 with no JSON-RPC result and the client could wait until its own timeout. A POST cut on shutdown keeps uvicorn's ERROR, an honest signal that a request was aborted. An unfinished response outside shutdown is a real bug and also keeps the ERROR; a non-http scope is a plain pass-through.
  - **Exceptions**: the SDK catches exceptions from the response on its SSE paths (`logger.exception("Error in standalone SSE response")` for GET, `"SSE response error"` for POST in `mcp/server/streamable_http.py`) and returns normally, so an exception does not necessarily propagate out of `handle_request`. A GET stream that died of a send error during shutdown therefore also reaches the completion: the SDK has already logged the error, and the closing message is attempted on the same connection, which uvicorn drops silently once it has disconnected.
  - **Dependency**: `sse-starlette>=1.6.1` is now declared in `pyproject.toml` (`constraints/deploy.txt` already pins 3.2.0). The `AppStatus` import is guarded: if a release moves or drops it, the shutdown check is False and the completion goes inert instead of failing daemon startup; the real-substrate test is what catches that.
  - **Removal**: drop the completion once sse-starlette completes the response itself on shutdown.
  - **Tested**: `tests/test_mcp/test_http_shutdown_streams.py`. Unit tests drive `_SessionManagerASGIApp` with a fake session manager and a recording `send`: a cut GET stream is closed with exactly one appended closing body on shutdown; nothing is appended with `should_exit` False, for a completed response (`more_body` False or absent), for a response never started, for a cut POST or DELETE, when `handle_request` raises after starting (the exception propagates), or when `AppStatus` is unavailable (patched to None); a closing send that blocks forever returns after the timeout (patched to 0.05 s, under `anyio.fail_after` so a regression fails instead of hanging). Two real-substrate tests run the real daemon (`run_http`) in a subprocess and send SIGTERM: with two MCP sessions each holding a standalone GET stream, the child's output has `StreamableHTTP session manager shutting down` and no `returned without completing response`, and each client stream ends cleanly; with the same two GET streams plus a POST `tools/call` blocked in its handler (response headers received, SSE 200), the GET streams end cleanly, the POST stream ends with an `httpx` error, and the output has exactly one `returned without completing response`.
  - **Seeded**: with the `http_app.py` change reverted (first version of this fix), the GET real-substrate test failed with two `ERROR:uvicorn.error:ASGI callable returned without completing response.` records (the shutdown line is present), and both client streams ended in `httpx.RemoteProtocolError: peer closed connection without sending complete message body (incomplete chunked read)`; two unit tests failed too. With the GET check removed (completing on any method), `test_sigterm_leaves_an_aborted_post_as_an_error` fails (the POST stream ends cleanly, `post_end` is None) and so do the two non-GET unit cases; with the timeout removed (`move_on_after(None)`), `test_stalled_close_is_bounded` fails with `TimeoutError` from `fail_after` instead of hanging. With the final code the file passes, five runs in a row.
  - **Reproduced**: on mcp 1.29.1 (the dev venv) and on the jail's pins, mcp 1.26.0: 3 open streams give 3 records, 4 give 4, 0 give 0.
  - **Declined**: a log filter that demotes the record, as `_SseWriterCloseFilter` does for #169. The cause is ours to close: the response really is left incomplete, and the client sees it, so the fix completes it instead of hiding the log line.
- **`trac-mcp` now runs an `.mcp.json` entry's `headersHelper` to get its bearer token (Trac /trac_mcp_server #254).** Claude Code lets an entry carry `"headersHelper": "<command>"` (a command that prints `{"Authorization": "Bearer <token>"}`) in place of a static `headers` block, so the token can live in `pass` instead of a shell profile. The CLI read only `headers.Authorization`, so for such an entry it sent no token, printed `no bearer token sent (TRAC_MCP_AUTH_TOKEN is unset)` and got a 401 (exit 3); everything that posts through the CLI (`trac-exact.py`, the scribes, `/finish`) failed in a project that moved to a helper.
  - **How the helper runs**: as Claude Code runs it. The string goes to a shell as written (the CLI does not expand `${VAR}` in it), in the `.mcp.json`'s directory, with the caller's environment plus `CLAUDE_CODE_MCP_SERVER_NAME` and `CLAUDE_CODE_MCP_SERVER_URL` (the expanded url), no stdin, and a 10 s limit (`HEADERS_HELPER_TIMEOUT_S`). The helper runs in its own process group, which is killed on a timeout and on any interruption (Ctrl-C no longer orphans it); after a timeout the wait for its pipes is bounded (1 s), so a descendant that called `setsid` and escaped the kill cannot hold the CLI until it exits. Stdout must be a JSON object of string headers. A `headersHelper` that is not a string (e.g. an argv-style list) is exit 3 (`must be a command string`); an empty or whitespace string counts as absent. `--token-env` skips the helper entirely and still overrides everything.
  - **Precedence**: a header the helper prints overrides a static `headers` entry of the same name (names compared case-insensitively), so the CLI uses the same Trac identity as the session's MCP calls; a static Authorization is used only when the helper prints none. An Authorization the helper prints but leaves empty or blank is exit 3 (`printed an empty Authorization header`), never a fallback to the static one. Only Authorization is sent, as before; the value must still be `Bearer <token>`.
  - **Failures** are exit 3 and never include the helper's stdout, which is a credential: a non-zero exit says `exited <code>` plus the last non-empty line of stderr (control and non-printable characters, ESC included, replaced by `?`, then cut to 200 characters); a timeout, a command that cannot start, and stdout that is not a JSON object of string values each have their own message, and the invalid-output one names the problem without quoting the output (not `json.JSONDecodeError`'s text, which quotes input).
  - **Message**: with no token from an `.mcp.json` entry, `main` now says `no bearer token sent (<file> (trac-http) supplies no Authorization header)`; the `TRAC_MCP_AUTH_TOKEN is unset` wording stays for `--url` and `TRAC_MCP_URL`.
  - **Tests**: `TestHeadersHelper` in `tests/test_cli_client.py` runs real helper commands (token, environment and cwd the helper sees, precedence both ways, `--token-env` not running it, a non-zero exit with a secret on stdout, invalid / non-object / non-string output, a missing command, a helper that sleeps past a shortened timeout, one whose `setsid` child outlives the timeout, a real SIGINT during the wait, empty / non-string `headersHelper` and Authorization values, an ANSI escape in stderr), and `TestRealSubstrate::test_headers_helper_token_reaches_the_server` pushes a page through `main()` to the real uvicorn server with the token coming only from the helper. Seen failing first: the helper-token test returned `None` instead of `fake-helper-token`, and the end-to-end test got `HTTP 401 Unauthorized` from the server.
- **Installed daemons no longer log `Cannot find pyproject.toml for version comparison` at startup, and installs now compare against the package metadata (Trac /trac_mcp_server #244).** `check_version_consistency()` looked for `pyproject.toml` three directories above `version.py`, a path that exists only in a source checkout or an editable install; from site-packages it names `lib/pythonX.Y`, so every non-editable install (the jail release venvs, the workstation CLI, the PyInstaller binaries) logged that warning from `_report_version` on every start and compared nothing. It now takes the first source that exists. (1) The source manifest (found through `Path(__file__).resolve()`, so a strict editable install's symlink tree still reaches the checkout), when it can be read and its `[project].name` is `trac-mcp-server`: a mismatch says `__version__` in `src/trac_mcp_server/__init__.py` and `[project].version` in pyproject.toml disagree and must be bumped together (the old advice to rebuild with `pyinstaller trac-mcp-server.spec` is gone: that spec is not in the repo and this branch is reachable only from a checkout). A manifest for another project, one with no name, or one that cannot be read (no tomllib/tomli on Python 3.10, a read or parse error) does not count and falls through to (2); an unreadable one adds `; pyproject.toml at <path> could not be read: <error>` to whatever (2) or (3) returns and does not decide the verdict, so a foreign or broken manifest at that path can no longer fail the check. It goes first because an editable install's dist metadata goes stale (the dev venv's says `2.2.0` while the code says `3.0.0`), so metadata-first would raise a false mismatch in every checkout. (2) Otherwise the installed distribution's metadata (`importlib.metadata`): a match logs `Version verified: <v> (installed package metadata)`; a mismatch is `False` and names both versions and says the installed package's metadata and its `__version__` disagree, meaning the build it came from had mismatched version literals or the install was modified (reinstalling cannot fix that, so it does not say to). Versions are compared in PEP 440-normalized form when `packaging` imports (it is not a declared dependency; otherwise the raw strings), because `importlib.metadata` reports `3.1.0-rc1` as `3.1.0rc1`. (3) With neither (a PyInstaller binary with no bundled metadata) it returns `True` with `Version <v>: no source manifest or package metadata to compare against`, so the server logs it at info, not as a warning. The signature and the caller are unchanged; the three branches live in `_compare_versions`, which takes the runtime version, the manifest path and the metadata lookup. `tests/test_version.py` drives each branch with real files in `tmp_path`, and builds the project into a wheel (`pip wheel --no-deps --no-build-isolation`, from a copy of the project in `tmp_path`), unpacks it, and runs `check_version_consistency()` in a subprocess that imports the unpacked copy; the build runs with `--no-index` and `--disable-pip-version-check` (no network), and the test skips with the reason when a build tool is missing or the installed setuptools is older than the `[build-system]` floor. The old `test_consistency_import_error`, which patched the function and then called the real one, is gone. Seen failing first: the installed-layout test failed against the unmodified function with `Cannot find pyproject.toml for version comparison`.
- **The adversarial-input test now bounds how CPU time grows with input size and caps each full-size run, not a one-second wall clock, so host load no longer fails it (Trac /trac_mcp_server #243).**
  - **Why**: `test_adversarial_input_finishes_quickly` asserted each of 12 inputs converts in under 1.0 s of wall-clock time. `many backtick runs` takes 0.68 s on a quiet host, so any load failed it, and with 12 busy processes on an 8-core host even CPU time (`time.process_time`) reached 1.01 s, so CPU time alone did not fix it.
  - **What changed**: `ADVERSARIAL` is now `name -> (flavor, build, n)`, where `build` is the repeated unit string (or a function of the repetition count, for the indented-lines and backtick-runs cases) and `build(n)` is the string the old table held (checked byte for byte against the old table for all 12). `test_adversarial_input_grows_linearly` (same parametrize ids) has `_growth` time the converter with `time.process_time` on `build(n // 8)` and on `build(n)`, take the minimum of 3 runs of each with the inputs built outside the timed region, and return the ratio in one place (refusing a zero small-size time with a message, not a `ZeroDivisionError`). It asserts the ratio is below `GROWTH_LIMIT = 16`, and that the full-size time is below `CPU_CEILING_S = 5.0`, which catches a constant-factor slowdown the ratio cannot see; a full-size run over the ceiling ends its repeats, so a catastrophic regression costs one slow run. The messages name the ratio, both times and both repetition counts.
  - **Measured** (minimum of 3 runs): the ratio of all 12 cases is 6.6-9.0 on a quiet host and under 12 busy processes (linear is about 8), and a deliberately quadratic function (`s[:i].count("a")` for every 4th `i`, on `"ab"*N`) gives 61-62 in both conditions. A time of linear + quadratic parts, with the quadratic share r of the time at n/8, has ratio (8 + 64r)/(1 + r), so a limit of 16 fails any r above about 17% and growth faster than about n^1.33, with a margin of about 1.8x over linear. The first limit of 20 let a quadratic term of that size through: `s[:i].count("a")` every 50 characters added to `many backtick runs` gave 17.0. The ceiling is about 5 times the slowest full-size run seen, 1.01 s for `many backtick runs` under that load.
  - **Seeded**: `test_growth_ratio_flags_a_quadratic_function` and `test_growth_ratio_passes_a_linear_function` pin `_growth` on a quadratic and a linear function (small size about 5 ms, above timer noise), and two more tests pin the zero-time refusal and the early stop. Not committed, on `many backtick runs` only (the other 11 cases passed): the converter wrapped in `s[:i].count("a")` every 50 characters failed the ratio (`CPU time grew 16.2x for 8x the input (limit 16x): 0.0851s at 2500 repetitions, 1.3797s at 20000 repetitions`); the converter run 10 times per call failed the ceiling (`the full-size run (20000 repetitions) took 6.89s of CPU time (ceiling 5.0s); growth was 8.9x`).
  - **Load run**: with 12 busy processes the new tests passed (16 passed) in three runs, with ratios 6.6-8.1 and a largest full-size time of 1.00 s; the old wall-clock test under the same load had failed `many backtick runs`.
- **A dropped connection or lost login in the single-read fallback now raises instead of returning every item as absent (Trac /trac_mcp_server #238).**
  - **What changed**: `_batched_multicall` falls back to a one-call-per-item reader after any multicall failure, timeout or dropped connection included, and its three readers (`_fetch_tickets_singly` behind `get_tickets`, `_fetch_milestones_singly` behind `get_milestones`, `_fetch_wiki_singly` behind the wiki search's page cache) each caught `Exception` per item and skipped it. They now share one `TracClient._fetch_each(items, read_one)`: a `requests.RequestException` (connection error, timeout, an `HTTPError` such as 401/403/5xx) propagates, because it says the server cannot be reached, or refuses the session, for every item; any other exception from reading one item (a `Fault` for a missing or not viewable ticket, milestone or page, an `ElementTree.ParseError` from a reply holding a control character, an `IndexError` or `ValueError` from decoding one value) skips that item, as before #238. The wiki reader was not named in the ticket but had the same defect, so it is fixed with the other two; its record that fails the shape check is still left out. The docstrings of `_batched_multicall`, the three readers and their callers say so.
  - **User-visible effect**: with the connection down or the session logged out, a read of N items made N single calls in a row, each waiting up to `rpc_timeout`, and returned success with every item missing; `milestone_list status=open` answered `0 milestone(s) (open): none` (with `details`, a "failed to load" warning for every name). Now a connection error (`ConnectionError`, `ConnectTimeout` included), a 401 or a 403 from the multicall raises at once, with no single read; a read timeout of the multicall costs the multicall's timeout plus one single read's (the first single read raises if the server is still unreachable) instead of N. `milestone_list` and the wiki search answer the transport or auth error (`server_error` through the registry). `ticket_check_list` (`mcp/tools/ticket_list.py`, `_load`'s `one()`) deliberately catches per chunk and reports the chunk's ids in `failed_ids`, so there a transport error shows as `failed_ids`, not as `server_error`.
  - **Tests**: `tests/test_client_single_read_errors.py` runs each case against all three readers: a multicall `ConnectionError`, `ConnectTimeout`, 401 or 403 `HTTPError` raises with zero single-read calls; a multicall read `Timeout`, a 413, 500 or 502, a `ParseError` or a non-multicall `Fault` still falls back and recovers every item; a connection error or 401 from the first single read raises after exactly one single-read call, also when it is a later batch (earlier batches succeeded) that fails; a `ParseError`, `IndexError` or `ValueError` from one item's single read leaves only that item out; and `milestone_list` through `handle_call_tool` with a real `TracClient` whose RPC raises `ConnectionError` returns an error result, not `0 milestone(s)`. Seen failing first on the unchanged code: the 12 transport and 401 single-read cases (`DID NOT RAISE`) and the tool case (`isError` False and `0 milestone(s) (open): none`).
  - **Review fixes**: (1) `_fetch_each` first skipped only a `Fault` and let `ElementTree.ParseError` propagate, but a `ParseError` (or an `IndexError`/`ValueError` from `_parse_xmlrpc_value`) can come from ONE bad item, such as a page or description holding `\x1b`, which xmlrpc emits raw and `ElementTree.fromstring` rejects; that failed the whole read where before #238 it left one item out. Now only `requests.RequestException` propagates and everything else skips the item. (2) `_refresh_wiki_cache` dropped deleted pages before the fetch but cleared the per-scope indexes after it, so a fetch that raised left a stale index able to return a deleted page; the clear is now in a `finally`. (3) `_batched_multicall` no longer falls back after a multicall `ConnectionError` or a 401/403 `HTTPError`, which the single reads would hit too after another timeout each; every other multicall error still falls back. Seen failing first on the commit before these fixes: the 9 skip cases (3 errors by 3 readers: the error propagated), the 12 global-error cases (`DID NOT RAISE`: single reads made and succeeded) and the wiki index case (the deleted page `Beta` still found), plus the tool test's assertion that no single read is made. The later-batch case and the fall-back cases pass before and after; they pin behavior.
  - **Unchanged on purpose**: a per-item `Fault` in the single read still leaves just that item absent (the others are returned), a multicall failure with healthy single reads still recovers every item, and faults inside a successful multicall reply are handled as before; tests in the same file pin all three. A lost login that surfaces as a per-item permission `Fault` is still skipped item by item, because it cannot be told apart from a real per-item permission denial.
- **A blockquote directly after a list item's nested list now stays in the item (Trac /trac_mcp_server #197).** `markdown_to_tracwiki("- a\n  - b\n  > q\n")` wrote the quote as a blank line plus text at the continuation indent (` * a\n   * b\n\n     q`), the pre-#145 form, and Trac ends the list at the blank line. This lifts the #145 limit ("a quote after a nested list keeps master's blank line").
  - **Write leg**: `_item_block_separator` has a new answer, `"marker"`, for a quote whose previous run is a nested list. The `>` lines go at the item's MARKER column (`len(indent)`, the column of `*` or `1.`), with no blank line, and every non-blank line of the quote is indented that way. Measured on `/trac_test` (Trac 1.6): a citation line at the marker column closes every nested list whose marker is deeper, and the quote lands in that item after the nested list, the next item a sibling. That held for several lines, text after the quote at the item's text column (it stays in the item, after the blockquote), a second sibling quote joined by the usual `>` gap line at the continuation indent (one blockquote, two paragraphs), quote then text then quote, an ordered item, and depth 1 (the quote goes in `b` after `c`'s list, with a nested `> >` and an empty `>` line). The item is innermost again afterwards, so the writer resets its nested-list flag, as it does after #176's "between" paragraph, and what follows is handled as after any citation: ` * a\n   * b\n > q\n   t\n * c`, or ` * a\n   * b\n > q\n   >\n   > r\n * c`
  - **Advisory**: `find_list_item_splits` asks the writer's `_item_block_separator` for a quote run directly after a nested list and, on `"marker"`, resets its walk with no finding, the way it treats #176's paragraph. A code block, a table or a refused text run directly after a nested list is still a finding, and a block after the quote is no longer directly after a nested list, so `- a` / `  - b` / `  > q` / fence is silent
  - **Tests**: in `tests/test_converter_quote_in_list.py` the three tests that pinned the old bytes (`test_quote_after_a_nested_list_is_unchanged`, `test_second_quote_after_a_nested_list_is_unchanged`, `test_text_after_a_quote_after_a_nested_list_is_unchanged`) were rewritten for the new form, with seven new rows (the ticket's tight shape, several lines, a nested quote and an empty `>` line, an ordered parent, depth 1, two sibling nested lists, an ordered item numbered 10) and a quote-text-quote row, each also asserting `find_list_item_splits(...) == []`; four rows were added to the live `LIVE` table and one live test for the depth-1 shape (not run offline). Seen failing first on the unchanged code: the three rewritten tests and all seven rows with the old bytes (` * a\n   * b\n\n     q`, a blank line and the quote at the continuation indent instead of ` > q`), the quote-text-quote row, and `tests/test_list_item_split_advisory.py::test_quote_directly_after_a_sublist_then_code_is_not_a_split` with a `blockquote` finding for item `a`. With only the advisory change reverted, the same rows and the advisory test fail again
  - **Unchanged on purpose**: a quote after a nested list that is NOT directly after it keeps master's blank line and indent. That covers a quote after a code block that attached to the nested item (` * a\n   * b\n   {{{\n   c\n   }}}\n\n     q`) and after a passed-through line, both pinned by test; `"marker"` is returned only when the previous run is the nested list itself, and `list_seen` with any other previous kind still answers `"blank"` (unmeasured)
  - **Review fixes**: `"marker"` is refused (the pre-#197 blank-line form, and the advisory's finding for the item, come back, byte-identical to master) in two cases measured on `/trac_test`. (1) The nested list's rendered text holds a blank line: `- a\n  - b\n    - c\n\n    = H =\n  > q\n- d\n` had been written ` * a\n   * b\n     * c\n\n     = H =\n > q\n * d`, where Trac has already closed every list at the blank line, so ` > q` rendered outside the list and `d` started a new one. (2) The nested list's text ends with a citation line: `- a\n  - b\n    > inner\n  > q\n- d\n` was written ` * a\n   * b\n     > inner\n > q\n * d`, and Trac merged `q` into b's blockquote (one `<p>` "inner q" inside b's `<li>`). `find_list_item_splits` now keeps the rendered text of the most recent nested list and passes it to the same `_item_block_separator` call, so the advisory shares the writer's decision instead of a placeholder. Two measured-correct shapes are pinned (bytes, advisory `[]` and two `LIVE` rows): `-\n  - b\n  > q\n- d\n` (Trac keeps `q` inside the empty first `<li>`, after b's list, `d` a sibling) and `- a\n  - b\n  > q\n  ```\n  c\n  ```\n- d\n` (the `<pre>` stays in `a`, after the blockquote). The #178 comment on `continuation_indent` now names the marker-column exception, `_item_block_separator`'s unit table has `"marker"` rows, and the depth-one live test asserts the `</ul>` is there before splitting on it.

## [3.0.0] - 2026-11-16

Everything on `master` since 2.2.0 (2026-07-26). A major release: the
inline tools, an advisory code, the http file tools and the converter API
changed incompatibly, as listed first below.

**Breaking**
- The inline ticket and wiki tools store and return TracWiki byte for byte; their `format` parameter is gone (Trac #69).
- `target_check_skipped` is replaced by three codes (Trac #83).
- Over http, the file tools refuse server-side paths by default; `file_access: local` restores them (Trac #111).
- TracWiki to Markdown needs Trac's rendered HTML: offline conversion, the regex `tracwiki_to_markdown` converter and `trac-convert --unknown-macros` are removed, and `auto_convert` raises for that direction (Trac /trac_mcp_server #210, #214). `TracClient.get_wiki_page_with_metadata` is removed (#189).

**Highlights**
- Streamable HTTP transport with bearer-token auth (Trac #26), and routing to other Trac projects through an `instance` argument on every tool (Trac #18).
- Markdown export through Trac's own rendered HTML, with front matter and a push refusal, a link-rewrite map, `--format html` and attachment download (Trac /trac_mcp_server #209 to #215).
- Markdown flavour profiles for text pasted from ChatGPT and Gemini (Trac /trac_mcp_server #216).
- The link probe no longer sends the caller's Trac credentials to a host an authored link names (Trac #118).

Entries under **Fixed** that name `tracwiki_to_markdown` or the read leg
fixed the regex converter during this cycle; #214 removed that converter
later in the same release, so those fixes do not ship as such.

### Security
- **The link probe no longer sends the caller's Trac password to any host an authored link names (Trac #118).** `TracClient` sets `session.auth`, so `requests` attached `Authorization: Basic ...` to every request on the session, whatever its host. The write gate (#64), `convert_preview` and the render checks probe every InterTrac-dispatcher-shaped href, on any host, so a document containing `[http://attacker.example/intertrac/wiki%3AX x]` handed the caller's username and password to `attacker.example`. Callers scoped by #110's allowlist were protected; every unscoped caller on the LAN daemon was not
  - **Enforced at the session, not at each call site**: `core/scope.py`'s `ScopedSession.send()` sees the first request and every redirect hop `requests` follows. It removes `Authorization` from any request off the client's own origin (`requests`' own `should_strip_auth` rule: same hostname, scheme and port, plus the http -> https upgrade on default ports), and, for a client with an allowlist, refuses an out-of-scope request unsent with `DestinationOutOfScope`. Any other code that uses `client.session` is covered too, not only the probe
  - **A foreign target is still probed, anonymously** (the operator's choice, option 1(a)). A public Trac stays checked; a private one answers 401/403, which reports as unchecked, never as a broken link. On the cached corpus of 1,020 documents from both stores, the only foreign origin is `trac.edgewall.org` (210 hrefs), and all 210 still resolve anonymously
  - **`preview/targets._get` lost its hand-followed redirect loop**; scope helpers moved from `preview/targets.py` to `core/scope.py` (still importable from `targets`). Every session now follows at most 5 redirects, the bound the scoped loop had, so a redirect loop is an unchecked target rather than 30 requests
  - **One derivation, mapped everywhere**: the session and the probe's up-front pass share `core.scope.scope_for(config)`, and a refusal raised inside any tool is reported as `instance_not_allowed` by the tool dispatcher, not as `server_error`. An IDN `trac_url` keeps its credentials (its own origin is compared in prepared form), and a scoped client's http base also admits its https upgrade on the default ports, the one origin change the credential rule allows
  - **Tested on real sockets**: two `http.server` listeners stand in for the client's own Trac and a foreign host. On `master`, the foreign listener received `Basic` decoding to the test's `username:password`. Seeded mutants (no credential strip; no scope refusal) each fail their tests

### Added
- **Markdown flavour profiles for the write leg: `flavor` (`commonmark`, `chatgpt`, `gemini`) on `wiki_file_push` and `convert_preview`, `--flavor` on `trac-convert` and `trac-mcp wiki-push` (Trac /trac_mcp_server #216).**
  - **Why**: text pasted from another bot is not plain CommonMark, and the converter corrupted it silently. A copy of a rendered Gemini page has no fences, so a Python `# comment` became a level-1 heading, `__name__` became bold, indentation was lost and `"/blog/<int:post_id>"` became a link, with no warning beyond "HTML tags detected". ChatGPT's math (`\\(R\_{\text{OK1}}\\)`, backslashes doubled, underscores escaped) was stored as literal LaTeX source, its favicon citation residue stayed as a link to a favicon, and cited links carried `utm_source=chatgpt.com`
  - **What**: a new module `converters/flavors.py`. A profile is text pre-normalisers, which skip code (fenced and indented blocks, code spans; every match is checked against `_code_mask`), plus extra mistune rules; `TracWikiRenderer` gains `inline_math` / `block_math`. `flavor` defaults to `commonmark`, which changes nothing: its output is byte for byte what it was, and no existing test or golden changed. An unknown name is a `ValueError` naming the valid ones (a `validation_error` in the tools)
    - **`chatgpt`**: inline `\(x\)` and `\\(x\\)` become a code span (`{{{x}}}` when the formula holds a backtick); in the doubled form only, Markdown escapes inside the formula are removed (`R\_{x}` is `R_{x}`; `\text` stays). A paragraph that is wholly `\[x\]` or `\\[x\\]` (one line or several) becomes `{{{#!latex ... }}}` (no math processor on this Trac; `latex` is accepted as Pygments highlighting, checked on `/trac_test`); in a table cell or a sentence it is a code span. A favicon link `[image](https://www.google.com/s2/favicons?domain=D...)` plus the label line after it (`name` or `name+N`) becomes `[name](D)`, or `[D](D)` with no label line (a label of several words needs its `+N`, so a prose line after the favicon is not eaten). `utm_*` query parameters are removed from link targets, autolinks and bare URLs, the other parameters kept in order
    - **`gemini`**: a line that is exactly a language name, the code, then a line `Use code with caution.` becomes a fenced block, the code byte for byte (code that holds a fence line is left unrebuilt), with one `gemini: rebuilt N code block(s) ...` warning; `•` lines become `- ` items; `[N] (https://...)` becomes `[N](https://...)`
    - **The default hints**: a line `Use code with caution.` outside code adds a warning saying to use `flavor="gemini"` (`--flavor gemini`); nothing is switched automatically
    - **Tools and CLIs**: a flavour implies a Markdown source, so `format="auto"` (inline content included, which is otherwise TracWiki) converts as Markdown and `format="tracwiki"` is a `validation_error`. `convert_preview`'s Markdown-source checks and `wiki_file_push`'s indentation-loss and list-split checks read the flavour-normalised text. `trac-convert --flavor` is Markdown -> TracWiki only (any other direction is a usage error, exit 2; `--from auto` reads Markdown); `trac-mcp wiki-push --flavor` sends `flavor` only when it is not the default, sends `format="markdown"` for `--format auto`, rejects `--format tracwiki` (exit 2), and against a daemon whose strict schema refuses `flavor` exits 1 with "the server does not know --flavor: it predates Markdown flavour profiles", nothing written. Ticket tools take no flavour (they never convert): a paste for a ticket goes through `trac-convert --flavor ...` first
    - **Connected MCP sessions keep the old `wiki_file_push` / `convert_preview` schema (no `flavor`) until the daemon is deployed and they reconnect**
  - **`html` is fenced as `text/html` in the `gemini` profile, not `html`**: the converter passes ```` ```html ```` through as `{{{#!html`, and Trac's `html` processor renders the markup instead of showing it (measured on `/trac_test`: no `<pre>`, the source vanishes into the page). This is true of any Markdown ```` ```html ```` fence and is left as it is for every other input
  - **Tested**: `tests/test_converter_flavors.py` (one block per rule with a row showing `commonmark` leaves the same input as today's output: doubled-form math with `\_` and `\*`, a backtick in a formula, multi-line display math, two bracket lines in a row, `\(` inside a fence and a code span; the favicon with and without a label and in code; `utm_*` removal keeping the other parameters, an escaped `\&`, a sentence-ending period; the Gemini block with `# comment`, `__name__`, indentation and a tab byte for byte, a label with no closing marker unchanged, code holding a fence line left alone, `•` and `[N] (url)` in and out of code; the hint; an unknown flavour), `tests/test_converter_flavors_fixtures.py` (two synthetic fixtures in `tests/fixtures/flavors/`, a ChatGPT dialogue and a Gemini page copy, each against a golden TracWiki file read line by line), `tests/test_mcp/tools/test_flavor_tools.py` (schemas, the `format` conflict, inline `auto` content converting, the checks reading normalised text) and `tests/test_cli_flavors.py` (the usage errors, the old-daemon refusal, one pass through the real server stack), plus one live row per fixture, `test_live_the_fixture_renders_without_a_system_message`: `convert_preview` against `/trac_test` renders with no `system-message`, and each Gemini code block's text is in a `<pre>` exactly
  - **Seeded**: written first and seen failing on the unchanged code: 134 failed, 13 passed and 2 (the live rows) were skipped, of the first draft's 149 items (every rule row, `ImportError` or `TypeError` for the `flavor` argument, `unrecognized arguments: --flavor`); the 13 that passed were the four per-file lint rows and nine rows that pin what must not change (no hint inside code, `commonmark` warnings, ticket tools without `flavor`, inline `auto` without a flavour still TracWiki)
  - **Review fixes**: (1) A `Use code with caution.` marker took the FIRST language line above it, so an earlier lone `python` line and the prose after it were fenced as code; it now takes the nearest one, only when both start at column 0, the label is outside code and no fence line (backtick or tilde) lies between them (so code that holds a fence line is not rebuilt, and a plain fence always closes the block). (2) The pre-normalisers rewrote indented code blocks and multi-backtick or multi-line code spans, which `_markdown_verbatim_mask` does not mask; they now use `_code_mask`, built on `common.blank_code_fences` and `blank_inline_code_spans` (new `multiline` argument, off by default so the preview checks are unchanged) plus tilde fences and CommonMark's indented-code rule (4 columns or a tab, not a paragraph continuation, not list-item content). That also ends the import of `markdown_to_tracwiki` from `flavors`. (3) A backtick can no longer be part of a URL, so a URL before a span across a line break leaves the span intact. (4) ChatGPT math holding a link-shaped word (`\[f:A\]`, all four delimiter forms and display paragraphs) was cut up by the `[word:x]` link stash before the math rule ran; math is now protected before that stash and put back after. (5) The inline math scan was quadratic on unclosed openers (a 64 KB line of `\(a ` took 7.6 s, 8000 x `x \[a ` 2.9 s, against 0.15 s and 0.07 s for the default flavour): a body is at most 1000 characters and never crosses another opener, display math at most 5000 and never crosses another opener line, and the Gemini pass computes its mask once and scans back only to the previous marker; both inputs now take 0.16 s and 0.09 s, and a timing row guards each adversarial shape under 1 s. (6) A display paragraph may follow any whitespace-only line, not only a blank line of one space or tab. (7) A URL that runs into a code span is cut at the span and still stripped. (8) `trac-convert --check-trac --flavor X` is a usage error (exit 2) instead of ignoring the flavour. (9) `ConversionResult.normalised_source` carries the flavour-normalised source (None for the default), and `wiki_file_push`, `convert_preview` and `trac-convert` use it instead of normalising a second time. Written first and seen failing on bbc2b56: 36 new rows, one per finding or more; no golden changed
  - **Second review fixes**: (1) A `utm_*` strip deleted the text glued after a link (`[a](https://a.com/?utm_source=x)abc` became `[a](https://a.com/`): a URL is now bounded as a URL is. A Markdown link target ends at its closing `)` (balanced parentheses inside are part of it), a bare URL or autolink is ASCII URL characters only (no whitespace, backtick, `<>"` or non-ASCII) and ends at an unbalanced `)` or trailing punctuation, and the text after it is never touched. (2) A `|` in inline math split a table cell; inline math is now hidden from the parser like a code span (a stash placeholder, rendered when the output is restored) while a display paragraph is still a `{{{#!latex}}}` block. (3) The nearest-label rule paired a marker with a language word directly above it, giving an empty `{{{#!python}}}` and leaving the code as Markdown, with "rebuilt 1" in the warning; a label needs a line of code between it and the marker, so that word is code and the search goes on upward, and no empty block is emitted. (4) The mask blanked backtick fences before tilde fences, so `~~~` around a ```` ``` ```` line hid the rest of the document; one scanner, `common.blank_fenced_code`, now handles both fence characters in one pass (closing only with the same character and at least the opening length, up to 3 columns of indent, more under a list item) and replaces the backtick-only pass in `_code_mask`. `blank_code_fences` is unchanged: its other callers (the preview checks, `wiki_conventions`) read TracWiki with its looser rule and keep their measured behaviour. (5) The hint accepted an indented `Use code with caution.` the rebuild ignores; both read column 0. (6) A percent-encoded favicon `domain=https%3A%2F%2Fexample.com` is decoded before its scheme is checked. (7) This entry no longer names `_markdown_verbatim_mask` for the mask or promises a longer fence. (8) The hint builds no code mask unless the marker text is in the source. Written first and seen failing on 15bd865: 23 new rows; no golden changed
  - **Third review fixes**: (1) The Gemini rebuild checked that the label was outside code but not the marker, so a `Use code with caution.` line inside a `{{{ }}}` block or a multi-line code span gave a nested block with a stray closer; a marker inside code is skipped. (2) A formula holding both a backtick and `}}}` has no inline TracWiki form (a code span cannot hold the backtick, `{{{ }}}` ends at the `}}}`); that one occurrence is left as written, read as CommonMark reads it, and a warning names its first 40 characters. (3) A CRLF or lone-CR paste got no favicon rewrite (the patterns end at `$`, which never matches before `\r`); the flavours fold CRLF and CR to LF first, the default keeps its bytes. (4) The favicon fallback `<https://D>` was read as an HTML tag by the converter's tag check and raised a false warning; it is now the link `[D](https://D)`, labelled by the decoded domain without its scheme. (5) A `Reuters +2` label kept its trailing space (`[https://reuters.com Reuters ]`); it is stripped. (6) A Gemini label is matched case-insensitively but was fenced as written (`{{{#!Python`, `{{{#!JSON`); it is lowercased first, and `html` is `text/html` in any case. (7) Measured, not changed: a 1 MB paste (the fixtures repeated) converts in 1.21 s under `chatgpt` against 0.96 s for `commonmark` (1.27x) and 1.14 s under `gemini` against 0.80 s (1.43x), under the 2x bound, so the code mask is still built per pass. Written first and seen failing on fe49371: 19 rows; no golden changed
- **`--assets DIR` (and `--assets-url PREFIX`) on `trac-mcp wiki-pull` and `trac-convert --from-wiki` download the Trac attachments an exported page references and rewrite their links (Trac /trac_mcp_server #215).**
  - **Why**: Trac renders every attachment as an absolute URL, so an exported page (the blog case: `[[Image(x.png)]]`) still loaded its images from Trac, which a published site cannot rely on, and fetching them was a manual `attach-get` per file followed by editing every link
  - **What**: one module, `cli/assets.py`, independent of the transport, used by both CLIs. It finds URLs in URL positions only (a Markdown link or image target, an `<autolink>`, a quoted `src`/`href`) whose path is `<instance>/raw-attachment/...` or `<instance>/attachment/...` of realm `wiki` or `ticket` (host ignored, absolute or root-relative, a query or fragment skipped, external URLs never matched), fetches each attachment once, and points both of its URLs at one local file. The path is decided by the attachment alone: `DIR/<page path>/<file>` for a wiki attachment (page `blog/my-post`, `x.png` -> `DIR/blog/my-post/x.png`) and `DIR/ticket-<N>/<file>` for a ticket one, so two pages' `diagram.png` never meet and a re-run never moves a file; only a clash (one path for two attachments, e.g. a wiki page named `ticket-5` and ticket #5; paths differing only in letter case; or a file where a directory is needed) keeps the later one's URLs, with a warning. URLs inside code (fenced blocks, code spans, `<pre>`/`<code>`/`<script>`/`<style>`/`<textarea>`) are neither fetched nor rewritten. On a root-served instance an absolute URL must be on the instance's own host. Links are the relative path from the output file's directory, or `PREFIX/<path>` with `--assets-url` (`--assets static/images --assets-url /images` links `/images/blog/my-post/x.png`)
  - **Order**: every attachment is written to a temporary sibling of its final file (`.<name>.tmp-<random>`, same directory) as soon as it is fetched, then the output file the same way, and only then are they all renamed into place, assets first and the output last. A fetch or write that fails (anything but not found or too large) deletes every temporary file and any directory made for them, so neither assets nor output change; all attachments go over one MCP session (`trac-mcp`), and both CLIs check the `-o` file and every planned path before fetching. A file with identical bytes is left alone and a changed one overwritten. A missing attachment warns, keeps its URLs and leaves the exit at 0. The report is one stderr line, `Saved N attachments to DIR (M unchanged, K missing)`
  - **Rules**: Markdown and HTML only (TracWiki is a usage error, exit 2); `--assets-url` needs `--assets`; on `trac-convert`, `--assets` needs `--from-wiki ... --to md`. `link_rewrites` is untouched and runs first: a map covering the attachment links wins for those links, and images are never mapped, so they are still localised. The MCP tools and config-file keys are out of scope
  - **Tested**: `tests/test_cli_assets.py` (the module: the golden `blog_scope_verification` image shape in Markdown and HTML, another host, root-relative, nested parent, ticket realm, query/fragment, external image, percent-encoded names, the per-page layout and its case clashes, code regions in Markdown and HTML, the root-instance host check, refused segments, nested output directory, `--assets-url`, identical and changed bytes), `tests/test_cli_client_assets.py` (the real server stack over a fake Trac: two attachments and one missing, `--format html`, tracwiki and flag-pairing refusals, a server error and a transport failure on the second fetch leaving nothing) and `tests/test_cli_convert_assets.py` (the same through the fake client), plus one live row, `test_live_wiki_pull_assets_saves_the_attachment_and_relinks`, on a `/trac_test` scratch page with a generated PNG
  - **Seeded**: written first and seen failing before the feature existed: the module's rows (`ImportError`, no `cli/assets.py`) and every CLI row (`unrecognized arguments: --assets`; the usage-error rows too, as argparse's exit 2 for that reason, not for the rule); with the query/fragment skip removed, `test_query_or_fragment_is_skipped` fails
