  - **Second review fixes**: (1) a backslash-escaped `\<pre>`/`\<code>` (how the exporter writes a literal tag in prose) opened a code element that never closed and hid every later attachment; in Markdown an HTML code element now opens only when its `<` is not escaped (an odd number of backslashes before it), and a corpus test pins the attachment references of every `tests/fixtures/trac_html` fixture. (2) The warning for two attachments with one path says "wiki page 'ticket-5' and ticket #5 both map to ticket-5/x.png; keeping the first" instead of blaming letter case; the layout is unchanged. (3) An attachment the server refuses for size (`validation_error` naming `max_inline_bytes`) warns with the limit, keeps its URLs and no longer aborts, and the report adds `, S too large`; every other error still aborts. (4) Writes are all or nothing (temporary siblings, then renames; see Order); the planned paths are checked first, so a file where a directory is needed, a target that is a directory, an output that is a directory or in an unwritable directory is an error exit before anything is fetched. Payloads are no longer all held in memory. (5) `trac-mcp wiki-pull` exits 1, not 2, when writing the assets or the output fails. (6) One warning names how many URLs have an attachment's path but are not under the instance path (Trac behind a proxy with another base path); they are left alone, and another host's URL on a root-served instance is not counted. (7) A rewritten autolink's label is Markdown-escaped, so `*draft*.png` renders literally. (8) Dead `find_refs`/`fetch_all`/`write_assets`/`plan_assets` removed for `scan_text` and an `AssetStage`/`AssetPlanner` pair
  - **Third review fixes**: (1) `trac-mcp wiki-pull --assets`: the stage wrote inside the real `_mcp_session`, whose anyio task groups wrap what leaves the block in an ExceptionGroup, so a disk or permission failure surfaced as "call to ... failed: AssetError" (exit 3) instead of exit 1 with `--assets: cannot write ...`; the stage's errors are now converted to `CliError` inside the block (exit 1; a path conflict exit 2). (2) A backslash-escaped tag in Markdown prose (`\<img src="...">`) yields no attribute hit (odd number of backslashes before its `<`). (3) An escaped `\]` before `(URL)` is not a link target (`[a\]b](URL)`, an escaped bracket inside a real label, still is). (4) The temporary file name stem is cut to 60 UTF-8 bytes at a character boundary, so a 252-byte name of 62 emoji plus `.png` no longer fails with "File name too long". (5) The asset-path check runs per attachment after it is fetched and only for one that will be written (found, not too large, not a clash), so a conflict at a missing attachment's path only warns; the output-path check stays before the page pull; the message says "nothing was written". (6) The output is handled at its real path (`realpath`): relative links start from the directory it is written in, and a dangling symlink whose target directory is missing is an early usage error with that directory never made. (7) The unmatched-URL warning names both causes: "N attachment-like URLs did not match instance path X (Trac behind a proxy with another base path, or a link_rewrites rule moved them); left as they are". (8) `_misses_instance` shares `_parse_url`'s scheme and root-host check (`_scoped`), no behaviour change. (9) An output file that is, is inside, or is where a directory of a planned attachment's path (`-o img/P/a.png --assets img`) is a usage error (exit 2) instead of a rename that overwrote the attachment or failed after the assets were in place. Written first and seen failing on 4caacc7: the rows named in each fix, among them `test_a_write_failure_under_the_real_session_is_exit_1_not_a_transport_error` (exit 3) against the real session, not the fake one
- **`trac-mcp ticket-comment-edit TICKET CNUM FILE [--base-ts TS] [--expect-file PATH]` edits an existing comment from a file, locked by `base_ts`, and verifies the stored text byte for byte (Trac /trac_mcp_server #220).**
  - **Why**: the rookery #69 slips. A 30-34 KB procedure comment was corrected eight times by passing the whole body inline to the `ticket_comment_edit` tool, re-typed each time, and three of those edits changed text nobody meant to change. `ticket-comment` adds comments only, and `ticket-pull --comment` had no write-side counterpart
  - **What**: the file is sent verbatim (empty file is exit 2, no call). `base_ts` is `--base-ts` or a fresh `ticket_get` `_ts`. `--expect-file PATH` reads comment `CNUM`'s stored body after that and refuses with exit 1 (nothing stored) unless it equals the file, naming the first differing byte offset and both lengths, never the bodies; a missing comment is exit 1. After the write the comment is read back: equal prints `Stored comment N matches the text sent, byte-for-byte` (exit 0), equal after CRLF/CR to LF folding says so (exit 0), anything else, or a failed read-back, exits 4 (stored, do not retry blindly). There is deliberately no 10000-character cap: that limit belongs to `ticket.update` (adding a comment); the edit path has none. The "find comment N in the changelog" loop of `ticket-pull --comment` is now one helper shared by `ticket-pull`, the expect check and the read-back
  - **Tested**: `TestTicketCommentEdit` in `tests/test_cli_client.py` (parser; `base_ts` from `ticket_get`, and `--base-ts` skipping the read; a body of over 30000 characters sent; expect match, mismatch with offset in bytes and no `ticket_comment_edit` call, missing comment; read-back equal, CRLF-folded, different, failed or comment-missing; tool error; the `instance` on every call; and three rows through the real server stack over a fake Trac, including a 400x `_HARD_BODY` round trip through `ticket-pull`), plus one live row, `test_live_ticket_comment_edit_long_body_and_expect_file`, on a `/trac_test` scratch ticket (a ~12000-character edit of a comment added under the limit, the file's bytes back from `ticket-pull --comment`, then the stale original refused by `--expect-file` and the comment left unchanged). The existing `ticket-pull` tests pass unchanged
  - **Seeded**: written first and seen failing before the command existed: all 21 `TestTicketCommentEdit` rows (argparse `invalid choice` for the command, `AttributeError` for the helper) and the live row (`invalid choice: 'ticket-comment-edit'`, exit 2)
  - **Review fixes**: `--expect-file` compares on CR/CRLF-folded text on both sides (a CRLF file this command pushed now works as the next `--expect-file`, and the mismatch offset is on the folded texts); the success lines say the stored comment matches "the text sent" (the comparison is on decoded text, so "the file" was false for a non-UTF-8 file); `FILE` and `--expect-file` both `-` is a usage error (stdin feeds only one); an empty or whitespace-only `--expect-file` is allowed (a stored comment can be empty); `docs/reference/cli.md` now says the 10000-character limit applies to adding a comment and `ticket-comment-edit` has none.
- **`ticket_search` lists the milestone of each ticket (Trac /trac_mcp_server #232, GitHub PR 136).** A search across several milestones (e.g. `milestone^=v2.&status!=closed`) showed only number, summary, status and owner, so telling which ticket belongs to which milestone took one `ticket_get` per ticket or one search per milestone
  - Each line reads `- #42: Fix login (status: new, owner: alice, milestone: v2.0) - <url>`, and each entry of `structuredContent.tickets` has a `milestone` key; an unset milestone is `""`, as for the other fields (Trac /trac_mcp_server #141). The value comes from the `ticket.get` the search already makes, so there is no extra RPC
  - **Tested**: `test_search_lists_the_milestone_of_each_ticket` (set and unset milestone, line and structured content) in `tests/test_mcp/tools/test_ticket.py`; `test_search_unset_fields_show_empty_not_none` covers the new slot
  - **Review fixes**: the text line reads `item['milestone']` like the other fields (the dict always sets the key); the `ticket_search` description in `docs/reference/mcp-tools.md` matches the tool's description string verbatim; the `docs/reference/structured-json-output.md` example is a response `_handle_search` can produce (`(showing N)` only when total > max_results, `failed_ids` present) and its table row lists the per-ticket keys and `failed_ids`; the test also asserts the line for ticket #11 (`v2.1`)
- **`milestone_list` filters by prefix and completion, with due and completion dates in one call (Trac /trac_mcp_server #231, GitHub PR 135).** Finding the open milestones of a release line took one `milestone_get` per milestone, e.g. 40 calls for 40 `Release_7.*` milestones, because `milestone_list` returned names only
  - New optional parameters: `prefix` (case-sensitive name prefix), `details` (also return `due` and `completed` per milestone) and `status` (`open`, `completed` or `all`; anything but `all` implies `details`). Without them the tool behaves as before
  - The dates are read with the new `TracClient.get_milestones`, one `ticket.milestone.get` per milestone in `system.multicall` batches of 50, with the batching and fallback rules of `_batched_multicall`. A milestone that fails to load is reported under `failed` and in a warning, never counted
  - **Tested**: `tests/test_client_get_milestones.py` (batch size, three batches for 120 names, de-duplication, per-entry fault, fallback without `system.multicall`) and `TestMilestoneListFilters` in `tests/test_mcp/tools/test_milestone.py` (prefix, case sensitivity, details, `open`/`completed`, a milestone that fails to load, invalid `status`)
  - **Review fixes**: with `status=all` a milestone that fails to load stays in `milestones` and the header count (line `<name> | (failed to load)`, still in `failed`, absent from `details`), so `milestones` no longer differs with `details`; `open`/`completed` still leave it out. Open vs completed is decided on the raw `completed` value (`0`, `None` or `""` is open), not the formatted string, so `""` is no longer "completed". A prefix matching nothing, with `details` or `status`, has the filtered-empty shape (`0 milestone(s) ... : none`, `details` and `failed` keys); the no-argument texts are unchanged. `milestone_list` and `milestone_get` descriptions and docs name `MILESTONE_VIEW`, the permission their ToolSpecs gate on, not `TICKET_VIEW`; `_batched_multicall`'s docstring names `get_milestones`
  - **Seeded**: written first and seen failing on the merged PR: `test_status_all_keeps_a_failed_milestone_in_the_list`, `test_empty_string_completed_counts_as_open`, `test_prefix_matching_nothing_with_details_has_the_filtered_shape`, `test_prefix_matching_nothing_with_status_has_the_filtered_shape`, `test_no_milestones_at_all_with_details_has_the_filtered_shape`
- **A live run (`ci-live.sh`, `pytest --run-live`) now probes `/trac_test` before it starts and stops with one line naming the cause when the origin is wrong (Trac /trac_mcp_server #224).**
  - **Why**: with `TRAC_TEST_URL` unset, `/trac_test` is derived from `TRAC_URL`'s host and port and answered 308 to another port; the run produced 47 failures and 18 errors and no line saying why, because the session sweep fixture swallows the error
  - **What**: `tests/live_helpers.py` `probe_trac_test()` POSTs `system.getAPIVersion` to `/trac_test` through the client's own session (its auth, TLS setting, scope and `rpc_timeout`), without following redirects (`_rpc_call` follows them and raises on status; the probe needs the raw first answer). Only a well-formed XML-RPC result passes. Anything else returns one line naming the URL (userinfo stripped, the password never) and what it answered. A malformed `TRAC_TEST_URL` is reported from `trac_test_url()`'s message, never the value
  - **Faults count as failures**: `system.getAPIVersion` needs nothing beyond `XML_RPC`, so a healthy Trac never faults on it; a fault (a permission fault, say) means the rows would fail one by one. The line is `answered XML-RPC fault <code>: <faultString>`, one line, truncated to 120 characters
  - **The advice follows the cause**: a redirect, an unreachable host, a 200 that is not XML-RPC, or another status: `set TRAC_TEST_URL in .env to /trac_test's own origin (it is set | unset, derived from TRAC_URL)`; 401, 403 or a fault: `check TRAC_USERNAME/TRAC_PASSWORD (the account needs XML_RPC on /trac_test)`; a TLS failure (`SSLError`): `check the certificate or set TRAC_INSECURE`; an allowlist refusal (`DestinationOutOfScope`, which is not a `requests` error and is raised before anything is sent): `the client's allowed instances exclude it`
  - **Userinfo is always dropped**: `strip_userinfo` removes everything up to the last `@` of the netloc, whatever the parser makes of the host; before, `http://u:pw@:8080/...` (no hostname) came back unchanged with its password. A URL the parser rejects is replaced by a placeholder
  - **Exit at collection finish, code 2 = not run**: `tests/conftest.py`'s `pytest_collection_finish` runs the probe on `session.items` under `--run-live` when a live item is selected and `TRAC_URL`, `TRAC_USERNAME` and `TRAC_PASSWORD` are set (`live_credentials_set()`, shared with the stale-leftover sweep), and ends the run with `pytest.exit(line, returncode=2)`: the third state beside pass (0) and fail (1), as `ci-live.sh --daemon` exits 2 for "could not run". It is not in `pytest_collection_modifyitems`, which ran before the mark plugin applied `-k`, `-m` and `--deselect`, so `--run-live -k <an offline row>` with a dead `/trac_test` exited 2. A failed probe stops the whole session, offline rows included, because the live run is only a pass when all of it runs
  - **Tested**: offline, in `tests/test_live_probe.py`. Against real local HTTP servers: a 308 (URL, code, `Location` and `TRAC_TEST_URL` named; written first and seen failing against a probe that returned `None`), a 3xx with no `Location` or with userinfo in it (also with no host), 401 and 403 (credentials advice, no `TRAC_TEST_URL`), 404 and 500, a 200 HTML page (content type named), a valid reply (passes), faults (credentials advice, long and multi-line `faultString` cut to one line), a real TLS handshake failure, a closed port, an allowlist refusal (nothing reaches the server), userinfo in `TRAC_TEST_URL` (also with no host), a malformed `TRAC_TEST_URL`, unset vs set; `strip_userinfo`; `live_credentials_set`; the hook with fake config and session; and the hook under real pytest in a subprocess (closed-port Trac, `.env` disabled): `--run-live -k <offline row>` and `-m "not live"` exit 0 with no probe, `-k <live row>` exits 2 with the line (seen failing first for the first two on the modifyitems version)
- **A Markdown export of a Trac page carries YAML front matter naming its page, and the guarded paths (`wiki_file_push`, `trac-mcp wiki-push` and `trac-convert` to TracWiki) refuse it unless forced (Trac /trac_mcp_server #212).**
  - **What**: `wiki_file_pull` (inline and to `file_path`), `trac-mcp wiki-pull` and `trac-convert --from-wiki PAGE --to md` (stdout, `-o`, clipboard) start a Markdown export with `title`, `source`, `trac_instance`, `trac_page`, `trac_version` and `exported`, then one blank line. `title` is the text of the first `h1` of Trac's HTML (anchor and icon marks dropped; the page name when there is none), `source` the page URL, `trac_instance` the path of the Trac URL, `trac_version` the pinned version (`null` when unknown), `exported` the UTC time. Strings are JSON strings, valid YAML double-quoted scalars. `format=html`, `format=tracwiki` and `trac-convert` text, file and clipboard input have none. New `converters/front_matter.py` (`build_front_matter`, `split_front_matter`, `exported_from`, `instance_path`); `ConversionResult.title` is read by `trac_html_to_markdown` without changing its Markdown (the #209 golden files are untouched)
  - **Why**: the Markdown export is one-way (Trac's HTML translated), and pushing it back re-shapes the page. This guard replaces the round-trip protocol that #214 deletes; it is for the accidental pull-edit-push loop, not a determined user, so deleting the front matter by hand defeats it, and that is accepted
  - **Push refusal**: `wiki_file_push` reads the text first and, when its front matter is a YAML mapping with a non-empty string `trac_page`, returns a `validation_error` naming the page, instance and version and makes no Trac call -- whatever `format` and `strip_frontmatter` say. New `force` boolean (default `false`) pushes it anyway. `trac-mcp wiki-push --force` sends `force: true` only when given; against a daemon whose strict schema refuses `force` it raises one `CliError` (exit 1) saying the server predates the guard and does not refuse either. Front matter without `trac_page` (Hugo-style) is stripped as before. `_strip_yaml_frontmatter` is now `split_front_matter(...)[1]`, so it also takes CRLF, a BOM and a closing fence at the end of the text
  - **trac-convert**: Markdown whose front matter carries `trac_page`, converted to TracWiki (`--to tracwiki` or `--to-wiki`), exits 1 with one `trac-convert: refusing to convert: ...` stderr line and writes nothing to Trac or the output; `--force` strips that front matter and converts the rest. Converting to Markdown, and front matter without `trac_page`, are as before
  - **Schema**: connected MCP sessions keep the old `wiki_file_push` schema (no `force`) until they reconnect; `trac-mcp` connects per call and sees it once the daemon is deployed
  - **Tested**: `wiki_file_push` of the pulled Markdown is a `validation_error` naming page, instance and version with no `put_wiki_page` / `get_wiki_page_info` call (written first and seen failing on the unchanged handler: `test_pulled_markdown_is_refused`, `test_refused_whatever_the_format`, `test_refused_without_stripping`, `test_schema_has_force_default_false`, `test_markdown_pull_starts_with_the_front_matter`); `force=true` stores it without front matter; plain and Hugo-style Markdown push as before. Front matter builder (exact output, YAML round trip of a title with `:`, `"` and non-ASCII, `null` version), splitter (LF, CRLF, BOM, fence at end of text, no block, `---`/`---`, unclosed), `exported_from`, `instance_path`; the title from the first `h1` (anchor marks, later `h1`, `h2`-only, inline markup, `stock_TicketQuery`); `--force` on the CLI and the old-daemon message (an error mentioning "enforced" is not it); `trac-convert` export, refusal, `--force`, `--to-wiki`; and a live row, `test_wiki_file_pull_front_matter_live.py`, that pulls a scratch page, sees the push refused with the version unchanged, then forced to version + 1 without `trac_page`
  - **Review fixes**: `exported_from` caught only `yaml.YAMLError`, so a Hugo-style `date: 2024-02-30` (a date-shaped value that is not a date, which PyYAML raises as a plain `ValueError`) made `wiki_file_push` an error and `trac-convert --to tracwiki` a traceback; it now treats any exception from the parser as "not an export", so that text is stripped and converted as before. `_scalar` left DEL, the C1 controls, lone surrogates and U+FFFE/U+FFFF raw, which PyYAML rejects, and U+0085 folded as a line break, so the front matter failed to parse or came back changed and the guard let the export through; those characters (and U+2028/U+2029) are now written as `\uXXXX`, readable non-ASCII stays literal. The docs and the module docstring said "every push path"; they now name the guarded paths (`wiki_file_push`, `trac-mcp wiki-push`, `trac-convert` to TracWiki) and say `wiki_update` / `wiki_create` store verbatim and do not check. `source` is the page URL with the page name percent-encoded (`page_url` in `front_matter.py`; `Notes/Q3 #2` is `Notes/Q3%20%232`), in the pull handler and in `trac-convert`; the `document_url` the translator matches in-page anchors by is unchanged. The `trac-convert` refusal line's `--from-wiki` hint shell-quotes the page name (`'My Page'`), and `--force -v` reports the bytes of the input as read, not of what is left after the front matter is stripped
- **`wiki_file_pull` and `trac-mcp wiki-pull` take `--format html`: Trac's own render of the page as a complete minimal HTML document (Trac /trac_mcp_server #211).**
  - **What**: `format` is now `markdown | html | tracwiki` (default still `markdown`) on the tool and on `trac-mcp wiki-pull --format`. The output is `client.get_wiki_page_html` unchanged -- the fragment Trac renders, its links already absolute -- inside `<!doctype html>`, `<html>`, `<head>` with `<meta charset="utf-8">` and `<title>` (the page name, `html.escape`d), `<body>`, and `</body></html>` plus a newline. No CSS, no link touched. The page version is read first (`get_wiki_page_info`) and the HTML fetched at it, with the markdown path's fallback and `warnings` entry when no usable version comes back (worded for html: there is no source to mismatch); no source is read. `converted` is `true` (it is not the stored source); a not-found fault from either read is the existing `not_found` error
  - **Trac's `getPageHTML` already wraps its render in `<html><body>...</body></html>`** (every wiki capture of the #208 set does; ticket text from `wikiToHtml` is a bare fragment). That wrapper is removed before the minimal document is built, so the document has exactly one `<html` and one `<body`; the inner HTML is not parsed or altered, and a response with no such wrapper (case and whitespace between the tags tolerated) is used as it is. Found by the stage hand check: the first version nested `<html><body>` inside its own `<body>`, and neither the fake-client tests (bare fragments) nor the live row (token checks) could see it
  - **`link_rewrites` is ignored for html**, exactly as for tracwiki: `link_rewrites_source` is `none` and the argument is not validated. `trac-mcp wiki-pull --format html` behaves like `--format tracwiki` there: it never reads the local export config (a broken one is not an error), and `--link-map` prints `no effect with --format html` and sends nothing (the warning now names the format)
  - **Connected MCP sessions keep the old `format` enum until they reconnect**: the schema is read at connect time, so a session opened before the daemon is deployed will refuse `format="html"` as not one of `markdown`, `tracwiki`. `trac-mcp wiki-pull` connects per call, so it sees the new enum as soon as the daemon is deployed; against an older daemon it fails with a message saying so (see the review fixes)
  - **Tested**: handler (the exact document, a page name with `<`, `>` and `&` escaped in the title, the HTML pinned to the info version and to a requested one, the unusable-version fallback and its warning, no source read, `link_rewrites` ignored and unvalidated, `not_found` from the info and the HTML read, the schema enum and default); the CLI (the choice, the document written, no `link_rewrites` sent from a valid or a broken local config, the no-effect warning, and one pass through the real server over uvicorn). A live row on `/trac_test` (`test_wiki_file_pull_html_live.py`, run by the operator) pulls a scratch page with a heading and a wiki link through the real handler and client and checks the document shell, the `<h1` and an absolute `href="http`. Seeded: with the `<meta charset="utf-8">` line removed from the document builder, `test_the_document_wraps_the_fragment_exactly` and the CLI's through-the-server row failed; with the unwrap removed, the wrapper tests (the exact stage shape, case and whitespace variants, three real captures) failed; restored, they pass
  - **Review fixes**: the reported `version` was `1` for any format when `get_wiki_page_info` answered `{}` or a non-dict, even with `version=N` passed (the content was N); it is now the info's version when usable, else the requested one, else `1`, and the "version could not be read" warning stays for the unpinned case only. The unwrap also accepts attributes on `<html>` / `<body>` (dropped with the wrapper), a leading BOM, and a leading `<?xml ...?>` and/or `<!doctype ...>`; it is still a full match with the inner HTML verbatim, anything else passes through. `trac-mcp wiki-pull --format html` against a daemon that predates #211 (its schema refuses `html` as not one of the enum) is one `CliError`, exit 1, saying to deploy it or use `--format markdown|tracwiki`. The text block's heading is `Warnings:` for html and tracwiki and stays `Conversion warnings:` for markdown. The schema's `format` description and the docs name the three formats and the html read order. `_handle_pull`'s combined markdown/html branch is split into one branch each around a `_pinned_version` helper (no change in behaviour beyond the above). Not done: html output does not rewrite in-page anchors (a follow-up ticket), and the unwrap stays in the tool, not in `TracClient.get_wiki_page_html`
- **The Markdown export can rewrite its links: an `export.link_rewrites` map, a `link_rewrites` argument on `wiki_file_pull`, and `--link-map FROM=TO` on `trac-convert` and `trac-mcp wiki-pull` (Trac /trac_mcp_server #213).**
  - **Why**: Trac renders a link to another wiki page as a URL on Trac; a page exported to be published elsewhere (the sibling posts of a blog) needs those links to point at their new home. `trac_html_to_markdown(link_map=)` already matched by longest prefix and nothing passed it a map
  - **The map**: `{from, to}` rules; a link target (`href`) starting with `from` has that prefix replaced by `to`, the longest `from` first whatever the order. The link text is never changed, so an autolink whose target is rewritten becomes `[original URL](new URL)`. `from` must be a non-empty string, `to` a string, and a duplicate `from` is refused; one shared validator (`link_rewrites.py`) serves the YAML schema, the environment variable, the tool argument and both CLIs, and names the bad entry
  - **Changed: `link_map` now maps `href`s only** (hrefs inside raw HTML the translator passes through included). It no longer touches `img[src]` (nor an `img` in raw HTML), which the #209 translator rewrote: images and attachments are #215's. An `href` starting with `#` is never rewritten, whatever the map says, so an in-page heading link survives a rule whose prefix would match it. Nothing passed a map before this change, so no output changed
  - **In-page anchors are same-document links, written `#frag`**: Trac does not render `[#Heading x]` as `href="#Heading"` but as the document's own URL plus the fragment (`<trac_url>/wiki/<page>#Heading` from `getPageHTML`, `<trac_url>#Heading` from `wikiToHtml`, which has no page context); bare `#frag` hrefs come only from macro output. So a `#` guard never fired on real input and a rule for the `/wiki/` prefix rewrote a page's links to its own headings. `trac_html_to_markdown` takes `document_url=`: a link with a fragment whose URL before the `#` equals it (two absolute URLs are compared by path only, after percent-decoding and ignoring one trailing `/`, since Trac builds its URLs from its own base URL, which can name another scheme or host than the daemon's `TRAC_URL`) is emitted as `#frag` and never rewritten, with or without a map, in Markdown links and in raw-HTML hrefs. The same document with no fragment, and another page with one, are ordinary links. `wiki_file_pull` passes `<trac_url>/wiki/<page>` (the routed instance's), `trac-convert --from-wiki PAGE` the same form, `trac-convert` on text `<trac_url>`; with no `document_url` only a bare `#frag` counts, as before
  - **Review fixes**: `trac-mcp wiki-pull` names a daemon that predates link rewriting (its strict schema refuses `link_rewrites`: one `CliError`, exit 1, saying to deploy it or drop the local key / `--link-map`) and warns `the server did not confirm the link map was applied` when a sent map comes back without `link_rewrites_source: call`. The in-page anchor match compares paths only, so a daemon that reaches Trac by a LAN address still recognises the public-host URLs Trac renders. The local config read loads `.env` first, so `${VAR}` in a rule resolves as for the daemon and `trac-convert` (it became `/wiki/` before), and a bad local rule is one line, not pydantic's report (a YAML syntax error is flattened to one line too). `--check-trac --link-map` prints the no-effect warning. One shared argparse type and `parse_pairs` serve both CLIs, and YAML rules are validated once, by `ExportConfig` (`load_config` validates only the environment's). The tool schema's strictness (an empty `from` or an extra key is refused before the handler; a duplicate `from` is the handler's `validation_error`) is now documented
  - **Config**: a new top-level `export:` section (`ExportConfig` on `UnifiedConfig`) holds `link_rewrites: [{from, to}, ...]`; `TRAC_MCP_LINK_REWRITES` is whitespace-separated `FROM=TO` pairs split at the first `=`, replaces the YAML list whole, and an empty value clears it. `Config.link_rewrites` is a tuple of pairs, empty by default, resolved env over `.env` over YAML like `write_gate`; the daemon's `bootstrap_config` fills it, and the per-instance `dataclasses.replace` copies carry it
  - **`wiki_file_pull`**: an optional `link_rewrites` array of `{from, to}` objects. Given, it replaces the daemon's configured map for that call (`[]` means no rewriting); omitted, the daemon's map applies; a bad entry is a `validation_error` returned before Trac is read; for `format=tracwiki` it is ignored. `structuredContent.link_rewrites_source` is `call`, `config` or `none`
  - **`trac-convert --link-map FROM=TO`** (repeatable) on TracWiki -> Markdown, for `--from-wiki` and for file, stdin and clipboard input; it replaces the configured map, which applies without it. On any other direction it prints one `warning: --link-map has no effect ...` line (not with `-q`). A value with no `=`, an empty `FROM` or a repeated `FROM` is a usage error, exit 2
  - **`trac-mcp wiki-pull --link-map FROM=TO`** (repeatable) is sent as `link_rewrites`. Without it, `wiki-pull` reads `export.link_rewrites` (that key only; not the environment variable) from the workstation's `.trac_mcp/config.yaml`, found by the same hierarchical discovery as every other command, and sends it; with no such key it sends nothing and the daemon's map applies. A local config that cannot be read or whose `export` section is invalid is a one-line usage error (exit 2), not a traceback. The rewrite map is a publishing choice, so it lives with the person exporting, and changing it needs no redeploy of the daemon
  - **Tested**: translator (longest prefix whatever the map's order, `#Heading` untouched even under a `#` prefix, link text unchanged, `img[src]` and a raw-HTML `img` unrewritten, a raw-HTML `a[href]` rewritten; the five #209 tests that expected a rewritten image now expect it untouched); validation in every source; precedence (YAML only, environment over YAML, an empty environment value clears, per call over config, per-call `[]` disables) with the matching `link_rewrites_source`; both CLIs through `fake_trac` and `trac-mcp` through the real server over uvicorn, including the local-config send / no-send / broken-config cases. A new `isolated_config` fixture hides the developer's own config files and `.env`, as `no_trac_configured` does. A live row on `/trac_test` (`test_wiki_file_pull_link_rewrites_live.py`) pulls a scratch page with one rule for that instance's `/wiki/` prefix, and checks the page link is rewritten and an in-page heading link is not
  - **Seeded**: with the map sorted shortest-first, `test_link_map_rewrites_by_longest_prefix`, `test_link_map_longest_prefix_wins_whatever_the_maps_order`, `test_link_map_is_repeatable_and_longest_prefix_wins` (`trac-convert`) and `test_an_in_page_anchor_and_an_image_are_never_rewritten` (the tool) failed; restored, they pass
- **`trac_html_to_markdown`: Trac-rendered HTML to Markdown, as a markdownify subclass (Trac /trac_mcp_server #209, phase 1 of #206).** `converters/html_to_markdown.py` holds `trac_html_to_markdown(html, source=None, *, link_map=None, keep_toc=False, deflist="extra") -> ConversionResult` (`source_format="html"`, `target_format="markdown"`, each warning once). Nothing calls it yet (#210 wires it in), `converters/__init__.py` does not export it, and `tracwiki_to_markdown.py` / `markdown_to_tracwiki.py` are untouched
  - **New dependencies**: `markdownify==1.2.3`, `beautifulsoup4==4.15.0`, `six==1.17.0` and `soupsieve==4.0.1` (bs4 requires it; the fourth new package, pure Python and MIT like the rest), pinned in `constraints/deploy.txt`; `markdownify>=1.2` and `beautifulsoup4>=4.12` in `pyproject.toml`. The parser is the `lxml` already shipped. `markdown-it-py` (already pinned through `rich`) joins the dev extra for the self-check. `build.sh` names the new module, `markdownify`, `bs4`, `bs4.builder._lxml`, `soupsieve` and `six` as hidden imports, and the built `trac-mcp-server` and `trac-convert` archives list them
  - **What is Trac-specific** (everything else is markdownify's own mapping): a code block is `div.wiki-code` (`div.code > pre`, `table.code`, `div.diff`) and takes its language from the n-th `{{{#!lang` opener of the TracWiki `source`, through `code_language_callback`, using #208's pairing rules (processor name `[\w+-][\w+/-]*`, so `#!/bin/sh` is a plain block; `#!comment`, `#!html`, `#!htmlcomment`, `#!default`, `#!Workflow` and the wiki processors make no block; a block nested in a wiki processor counts, one nested in a plain or code block is content). A paired block emits the source body (`table.code` interleaves line numbers and `div.diff` lost its `+`/`-` columns). Blocks pair by position only when every pair's text agrees (whitespace-insensitive; a `div.diff` is compared with the `+`/`-`/space column removed from each source line and its `---`/`+++`/`@@` header lines allowed to be absent; a `table.code` on its `td` cells), otherwise all of them pair by text; an unmatched block gets no language, its rendered text and a warning. The external-link `span.icon` and its zero-width space, `span.wikianchor` and heading ids are not emitted; a `missing` link is plain text; `link_map` rewrites `href` and `src` by longest prefix, in pass-through HTML too; an `img` takes its `title` as the Markdown title, and one with `width` / `height` is written as an HTML `<img>` with no warning; `div.wiki-toc` / `div.wiki-toc-un` (`PageOutline`, `TracGuideToc`) are dropped with one warning unless `keep_toc`; cell `text-align` is dropped without a warning (the operator's call); `sup` / `sub` / `span.underline` are HTML, `del` is `~~`, `small` is plain; a definition list is `term` / `:   definition` or, with `deflist="bold"`, a bold term; lettered and roman lists become numbered with a warning; macro output (`TicketQuery`, `TitleIndex`, `TracIni`, `MacroList`, ...) is translated through the lists and tables it holds with one "exported as rendered, live content" warning each, and `TicketQuery`'s paging and progress chrome is dropped; `div.system-message` and the `#!Workflow` graph are dropped with a warning that quotes the text
  - **Whatever is not covered passes through as HTML with a warning that names it**: an allowlist of tags, classes and attributes, so a styled `div`, `form` / `input`, a styled `span` or an `h1` with an unknown class is written as its own HTML, tags and content, with no Markdown converted inside it (its uncovered descendants are named in warnings too). A table with `colspan` / `rowspan`, a block (list, code, quote, table) in a cell, or an attribute outside the allowlist passes through whole (so does a table whose own `style`, `border` or `cellspacing` is outside the allowlist), as HTML with no blank line in it (a blank line would end the HTML block; one inside a `pre` becomes a line holding `&#32;`), because a pipe table cell holds one line. Text is escaped only where it could change the structure (`*`, non-intraword `_`, backticks, `<tag`, entities, every `[` `]` and `~`, a backslash before a newline, and line starts that read as a heading, quote, list or rule, in a `dt` and in an inline-only parent too)
  - **Tested**: unit tests per override on small inline HTML; a golden Markdown file per #208 fixture (`tests/fixtures/trac_html/<slug>.golden.md`, each read by hand before it was committed; `UPDATE_GOLDEN=1` rewrites them); and a self-check that renders the emitted Markdown with markdown-it-py (CommonMark plus tables) and compares its block skeleton (`ul` / `ol` / `li` / `blockquote` / `pre` / `table` / `h1`-`h6` / `hr` / `dl`) with Trac's, after removing what the converter drops by design. 18 of 19 fixtures match outright; `stock_TicketQuery` is the one listed exception (a `dl` in a quote, which CommonMark has no node for), and it must still agree once `dl` is removed from both sides. Seeded first: with nested list items flattened in the converter the check failed on `stock_TracWorkflow` and `ticket_code_in_list_item`, the two fixtures that nest content in emitted Markdown (the others keep their nesting in pass-through HTML tables)
  - **`ruff format` skips `tests/fixtures/trac_html/*.golden.md`** (`[tool.ruff.format] exclude`): ruff formats the Python code blocks inside Markdown files, which would rewrite the converter's exact output
- **A fixture set of Trac-rendered HTML for the HTML -> Markdown export (Trac /trac_mcp_server #208, phase 0 of #206).** `tests/fixtures/trac_html/` holds 19 documents, each as its stored TracWiki (`<slug>.tracwiki`) and the HTML Trac rendered for it (`<slug>.html`), with `manifest.json` naming each one's origin, render call and the element/class combinations it covers. They are chosen by set cover over an inventory of every wiki page, ticket description and ticket comment in `/auto_pm` and `/trac_mcp_server` plus the 13 blog posts on `/bcs` and `/scopemate` (2,542 documents, 0 render failures), and cover 136 of its 144 structural combinations (Pygments token spans aside). 11 are stock Trac pages, 2 are blog posts and 6 are this project's own ticket comments. The measurements themselves are on the ticket; no product code changes
  - **Scrubbed for a public repo**: the internal Trac base URL becomes `https://trac.example.org`, and private addresses, hostnames, home paths and mail addresses become placeholders. `tests/test_trac_html_fixtures.py` fails on any fixture that still carries one (`manifest.json` is scanned too), and checks that every fixture has its source, HTML and manifest entry. Seeded: the same check run over an unscrubbed build of the set flagged all 19 HTML files (1,130 matches)
- **A deployed daemon reports which commit it runs, and `ci-live.sh --daemon <url> --expect-sha SHA` checks it (Trac /trac_mcp_server #170).** `/healthz` returned only `{"status": "ok"}` and MCP `serverInfo.version` is the package version on every commit, so the daemon smoke could not tell a fresh install from a stale one with the same toolset
  - **A build stamp**: a new root `setup.py` (`pyproject.toml` stays the metadata source) adds a `build_py` subclass that writes `trac_mcp_server/_release.py` -- exactly `RELEASE_SHA = "<40 hex>"` -- into the BUILD directory, never `src/` (the path is also in `.gitignore`). The value is `git rev-parse HEAD` in the directory of `setup.py`, with `-dirty` appended when `git status --porcelain` prints anything (an untracked file counts, since it may ship in the wheel; gitignored files such as `build/` and `*.egg-info` do not). git runs with every `GIT_*` variable dropped from its environment. When the commit cannot be determined -- git missing or failing, no commit, or a directory that is not itself a checkout (an sdist, or one unpacked inside another repository) -- no stamp is written and any `_release.py` already in the build directory is deleted, so a stale copy from an earlier build in the same `build/` cannot ship a wrong sha, and `setup.py` writes one line to stderr saying why (`setup.py: no release stamp (<reason>); the build will report release "unknown"`). An editable install is never stamped. `setup()` runs only under `__name__ == "__main__"`, which is how `setuptools.build_meta` executes the file, so the stamp functions are importable and tested
  - **`/healthz` is now `{"status": "ok", "release": "<sha>"}`** (`"unknown"` when the build carries no stamp), still unauthenticated. `trac_mcp_server.version.release_sha()` is the accessor. `serverInfo.version` becomes `<version>+g<first 12 of the sha>` (`.dirty` appended for a dirty build) when a sha is known, and stays `<version>` otherwise; `__version__` and `check_version_consistency` are unchanged. The stdio transport reports the same version (its `InitializationOptions` reads `server.version`)
  - **`scripts/daemon_smoke.py --expect-sha SHA`** (7 to 40 lowercase hex characters, otherwise exit 2 NOT RUN before any network call; `ci-live.sh --daemon` passes it through like `--instance`). Check 1 now accepts any body with `"status": "ok"` and shows the reported release in its PASS detail (`release none reported` for an older daemon). With `--expect-sha` it FAILS when the release is absent, `unknown`, ends in `-dirty`, or does not start with SHA, naming both (`release <reported> is not the expected <sha>`). The comparison is the unit-tested `check_release`. The docstring's "cannot tell WHICH commit is running" is replaced by what is now true: with `--expect-sha` it can, for a release built from a git checkout. The Docker image builds without `.git` and `setup.py`, so a container reports `"release": "unknown"`, and `--expect-sha` fails against it by design
  - **Tested**: the stamp functions against real temporary git repositories (clean, modified, staged, untracked, gitignored, inherited `GIT_DIR`, not a checkout, a subdirectory of another checkout, no commit, git missing, stale-stamp removal) and the real `build_py` command by subprocess; the accessor with and without an injected `_release` module; `/healthz` through the Starlette app; the `serverInfo.version` formatting; `check_release` and the argument validation; and `--expect-sha` end to end both through `ci-live.sh` against an `http.server` and through the real app and the SDK client. `test_server_version_matches_project_version` now expects the stamped form. Seeded: with the comparison always passing, the mismatch tests failed; restored, they pass
- **`--read-only` / `TRAC_MCP_READ_ONLY` / `server.read_only` expose only the tools marked read-only, on every transport.** The filter reads each tool's own `Tool.annotations.readOnlyHint`, so it needs no separate list; it combines with `--permissions-file` (a tool must pass both). A hidden tool is not dispatchable by name either. `ticket_attachment_get`, `wiki_attachment_get` and `wiki_file_pull` are hidden too, because they can write a local file. An unrecognised env value is refused at startup, an empty one counts as unset. Docs: [Tool Architecture: Read-Only Filtering](docs/reference/tool-architecture.md#read-only-filtering---read-only), README, CLI and configuration reference
- **`TRAC_MCP_DISABLED_TOOLS` / `server.disabled_tools` leave single tools out**, e.g. a tool that needs a Trac plugin the server lacks. A disabled tool is not dispatchable by name either. A set env value replaces the YAML list, an empty one counts as unset; names are stripped in both sources. At startup stderr lists the matched names and warns about unknown ones and about a disabled `list_instances`
- **A `Dockerfile` runs the http transport in a container.** Multi-stage build installed with the deploy pins (`constraints/deploy.txt`), non-root user, `HEALTHCHECK` on `/healthz` at `TRAC_MCP_PORT`. Transport, host and port are set by `ENV`, not `CMD`, so arguments to `docker run` keep the http transport. A client that reaches the container by a name other than `localhost` needs `TRAC_MCP_ALLOWED_HOSTS`. `.dockerignore` keeps caches, build output, logs and `.env*` out of the build context
- **`TRAC_MCP_ALLOWED_HOSTS` and `TRAC_MCP_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` set the DNS-rebinding allow-list from the environment.** Comma-separated; a set value replaces the YAML `server.allowed_hosts`/`server.allowed_origins` list, an empty value counts as unset. Needed to reach the http transport by a name other than loopback or the bind address, such as a docker-compose service name (`trac-mcp:8080`), which was otherwise refused with `421 Invalid Host header`. A Host entry is `host[:port]` or `host:*`; an Origin entry includes the scheme, e.g. `http://trac-mcp:8080`
- **`ci-live.sh --daemon <url>` smoke-tests a deployed daemon over HTTP, and the `tracrpc_comment` plugin can be pip-installed by commit (Trac /trac_mcp_server #161).** The in-process live suite builds its own app from the checkout, so it cannot see what only a deployed daemon has: the FreeBSD wheels it installed (`constraints/deploy.txt`), the config it ships, its rc script and its environment. A release could pass `ci.sh` and `ci-live.sh` and still be broken on the host
  - **`ci-live.sh --daemon URL [--token-env NAME]`** skips the `.env` probe and the pytest run and `exec`s the new `scripts/daemon_smoke.py`, so its exit code is the script's. With no arguments the script is unchanged. Any other argument prints usage and exits 2. `URL` is the daemon base; a trailing `/mcp` or `/` is accepted and removed. The token comes from `TRAC_MCP_AUTH_TOKEN_STAGE`, or the variable `--token-env` names, and is never printed (every exception and server reply is scrubbed first, and the SDK's own stderr logging is silenced)
  - **Six checks, in order, stopping at the first failure**, one `PASS <n> <name>` / `FAIL <n> <name>: <reason>` line each: (1) `GET /healthz` is 200 `{"status": "ok"}`; (2) `POST /mcp` with no `Authorization` is 401; (3) `initialize` + `tools/list` over the SDK client, failing with the name of every tool this checkout defines that the daemon does not list (the names come from the server's own `ping` + `ALL_SPECS`, not a list in the script; extra tools on the daemon are noted, not failed); (4) `wiki_get WikiStart` on the test instance; (5) write `Smoke/DaemonSmoke` with a fresh uuid4 + UTC-stamp nonce (so a concurrent run cannot collide on identical content, #159), read it back and compare byte for byte; (6) find or create the `ci-live.sh --daemon smoke ticket (do not close)` ticket, post a comment, `ticket_comment_edit` it, and require `ticket_comment_history` to show both texts, with the plugin-missing answer reported as `tracrpc_comment plugin not loaded on the daemon's Trac`. Checks 4-6 never use the daemon's default instance: see the next bullet
  - **Writes go only where `--instance` says (default `/trac_test`)**: on the shared daemon the default instance is the production rules store (`/auto_pm`), so checks 4-6 pass `instance=<--instance>` explicitly on every tool call (`Scoped` wraps the session at the one place every call passes through) and read each result's reported `instance`/`instance_source` -- structured or the trailing `[instance: ...]` block -- failing the check if it is missing, is another instance, or is not `explicit`. An `--instance` other than `/trac_test` is refused (exit 2, NOT RUN, before any network call) unless `--allow-instance-writes` is also given, and a value that is no instance path is refused outright. `ci-live.sh --daemon` passes both flags through
  - **Exit contract is three-state**: 0 every check passed; 1 a check failed, an unreachable daemon or refused connection included (that is a finding about the release); 2 could not run -- the token variable unset, empty or not a usable bearer token, a malformed URL, or an `--instance` other than `/trac_test` without `--allow-instance-writes` -- decided before any network call, with a message saying "NOT RUN" and "this is not a passing result". The final line is `DAEMON SMOKE PASSED <base> (server <version>)` or `DAEMON SMOKE FAILED at check <n>`
  - **`plugins/tracrpc_comment/pyproject.toml`** (only a `[build-system]` table, `setuptools>=40.8.0`; `setup.py` stays the metadata source, version unchanged at 1.2.0) makes the plugin installable by commit: `pip install "git+https://github.com/nerpatech/trac-mcp-server@<sha>#subdirectory=plugins/tracrpc_comment"`. `plugins/README.md`'s "Installing on the Trac host" now leads with that path (one process-wide install, so one version in the working set and no `VersionConflict`; enabling stays per instance with `config set components "tracrpc_comment.*" enabled`, then one reload), and keeps the per-environment egg steps and the upgrade notes as the current state of the production instances until rookery ticket 69 migrates them
  - **Tested**: offline, in `tests/test_daemon_smoke.py` -- URL normalisation, the tool comparison, scrubbing; the shell dispatch and exit contract by running the real `ci-live.sh` (no token and a malformed URL exit 2 with no connection made to a listening socket, `--token-env` is honoured, bad arguments are usage + exit 2, a closed port exits 1 at check 1 with the token nowhere in the output, an `http.server` that answers the unauthenticated POST 200 fails check 2); and all six checks end to end over a real uvicorn server and the SDK's streamable-HTTP client, against a stand-in that advertises this checkout's tool definitions, validates every call against their real input schemas and answers in the real handlers' shapes (a hidden tool fails check 3 by name, a missing plugin fails check 6, a mangled page fails check 5)
  - **Seeded**: with `compare_tools` ignoring missing tools, `test_compare_tools_reports_missing_and_extra_sorted` and `test_a_missing_tool_fails_check_3_and_names_it` failed; with check 2's status test disabled, `test_an_open_daemon_fails_check_2` failed; with the failure path echoing the token and the reporter's scrub removed, `test_an_unreachable_daemon_fails_at_check_1_and_hides_the_token` failed; with `ticket_comment_history` sent without `instance`, `test_no_call_in_checks_4_to_6_omits_the_instance` and seven other tests failed; all passed again once restored
  - **Review fixes**: the script imported `trac_mcp_server` from the editable install, i.e. the MAIN checkout's `src/`, when run from a worktree (`python scripts/daemon_smoke.py` puts `scripts/`, not `src/`, on `sys.path`), so check 3 compared the daemon against master's tool list; it now puts its own checkout's `src/` first on `sys.path` before importing anything. `httpx`, the MCP SDK and `trac_mcp_server` were imported lazily inside the checks, so a missing one became `FAIL`, exit 1, after checks 1-2 had used the network; `main()` now imports all of them first and a missing one is NOT RUN, exit 2, naming the module. `Scoped.call_tool` raised "did not report which instance answered" before looking at `isError`, which hid the real error of a call that reached no instance (`unknown_instance`, an SDK schema error); such a result now fails with the server's error line, and the missing-echo failure applies only to a non-error result (an error that did reach an instance is still returned to its check, so check 6 still names the missing plugin). `scrub`'s generic `bearer\s+\S+` mangled the script's own "not enforcing its bearer token" into "bearer ***" and a `Bearer realm=` reply; it now replaces the token literally, `Authorization:` header values, and only a token-shaped `Bearer <16+ chars>` value, never `realm=`. Check 5 treated any `wiki_get` error as "page absent" and wrote; it now creates only on `not_found` and fails with the error otherwise. `ticket_search`'s `failed_ids` were ignored, so a transient load failure created a duplicate smoke ticket; check 6 now fails naming them instead of creating. The edit takes its `base_ts` from the `_ts` `ticket_update` returned, calling `ticket_get` only when it did not. A daemon started with `--permissions-file` filters its tool list, so check 3 fails on it by design; the script docstring and `ci-live.sh` now say a permission-restricted daemon is unsupported. The docstring no longer claims to check that "what answers is this checkout's release": the version string is the same on every commit, so check 3 proves only that the daemon lists every tool this checkout defines, and a same-toolset stale install is not detectable by this suite (wording only, no code change). `pyproject.toml` now requires `setuptools>=40.8.0`: `setuptools<81` constrained only pip's isolated build environment, not the `pkg_resources` Trac 1.6 imports at run time; `plugins/README.md` says to keep the stack venv on `<81`, to install with `--no-build-isolation` on a host with no PyPI route, and that because every commit keeps version 1.2.0, moving to a newer commit takes `pip install --force-reinstall --no-deps "git+...@<newsha>#subdirectory=plugins/tracrpc_comment"`. Tested: a stand-in checkout in a temp dir with a one-tool `trac_mcp_server` shows the copied script imports that checkout's `src/` with `PYTHONPATH` unset; a subprocess with `httpx` hidden exits 2 with "NOT RUN ... httpx" and a listening socket sees no connection; an `unknown_instance`-shaped stand-in error shows in `FAIL 4 read`; scrub keeps "bearer token" and `Bearer realm=` and removes the token, a header value and a long bearer value; a `server_error` on the smoke page's `wiki_get` fails check 5 with no `wiki_create` or `wiki_update` (`not_found` still creates); `failed_ids` fails check 6 with no `ticket_create`; the happy path makes no `ticket_get` between `ticket_update` and `ticket_comment_edit` (and falls back to one when `_ts` is absent). Seeded: with the `sys.path` insert removed, `test_the_script_imports_its_own_checkouts_src` failed; with the up-front import check disabled, `test_a_missing_dependency_is_not_run_before_any_network_call` failed; with the old generic bearer regex, `test_scrub_leaves_the_scripts_own_wording_alone` and `test_an_open_daemon_fails_check_2` failed; all passed again once restored. The `pip install` by commit was re-proved from a clean scratch venv (`setuptools<81`, `Trac==1.6`, `pytest`) after this commit, and `entry_points(group="trac.plugins")` listed the three `tracrpc_comment.*` entries
  - **Live acceptance** on `mcp_stage` (`http://mcp.example.org:8092`, `/trac_test`), each release staged with `ssh <stage-host> mcp <sha>`: against `master` `0f028dd`, all six checks passed (smoke ticket and `Smoke/DaemonSmoke` created on the first run, found and rewritten on the second). Against a throwaway commit that hid `ticket_comment_edit` from `ALL_SPECS`, it exited 1 at check 3 naming that tool. Against an earlier seed that crashed the daemon at import, it exited 1 at check 1 (`/healthz` answered 502). With a wrong token in `--token-env`, it exited 1 at check 3 on the 401, and neither that token nor the real one appeared in the output. The seed branch was deleted from `origin` afterwards and `master` restaged

- **`ticket_render_check` and `wiki_render_check` now return the de-duplicated ticket list in `structuredContent`, as `ticket_refs` (Trac /trac_mcp_server #152).** The `Ticket refs (N): ...` line from #151 is in the text block only, and Claude Code shows `structuredContent` in preference to the text, so the one list that exists to catch a mistyped `#N` that hits a real ticket never reached it
  - **New top-level `ticket_refs`**, after `sections` and before `stats`: a list of `{"ticket", "ref", "summary", "first_section"}`. `ticket` is the int (`null` only when no link to it had a `/ticket/N` href), `ref` the key the text line prints (`#N`, or the whitespace-collapsed link text when the href has no `/ticket/N`, uncapped), `summary` Trac's title without its leading `#N: ` or `Comment M for #N: ` (`null` only when no link to that ticket had a title), `first_section` the section the ticket was first seen in (`description`, `comment 3`, `page`), even when a later link supplied the summary. Complete and not shortened: the 50-entry and per-entry caps apply to the text line only, whose overflow tail now reads `(all in structuredContent ticket_refs)` instead of `links.ticket`. `links.ticket` is unchanged
  - **One helper for both**: `_ticket_refs` holds the de-duplication (missing links skipped, first-seen order, the first link's values win; a later link fills in a still-null `ticket` or `summary`, and a `#N: ` titled link replaces a summary that did not come from one) and `_ticket_refs_line` only formats its list, so the two cannot drift. `_section_name` is the bracketless form of `_section_label`, shared by `first_section` and the text block. `docs/reference/mcp-tools.md` documents the key
  - **Tested**: a ticket cited in the description and a comment gives one entry; `first_section` is the first section, including when only a later link has a `#N: ` title (the summary comes from the later one); a missing ticket is absent; a `comment:` link title is stripped; an untitled link and an href with no ticket number give `null` summary / `ticket`; no ticket links gives `[]`; `wiki_render_check` returns it; 60 tickets are all in the structured list while the text line stops at 50 and names `ticket_refs`; and the tickets the text line names equal the structured `ref`s in the same order over a fixture with a duplicate
  - **Seeded**: with `_document_response` building `ticket_refs` by its own pass that drops the `shaped` rule, `test_first_section_stays_first_when_a_later_link_has_the_summary` failed; with that pass returning the list reversed, it and `test_text_line_and_structured_list_share_one_helper` and `test_structured_list_is_complete_while_the_text_line_caps` failed; all passed again once restored
  - **Review fixes**: both tool descriptions said the ticket list was in the text result "for clients that only see text"; they now point at `structuredContent.ticket_refs` as well as the `Ticket refs` line. `_ticket_ref_key` returns `(key, number)`, so `_ticket_refs` no longer searches the href twice, and a fallback key is no longer cut at 80 characters (the text line still caps each entry). A later link now fills in a `ticket` that was `null` (a first link whose href had no `/ticket/N`, keyed by its text `#5`) and a `summary` that was `null` (including from a `Comment M for #N:` title). Tested: each fill-in, an over-80-character fallback `ref` returned whole while the text line entry is capped, and the descriptions. The shared-helper test now compares the text line with `_ticket_refs_line(structuredContent["ticket_refs"])` instead of parsing it. Seeded: with `_document_response` returning the structured list reversed, that test and the 60-ticket test failed; restored, all passed

- **A versioned wiki write can no longer silently replace a write that lands while it is in flight (Trac /trac_mcp_server #129).** Stock `wiki.putPage` ignores its `version` attribute, so `put_wiki_page` checked the version with a separate `wiki.getPageInfo` first (#120); a write by someone else between that read and `putPage` was overwritten, which #130 could only flag after the fact
  - **New plugin method `wiki.putPageIfVersion(pagename, content, attributes, expected_version)`** in `plugins/tracrpc_comment` (component `WikiConditionalPutRPC`, module `tracrpc_comment.wiki_put`; the egg goes to 1.1.0). It loads the page, compares its version and calls `WikiPage.save` inside one `env.db_transaction`; the `wiki` table's `(name, version)` primary key makes a write that commits between the comparison and the `INSERT` fail the `INSERT`, so the call refuses on every backend. An `IntegrityError` is reported as the conflict only if the re-read version differs from `expected_version` (another writer stored first); otherwise the original error propagates. A mismatch raises fault code **409** whose message starts `[wiki-version-conflict current_version=N]` (`none` for a missing page), and stores nothing. `expected_version` 0 means "create only if absent". Saving goes through `WikiPage.save` itself (validation, "Page not modified", change listeners); permissions are `WIKI_VIEW`, then `WIKI_MODIFY`/`WIKI_CREATE`/`WIKI_ADMIN` as for `putPage`, checked against the page's resource. The author is always the caller, and `readonly` changes only when passed
  - **`put_wiki_page` uses it for every versioned write** and maps the conflict fault to the existing `WikiPageConflict`, so `wiki_update`, `wiki_file_push` and `trac-mcp wiki-push` have no new error path and their responses are unchanged. A method-not-found fault (no plugin) falls back to the #120 precheck + `putPage`, unchanged, and is remembered per client for five minutes, so a plugin-less instance pays the extra round trip once per burst while a plugin deployed later is picked up without restarting the daemon. A `version` below 1 (0 or negative; versions start at 1) skips the plugin and is refused by the precheck, so `0` cannot create a page through the plugin path and a negative never reaches the plugin's "non-negative integer" fault, which `wiki_update` would read as a conflict. A create (`version=None`) keeps today's path. The #140 read-back and the #130 `possible_overwrite` check run on both paths. Any other fault from the plugin propagates without TracXMLRPC's `while executing 'wiki.putPageIfVersion()'` suffix, whose "version" `wiki_update` would otherwise read as a conflict
  - **Tested**: the plugin offline against real Trac: a matching version stores, a stale one raises the 409 and stores nothing, permission is refused without `WIKI_MODIFY` on that page (including a policy that denies one page only), and a **forced interleave** on a file-backed SQLite environment -- a second writer on its own thread commits right after the version is read -- is refused with the second write intact. The client with `_rpc_request` mocked: the plugin path is taken, the conflict becomes `WikiPageConflict`, a missing method falls back and the precheck still refuses a stale version, a create never calls the plugin; `wiki_update` and `wiki_file_push` run through the plugin path with a real client. Seeded: re-reading the page before the save (the old check-then-write shape) or dropping the `IntegrityError` mapping fails the interleave tests; dropping the conflict mapping, the fallback or the fault unwrapping fails their tests. Deploying the plugin and the live interleave test on `/trac_test` are left to the local session

- **`convert_preview` can now be told where the draft is going, so `attachment:` and `comment:` links are checked in context (Trac /trac_mcp_server #126).** The preview called `build_warnings` with no attachment list and no comment numbers, so a valid bare `[attachment:FILE label]` link to a file the target holds reported `missing_local_target` at `error`, while the write gate accepted the same text; `dangling_comment_ref` never ran in a preview at all
  - **Two optional, mutually exclusive arguments**: `page_name` (the wiki page the draft will be written to) and `ticket_id` (integer >= 1, the ticket whose description or comment it will become). Both together, a boolean or non-integer or sub-1 `ticket_id`, or an empty `page_name` is a `validation_error`. Neither given leaves the behaviour unchanged: no lookups, no new response line, and `target` is `null` in the structured result
  - **Reuses the gate's helpers**: `write_gate._attachment_names` and `_known_comment_numbers` are now public (`attachment_names`, `known_comment_numbers`). The attachment lookup runs only when the converted TracWiki contains `attachment:` and fails closed (a failed lookup, or a page that does not exist yet, keeps the links as errors); with `ticket_id`, the comment lookup runs only when it contains `comment:` and fails open (no changelog, no `dangling_comment_ref`). `page_name` never fetches a changelog
  - **Response**: a `Checked against: ticket 126` / `Checked against: wiki page Foo/Bar` line right after the summary line, with ` (N attachment(s))` when the attachment lookup returned a list, or ` (attachment list unavailable: bare attachment: links stay errors)` when it failed. The structured result gains `target`: `{"realm": "ticket"|"wiki", "id", "attachments_checked", "comments_checked"}`. `docs/reference/mcp-tools.md` gains the missing `convert_preview` section
  - **Tested**: against mock clients using the write gate's own fixture shapes (`<a class="missing attachment">`, a changelog of 5-tuples). A held file clears the finding for a ticket and for a page; no target, a file the target lacks and a failed lookup each keep it at `error`; no `attachment:`/`comment:` in the content means no lookup; the validation cases refuse. Seeded: with the `attachment_names=` pass-through to `build_warnings` removed, `test_ticket_target_clears_a_held_attachment` and `test_page_target_clears_a_held_attachment` fail; with `known_comment_numbers=` removed, `test_ticket_target_runs_dangling_comment_ref` fails
  - **Review fixes**: a failed comment lookup no longer reads as clean. `target.attachments_checked` and `target.comments_checked` are now tri-state (`null` = lookup not needed, `true` = ran and returned a set, `false` = ran and failed; `comments_checked` is always `null` for `page_name`), and the `Checked against:` line gains ` (comment list unavailable: comment: links not checked)` after any attachment clause when the comment lookup failed. Each argument's own shape is validated before the mutual-exclusion check, so `page_name: ""` with a `ticket_id` reports the empty name. Tested: a raising `get_ticket_changelog` says so and sets `comments_checked` false; unneeded lookups give `null`; seeded with the comment-unavailable clause removed, the new test fails

- **A disabled write gate now says so, on every gated write, in `list_instances` and `ping`, and in the startup log (Trac /trac_mcp_server #153).** `TRAC_WRITE_GATE=0`, left over from a test, sat in the shared daemon's env file and turned the link gate off for every project on the host, and nothing showed it: with the gate off, `gate_or_refuse` returned no lines at all, so a write it never checked looked exactly like a clean checked one. It came to light only when a broken bare ticket link went through
  - **Every gated write carries one line when the gate is off**: `NOTE: the write gate is OFF on this daemon (TRAC_WRITE_GATE=0, or write_gate: false in its config file), so this write was NOT link-checked -- not verified clean.` The write still succeeds; the switch does what it did (ticket #64 ruling 5). `gate_or_refuse` returns it, the same way a gate that could not run returns its `UNCHECKED` note, and all five callers (`ticket_write`, `ticket_comment`, `wiki_write`, `wiki_file`, `milestone`) already append the returned lines on every success path. The line is returned on every call, also for a write with no text, since it reports the daemon, not the content. It names the YAML key as well as the env var, because the switch can be set either way
  - **The batch tools say it too**, once per call rather than per item: they gate each item through `check_write` and never reached `gate_or_refuse`
  - **It is in `structuredContent` too, as `gate_notes`**, on the tools whose handler returns a dict (`wiki_file_push`, `ticket_batch_create`, `ticket_batch_update`): Claude Code shows `structuredContent` in preference to the text block, so a note carried only in the text was invisible there. `wiki_file_push`'s `gate_notes` also carries the gate-on advisory lines it appends to its text. A list, empty when there is nothing to say
  - **`list_instances` and `ping` report `write_gate: true|false`**, top-level in `structuredContent` and as a text line. `ping` now returns `message` (its full text) and `api_version` in `structuredContent` as well, for the same reason. `list_instances` reads it from the client of the call, like `ping`; a caller whose allowlist leaves out the default instance, and so has no client, gets the instance registry's value, read from the default config every routed instance's config is copied from (ticket #117). Both tools print the same line, with an `OFF on this daemon` warning when the gate is off
  - **The startup log gains a `write_gate=on|off` line**, at `WARNING` when the gate is off, written once the lifespan has loaded the Trac config that carries the switch. The `file_access=..., max_inline_bytes=...` line stays where it was, straight after `set_file_access` and before the lifespan, so a failed lifespan validation still leaves it in the journal
  - **Tested**: with the gate off, every gated write path (`ticket_create`, `ticket_update` comment, description and field-only, `ticket_comment_edit`/`_reply`, `wiki_create`, `wiki_update`, `wiki_file_push`, `milestone_create`/`_update`) stores a broken link, carries the line and never renders. With it on, the line is absent. Both batch tools carry it once when off and not at all when on, and `wiki_file_push` and both batch tools carry it in `gate_notes` as well. `list_instances` and `ping` report both states, and the startup log line is tested at both levels and through `main()`, with `file_access` logged even when the lifespan fails. Seeded check: with the line removed from `gate_or_refuse` the twelve gate-off tests failed, and with it removed from the batch helper the two batch tests failed; removing each of the `gate_notes` keys (`wiki_file_push`, the batch tools) and `ping`'s `message`, `api_version` and `write_gate` keys failed the test written for it; all passed again once restored

- **`wiki_conventions`, a read-only tool for an instance's wiki style (Trac #123).** "How do I write a page on this instance?" took a `WikiStart` read, a hand-followed style link and a hunt for template pages; now it is one call with no arguments of its own (the usual optional `instance` routes it)
  - **Returns four things**: `style` (the label of the instance's declaration, or `undeclared` / `malformed`), `declaration_lines` (the marker lines found), `style_page` (the style page's body as stored with its version and the instance it was read from, or `null` when no style was parsed) and `templates` (every `PageTemplates/*` page, name and version and body as stored, sorted by name; the stock `PageTemplates` help page is not one)
  - **The declaration is a line of `WikiStart`, from column 0**: `'''Wiki style:''' [auto_pm:wiki:Rules/documentation/WikiStyleAgent agent]`, or `[wiki:... agent]` for a page on the same instance. Nothing is hardcoded, neither an instance nor a style name. A `{{{ }}}` block is blanked first, so a quoted line never counts. Any line starting `'''Wiki style:'''` (after whitespace) that fails the pattern, or valid lines that disagree, make the style `malformed`; identical duplicates are accepted; no line, or no `WikiStart`, is `undeclared`
  - **A style page on another instance is read within the caller's allowlist** (the InterTrac prefix is the instance's path, same host), through the same registry the dispatcher uses. A refusal never fails the call: `style_page` carries `available: false` and a `reason` (`instance_not_allowed`, `unknown_instance`, `not_found`, `permission_denied` or `error`) with the message, and the style and templates are still returned
  - **Tested**: the parser as a pure function (labels, same-instance and prefixed links, garbage, leading whitespace, missing label, disagreeing and duplicate lines, fenced lines, empty text) and the handler against a fake client and registry; a live test on `/trac_test` writes a probe template and a probe style page, appends a declaration to `WikiStart` and restores it. Seeded parser defects (fences not blanked, leading whitespace accepted) each fail their tests

- **A wiki update that may have overwritten someone else's write is now flagged (Trac /trac_mcp_server #130).** `put_wiki_page` refuses a stale `version` before calling `wiki.putPage` (#120), but a write that lands between that check and `putPage` is still replaced without anyone noticing: A checks version N, B stores N+1, A stores N+2, and A got a plain success. Since #140 the page is read back after the write, so the evidence was already there
  - **Detect only, no extra RPC** (preventing it is #129). When `version` was given and the read-back says anything other than `version + 1`, `put_wiki_page`'s result carries `possible_overwrite: True` and `intervening_versions` (`version + 1 .. new - 1`). Every result now carries `possible_overwrite`; a create (`version=None`) never sets it, and a failed read-back (`WikiPageStoredReadbackFailed`) is unchanged, since it says nothing about versions
  - **`wiki_update` and `wiki_file_push`** add a warning line naming the intervening versions and suggesting `wiki_diff(page_name=..., from_version=N, to_version=...)` to inspect them. The result stays a success: the page was stored. `wiki_file_push`'s structured result carries `possible_overwrite` next to `readback_failed` (plus `intervening_versions` when true); both are documented in `docs/reference/mcp-tools.md`
  - **`trac-mcp wiki-push`** prints `trac-mcp: warning: possible overwrite ...` on stderr and still exits **0**: the write succeeded, and a non-zero code would read as "not stored" and invite a retry that writes the page again
  - **Caveat**: a write that lands just *after* this one, before the read-back, also raises the flag, and the read-back then reports that later version (the reported `version` was already that later one before this change). Hence "possible", and a warning rather than an error
  - **Tested**: a real `TracClient` with `_rpc_request` mocked. An update sent with version 3 that reads back 5 is flagged with intervening version 4 in the client result, and warns in `wiki_update` and `wiki_file_push`. One that reads back 4, and a create, stay quiet. Each flag/warning test failed with the fix removed (on `master`, and against seeded mutants of the client check, each tool's warning, the push's structured flag and the CLI's stderr line); the quiet-path tests failed against a client that flags every update. The live check that an uncontended write stays quiet on the test instance is left to the local session
  - **Review fixes**: a float `version` such as 3.0 (a JSON-schema "integer") no longer raises `TypeError` after the page is stored, and the warning says that if the diff is exactly this write's own change, nothing was dropped

- **A Markdown list item that continues after its nested list now draws an advisory (Trac #125).** Trac cannot continue a list item once a nested list has started: it ends the list, renders the next block outside it (a paragraph as a blockquote, a fenced code block as a `<pre>`) and starts a new list for the following item. Since #112 the converter keeps an item's children in source order, so `- Cause: unknown. Suspects:` + a nested list + an indented paragraph + `- Guard: next item.` converts faithfully and then renders split, with nothing to tell the author (measured live on `/trac_test`)
  - **New code `list_item_split`, severity `warning`** (never `error`, so the write gate does not block it; listed in `ADVISORY_CODES`). The content survives and only the list structure splits, so refusing would be wrong; the message names the item, the block that follows the nested list, and the remedy (move the block out of the list in the source, or accept the split). Detection is `find_list_item_splits`/`describe_list_item_split` in `converters/markdown_to_tracwiki.py`, parsing with the converter's own mistune AST so the two agree on what is a list and what sits inside a fence. Nested items count (`depth` 1 for an item of a nested list), and one finding is recorded per item
  - **Where it runs**: `convert_preview` with `format` markdown, and `wiki_file_push` on a Markdown push, where it is appended to the response's `warnings` ("Conversion warnings:") and never refuses the push. TracWiki-declared writes skip it, as they skip the other Markdown-source checks
  - **Recall sweep**: both stores refreshed 2026-11-03, 2012 documents (wiki pages, ticket descriptions and comments; the stock `TracLinks` page is excluded from both stores because it faults on render). Each stored TracWiki source was run through `tracwiki_to_markdown` and then the detector: **0 documents raise it**, so there is no existing population to be noisy for. A seeded check shows the sweep can see the shape: the converter's TracWiki output for the ticket's input, read back through `tracwiki_to_markdown`, does raise it. Reading the raw stored sources as if they were Markdown gives 2 hits, both the ticket's own example quoted inside a TracWiki `{{{ }}}` block (#112, #125), unreachable because TracWiki-declared writes skip the Markdown checks
  - **Tested**: the ticket's input yields exactly one finding (item, block `paragraph`, depth 0) and one `warning` through `build_warnings`; silent controls for a sub-list with nothing after it and for the same input inside a fenced block; a fenced code block after the sub-list, an ordered parent and a depth-1 split are reported; item text holding a code span or `[[...]]` is reported restored with no NUL byte; the gate does not block it; `wiki_file_push` stores the page and carries the message. The seeded tests failed against a detector stubbed to return nothing before the real one was written

- `ticket_check_list`: checks a whole list of ticket numbers, or texts in summary/description/keywords, against Trac in one call and returns which exist (with the requested fields) and which are missing, instead of one `ticket_search`/`ticket_get` call per value. Values are comma or new line separated, or a JSON array; texts match as whole words; an optional query restricts where to look (Trac #139, PR 116)
  - **Review fixes (Trac #139)**: the caller's `query` is refused if it could override the chunk query (`max`, `page`, `order`, `desc`, `group`, `groupdesc`, `col`, `format`, `report`, `verbose`, `rows`, `row`, an `or` clause, a clause without `=`, or the chunk's own field); `match_field` must be a plain field name. Text values are escaped, not blanked: the Trac query searches the longest segment of a term (split on whitespace, `\` and `"`, leading `-` stripped) with `|` and `&` escaped, and the whole-word filter still decides on the full term; a term with nothing to search is listed in `unsearchable`. One failed `get_ticket` no longer fails the call: its id is in `failed_ids`. Unset fields are `""`, not null; a requested `id` field keeps the real id; `²`, `##5` and `#` are `not_a_ticket_number`; `values="[]"` is an empty-values error; each hit carries its `url`
  - **Review fixes, round 2 (Trac #139)**: `match_field` is checked against the instance's real fields (`get_ticket_fields`, text mode only) and must be the exact name of a `text` or `textarea` field, else a `validation_error` lists the allowed ones; the shape check is `fullmatch`, so `"summary\n"` no longer passes. The query needle depends on the field: a list field (`type` text, `format` list: `keywords`, `cc`) keeps the longest word, any other field searches the whole value (`disk quota exceeded`), split only at a backslash; the literal `$USER`, which Trac replaces with the caller's name, separates segments in both. Each ticket is fetched once per call and a failure is not retried. In text mode a value with no match while a candidate of its batch failed to load is listed in `unresolved`, not `missing`. In id mode a ticket that exists but failed to load stays in `found` with `id` and `url` only (and in `failed_ids`), so `found_count` no longer depends on `only_missing`. A value of more than 18 digits is `not_a_ticket_number` instead of reaching `int()`. `url` cannot be requested as a field (as for `id`). Docs: `only_missing` in text mode still fetches every candidate; SQLite folds case for ASCII only, so a non-ASCII case difference can be reported missing
