- OIDC per-user auth for the http transport, for shared/multi-tenant deployments (e.g. one `trac-mcp-server` serving many users through a gateway like LibreChat, each with their own Keycloak/OIDC identity) where a single static bearer token would misattribute every action to one shared identity
  - New `TRAC_MCP_OIDC_RPC_URL` / `server.oidc_rpc_url` setting: when configured, every request must carry the caller's own OIDC access token in the standard `Authorization: Bearer <token>` header, forwarded verbatim to that URL (typically an Apache + `mod_auth_openidc`-protected Trac endpoint, validating the token and mapping it to a Trac username) -- the standard header, not a custom one, matches how an MCP client's own OAuth flow per server (e.g. LibreChat's `oauth:` config) actually attaches a token
  - No fallback to a shared service-account identity, by design: a request missing a bearer token gets `401` at the transport layer (new `OidcTokenRequiredMiddleware`) before it ever reaches tool dispatch, and `TRAC_USERNAME`/`TRAC_PASSWORD` are not required in this mode -- `Config.oidc_only` relaxes that requirement and skips the startup connectivity self-test (there's no shared credential to test with) unless real credentials are configured anyway
  - Mutually exclusive with the existing static `TRAC_MCP_AUTH_TOKEN` gate -- both are read off the same `Authorization` header for different purposes, so `validate_server_config()` now rejects configuring both at once; `oidc_rpc_url` alone satisfies the existing non-loopback-bind safety check
  - `instance` argument (multi-instance addressing) is rejected with a clear error in this mode rather than silently ignored -- OIDC per-user auth serves only its single configured endpoint
  - Per-token `TracClient` cache (`mcp/oidc.py`: `OidcClientCache`), bounded to 256 most-recently-used tokens with FIFO eviction, so a session's calls reuse one connection pool instead of paying for a fresh one per call
  - New `src/trac_mcp_server/mcp/oidc.py` (`OidcClientCache`, `extract_bearer_token`); `TracClient`/`Config` gained `bearer_token`/`rpc_url_override`/`oidc_only` for the Bearer-vs-Basic-auth and RPC-URL-override split this required
  - `TRAC_MCP_OIDC_RPC_URL` is checked at startup: it must be an http(s) URL with a host, and plain `http` on a non-loopback host needs `TRAC_MCP_OIDC_ALLOW_INSECURE_HTTP=true`, since every request forwards a user's token to it. It is refused outside the http transport
  - `list_instances` reports only the configured endpoint and the write gate status (#153) in this mode and makes no outbound call: its project discovery would authenticate as the shared account
  - The middleware and dispatch read the same one of several `Authorization` headers, the first; an evicted client's pooled connections are closed (`TracClient.close()`)
  - `ticket_update` finds a new comment's number in this mode: the lookup filtered by a username the client does not have, so every comment got a warning instead
  - `tests/test_mcp/test_oidc_no_shared_identity.py` runs every registered tool with the transport replaced and asserts that nothing but the caller's token goes out
  - Runnable setup with Keycloak, Apache and Trac: [`examples/oidc/`](examples/oidc/README.md)
  - New docs: [HTTP Transport Reference: OIDC Per-User Auth](docs/reference/http-transport.md#oidc-per-user-auth), including a LibreChat integration example; updated README and deployment docs
- **A render where a code span collided with bold or italic markup is no longer reported clean (Trac #115).** `'''Contact point `telegram-operator''''` makes Trac close the bold first; the span then swallows the quotes, the next backtick comes out as literal text and the bold flips for the rest of the line. `literal_markup_in_render` looks for five other patterns, so `ticket_render_check` answered `0 warnings` twice on 2026-10-19
  - **New code `broken_inline_markup_in_render`, severity `warning`** (not blocking), one finding per pattern, named in `evidence.pattern`: `backtick_in_prose` (a backtick outside any code element), `code_span_with_quote_run` (a span holding a `'''` run that it did not open with; `''` alone is an empty-string literal and stays silent), `empty_code_span` (an empty `<code>`, a span cut in two), `quote_run_in_prose` (a `''` or `'''` run left as text). It reads `facts` only, so `convert_preview`, the render checks and the write gate all see it
  - **Why a warning, not an error**: a sweep of the cached corpus (1,020 documents, both stores, cache of 2026-09-03, which predates the two 2026-10-19 comments) found legitimate hits for every pattern -- pages that document `'''bold'''` or a literal backtick -- so none is blocking. Expect some noise at `warning`: a `''` run or a literal backtick in a sentence. A span that opens with its quotes (`'''bold'''`) and a span holding only `''` (`name = ''`) stay silent, and `<code>` inside a `<pre>` block is never scanned (`PreviewFacts.inline_code_spans`)
  - **Tested**: offline cases built from the ticket's two real renders, silent controls for the shapes above, and a live test that pushes the ticket's replication string through the real renderer. The offline positive tests failed on `master` before the check existed; the live test was written after the check and was not observed failing

- **`trac-mcp` gets a file route for ticket bodies (Trac #114).** Ticket descriptions and comments had none, so every long body was typed inline into `ticket_create`/`ticket_update` -- the path auto_pm `Rules/trac/LongBodiesGoThroughFilePush` warns against, because a model-serialized string can be stored JSON-escaped (literal `\n`) and a render check still passes it. Four new subcommands; a program reads the file and sends its bytes, so the body never passes through a transcript
  - **`ticket-comment TICKET FILE [--reply-to N]`, `ticket-describe TICKET FILE`, `ticket-create --summary S --description-file FILE [--type --component --keywords --owner --priority --milestone]`, `ticket-pull TICKET OUT [--comment N]`.** Bodies are read as UTF-8 and sent verbatim as TracWiki (no format detection, no conversion; the rule #62/#69 set for inline writes). `ticket-pull` writes the stored description, or one comment, byte for byte, so `cmp` against the pushed file verifies the write. A whitespace-only body is a usage error (exit 2); the body that is sent is never stripped
  - **Optimistic locking by default**: `ticket-comment` and `ticket-describe` read the ticket's `_ts` just before writing and pass it as `base_ts`; a change in between is a `version_conflict` (exit 1) and nothing is stored. `--base-ts` supplies the token and skips the read. A ticket with no `_ts` is refused rather than written unlocked
  - **The comment number is read, never counted**: after the write, `ticket-comment` finds its own body in `ticket_changelog` and prints that comment's stored number, so a gap left by a deleted comment cannot misreport it. If it cannot be found (or the read-back call fails) the command prints the write's result first, then exits **4** saying the comment *was* stored -- exit 1 stays "nothing stored", so a caller that retries on 1 cannot post the comment twice. A comment over the server's 10000-character limit is refused with exit 2 before any call; stdin is read as bytes so CRLF survives as it does from a file
  - **Same gates as the tools**: every write goes through the existing `ticket_*` tools, so the write-time link gate and notes still run and are printed. `--type` is sent as `ticket_type`
  - **Tested on the real substrate**: the CLI's real `main()` against the production server over real uvicorn and an in-memory ticket store, a body of quotes, backslashes, a literal `\n`, a tab, an em dash and a `{{{` block round-tripping byte-exact through each write command and `ticket-pull`. Seeded defects (no `base_ts`, comment number counted, body stripped before send or before write-out, `type` for `ticket_type`) each fail a test

- **Inline forms for the seven file tools, and the `trac-mcp` CLI (Trac #111).** `wiki_file_push`, `wiki_file_pull`, `wiki_file_detect_format` and the ticket and wiki `*_attachment_put`/`*_attachment_get` pairs resolved every `file_path`/`output_path` on the *server's* filesystem. That was harmless while the http daemon ran on its callers' machine; once it moved into a jail on another host (auto_pm #186), a caller's path no longer existed there, and `wiki_file_push` answered `File not found` for every LAN client
  - **Each tool now also takes its bytes inline**: `content` (+ an optional `filename` for extension-based format detection) for the wiki-file tools, `content_base64` (+ a required `filename`; line-wrapped base64 accepted) for attachment uploads. Where `file_access` is `off`, `wiki_file_pull` returns the text in `structuredContent.content` and `*_attachment_get` returns `structuredContent.content_base64`. Inline payloads in either direction are refused rather than truncated above the new `max_inline_bytes` setting (default 5 MiB)
  - **Inline `content` with neither `filename` nor `format` is stored as TracWiki, verbatim** -- no content re-detect, the rule #62/#69 set for every inline write
  - **With `file_access: local` the output tools keep their old contract**: `file_path`/`output_path` is required, so a stdio model that forgets it gets an error rather than an attachment's bytes in its transcript
  - **`trac-mcp`, a third console script**, is the caller-side half: it reads and writes local files and calls those tools over streamable HTTP with the inline forms (`wiki-push`, `wiki-pull`, `attach-put`, `attach-get`; `detect-format` runs locally). A program serializes the bytes, so they never enter a model's transcript -- which is what the path design existed to guarantee, and what an inline `wiki_update` body gets wrong (literal `\n`, auto_pm `Rules/trac/LongBodiesGoThroughFilePush`). It connects with the nearest `.mcp.json`'s `trac-http` entry, expanding `${VAR}` in its URL and `Authorization` header the way Claude Code does, so it runs as the same Trac identity as the project's MCP session with no setup; `--url`/`--token-env` and `TRAC_MCP_URL`/`TRAC_MCP_AUTH_TOKEN` are the fallbacks
  - **Tested on the real substrate**: the CLI's real `main()` drives the production server (real registry, dispatch and instance annotation) over real uvicorn, against an in-memory Trac, and a page body full of quotes, backslashes, tabs and an indented code block, plus a 256-value binary attachment, round-trip byte-exact; a live test does the same through `/trac_test`. Seeded defects (one byte dropped from an upload, backslashes stripped from a body) fail those tests

- **Native multi-identity support in the shared HTTP transport (Trac #102).** A `TRAC_IDENTITIES` file maps several distinct bearer tokens to their own Trac username/password, so one shared daemon can serve several operators under their own Trac identities -- replacing today's one-process-per-identity deployment. `BearerAuthMiddleware` now checks a request's token against *every* configured token (the static one and every identity's), stashing a match on `scope["trac_identity"]`; `mcp/server.py`'s two dispatch sites (`handle_call_tool`, `handle_read_resource`) read it back via `_caller_identity()` and pass it into `InstanceRegistry.resolve()`/`get_client()`
  - **Precedence: a declared instance's own explicit credentials always win over the caller's identity.** Only *inherited* credentials -- the default instance itself, a declared instance that leaves `username`/`password` unset, or ad-hoc same-host addressing -- are replaced by the identity's. Otherwise one identity's password could reach a host a different declared instance deliberately points credentials at
  - **The client cache key now includes the password**, not just the URL and username: two identities sharing a username but a different password would otherwise have the second silently reuse the first's already-authenticated `TracClient`
  - **`list_instances`'s discovery scrape now authenticates as the caller's own identity**, not whichever identity happened to start the process -- `describe()` itself stays identity-agnostic, since it never returns credentials, only names and URLs
  - **Identities alone (no static `TRAC_MCP_AUTH_TOKEN`) satisfy the same non-loopback bind-safety check the static token does** -- they gate every request exactly like it, so requiring a token in addition would be redundant, not safer
  - **Validated at startup**: every identity needs a non-empty token/username/password, every token must be unique (including against `TRAC_MCP_AUTH_TOKEN`), `TRAC_IDENTITIES` on the `stdio` transport is rejected (no per-request signal for stdio to read an identity from), and `TRAC_IDENTITIES` with `allow_unauthenticated` and no static token is rejected (declaring identities and then opting out of authentication is contradictory)
  - **Backward compatible**: a deployment using only `TRAC_MCP_AUTH_TOKEN` -- no `TRAC_IDENTITIES` at all -- behaves exactly as it did before this ticket, token-for-token
  - **Tested against the real substrate, not mocks** (`Rules/testing/RealSubstrateNotMocks`): a new hermetic suite drives real `uvicorn` + the SDK's real `streamablehttp_client`/`ClientSession` (no `TestClient`, no `AsyncMock`) through ~50 interleaved calls per session across two concurrently-connected identities, confirms the legacy static token still resolves to no identity, and confirms identity is read per REQUEST rather than cached per session (a request carrying session A's `mcp-session-id` but session B's bearer token resolves to B). A seeded-defect run (`Rules/testing/SeededDefectFirst`) froze `_caller_identity()` to whichever identity resolved first and confirmed the concurrency check then fails, before reverting. `ci-live.sh` gained a full production-server live test writing to `/trac_test` as two real Trac accounts concurrently and reading each write's `author` back to confirm it matches
  - **`ci-live.sh` now requires a second real Trac account**, `TRAC_USERNAME_2`/`TRAC_PASSWORD_2`, and its credential pre-check exits non-zero before pytest runs if either is missing -- not a silent skip (the shape `ci-live.sh` itself exists to prevent, Trac #81)

- **Every tool result now names the instance it actually reached (Trac #94).** The optional `instance` argument (#18) falls back to the server's single legacy `TRAC_URL` triple when omitted, and that fallback was silent — no field in the result said which Trac project the call landed on. For a read that is confusing; for a write it is a correctness hazard, because a page or ticket that exists on both the intended instance and the default one is written to the wrong project and reports success. Results now carry `instance` (the resolved path, e.g. `/bcs`) and `instance_source` (`explicit` / `server_default`)
  - **Measured before it was designed: nine slips across eight sessions since 2026-08**, every one of them caught only by luck — seven `wiki_update` calls on a `/bcs` page refused by the link checker because that page's local links do not exist on the default instance, and two `ticket_update` closes that got `not_found` because the default instance happens not to carry those numbers. A wrong-instance write whose target name exists on both instances leaves no trace at all, which is the case this removes
  - **Both surfaces carry it, because which one a client shows its model is the client's choice.** `structuredContent` gets the two keys when the handler produced a dict — Claude Code surfaces that in preference to the text block — and the content list gets one extra `TextContent`. Appended as its own block rather than folded into the existing text: `ticket_component_list` and `ticket_enum_list` return `json.dumps(...)` as their entire text body, so `content[0]` must stay parseable byte-for-byte. An existing key of either name is never overwritten
  - **Annotated at the one dispatch chokepoint**, after instance resolution, so it covers error results too — including `unknown_tool` — and no handler had to change. The `unknown_instance` branch is deliberately *not* annotated: nothing was reached, so there is no instance to name
  - **The ticket's second suggestion was considered and not taken.** Treating an omitted argument as an error once a session has used an explicit one needs per-session state, and this daemon is shared: one process serves every project at once, so a process-global "this caller addresses several instances" flag would leak across concurrent sessions from different projects. The offline equivalent — flagging the same shape in transcripts after the fact, where session state is free — is auto_pm #107
  - **Schema note:** the `instance` parameter description changed, so an already-connected session needs a reconnect to see it. Tool behaviour is unchanged for callers that ignore the new fields
- **A realm-less, slashed InterTrac target on a LOCAL dispatcher is now caught (Trac #86).** `auto_pm:Rules/trac/RenderVerify` (no `wiki:` realm, a full-path page name) renders as a perfectly plausible link, but this host's Trac dispatcher misreads the first path segment as a realm and 302s to a URL missing `/wiki/`, which 404s — whether or not the target page exists. No live probe fires, because `is_probeable_href` only recognises the `wiki:`/`ticket:` realm forms. New code `missing_intertrac_realm` (`error`, blocking) needs no fetch to know the shape is dead
  - **Scoped to LOCAL dispatchers only, and that scoping is the actual fix.** Measured against `trac.edgewall.org` the same identical shape resolves *correctly* upstream, so the check cannot be a pure function of href shape — `build_warnings` gains an optional `local_intertrac_bases` parameter (the caller's instance table), and the new code fires only when a realm-less/slashed target's dispatcher base is in that set. Empty (the default) degrades to the check never firing, which is the status quo this ticket is about, not a regression
  - **Recall gate found the ticket's own premise needed correcting.** The corpus's 26 findings across 18 documents matching the shape (reproduced exactly) are *all* `trac:`-prefixed links to `trac.edgewall.org` — none local — so the properly-scoped check reports **zero** findings on real content, not 26. Authored population is 0 either way

- **The write gate now covers milestone descriptions (Trac #87).** `milestone_create` and `milestone_update` accept a `description` that is TracWiki, stored verbatim and rendered by Trac — the same substrate as a wiki page body or a ticket description — but they were not among the six write paths #64 enumerated, so a broken link in one was written silently while every other surface refused it. Both handlers now gate on the description as submitted, and both gained the shared `target_cap` parameter
  - **Measured rather than assumed, per the ticket's own caution.** A milestone description does render through the wiki formatter: on a live milestone page Trac marks a dead local target `class="missing wiki"` — the *same* classes `wiki_to_html` hands the gate — so what the gate refuses is what a reader of the milestone page would have seen. The `preview-checks: allow <code>` hatch needed no work either: it is matched against the source line, not the render, so it was already field-agnostic
  - **The live rows run against `/trac_test`**, not the project's own instance, because they create and delete a real milestone and there is no scratch milestone to reset the way #84 seeded a scratch wiki page. Each row asserts the store, not the response: a refused create leaves no milestone behind, and a refused update leaves the previous description byte-for-byte intact

- **`ci-live.sh`, the half of the suite `ci.sh` cannot run (Trac #81).** `ci.sh` passes no `--run-live`, so every `@pytest.mark.live` test is skipped — and the live suite is the only place this project exercises the real Trac substrate. Two defects landed green through `ci.sh` in one session: a live test still asserting the false positive #79 had just removed, and `stats.targets_checked` counting skipped targets as checked. Neither was found by the change that caused it; both were found later, by accident
  - **`--run-live` was deliberately not added to `ci.sh`.** That script stays hermetic — no credentials, no daemon, no network — so an ordinary local run can only fail for reasons belonging to the change under test. Making it depend on live Trac would be this ticket's own failure mode, inverted
  - **Absent credentials exit non-zero without running pytest at all**, so there is no green summary line to misread. Same shape as #80's `target_check_skipped`, one layer out: *not run* must not be able to print like *passed*. Credentials are resolved the way the tests resolve them — `.env` then the environment — so a developer with a working `.env` is not told they have none
- `ticket_search`/`ticket_get` results now include each ticket's web URL (`{TRAC_URL}/ticket/{id}`, in both the text response and `structuredContent`). Without it, an agent asked to "include links" had no way to know the Trac host and would guess -- often producing a link to the MCP client's own host instead of Trac's. (GitHub PR 113, Trac #135)
- **A warning suite rule for damage that is invisible: `code_block_indentation_loss` (Trac #68).** Every other rule catches something a reader can *see* is wrong — literal markup, a dead link, a reference stuck in a code span. Feeding a TracWiki `{{{ }}}` block to the Markdown converter strips the leading whitespace from its body (the block is not recognised as a code construct, so ordinary paragraph handling eats it), storing syntactically invalid code with an **empty** warning list under a render that looks entirely plausible. It is visible only in the stored bytes, and it was committed twice inside #62 — a ticket whose subject was that exact corruption — caught both times only by reading the source back by hand
  - **`tracwiki_markup_in_markdown` could not have been widened to cover it.** That rule runs `blank_code_fences` first, which blanks a `{{{ ... }}}` region *including its delimiters*, so the `{{{` it scans for is erased before the scan reaches it. The shape that gets destroyed is precisely the shape it is built to ignore; #65's narrowing and this check are complements, not overlapping
  - **Detection lives in `converters/common.py`, not in the preview module**, so all three surfaces that still convert can reach it — `preview` is not packaged into the `trac-convert` binary, and #68 comment 4 measured `wiki_file_push` storing damaged bytes without ever calling `build_warnings`
  - **`wiki_file_push` refuses rather than warns.** An `error`-severity finding on a path that then stores the damaged bytes anyway would leave the corruption in the page and the recovery to whoever reads the response. `format="tracwiki"` still pushes the same file verbatim — the check gates on the declared format, not on the content. This is the one remaining write path that converts; #69 left it converting because it has a *filename* to go on
  - `convert_preview` reports it as a structured warning at severity `error` (the precedent is #59, where `intertrac_target_captured_punctuation` became an error for naming a genuinely dead link), gated off for TracWiki-declared input, where nothing converts and nothing can be lost. `trac-convert` reports it on stderr and still writes its output: that binary stores nothing, so the caller still holds the input
  - **Deliberately narrow, and measured that way.** Blocks are paired by their stripped body content rather than by position (a positional rule skips any document whose block counts differ, e.g. one holding both an indented-Markdown block and a damaged `{{{` one — a false negative on a real defect), and indentation is compared *relative* to each block's own minimum (mistune legitimately dedents a fenced block nested in a list item, which absolute comparison called loss). Two accepted residuals: a uniform dedent of a whole block, and two identical damaged blocks, both stay silent. Zero new warnings across all 72 preview fixture rows, the store corpus, and every Markdown file in the repo
- Streamable HTTP transport (Trac #26): `trac-mcp-server --transport http` serves MCP over `POST/GET/DELETE <path>` (default `/mcp`) via a Starlette app run by `uvicorn`, alongside the unchanged stdio default
  - Stateful sessions (`Mcp-Session-Id`, the MCP SDK default), so one long-lived process can serve multiple concurrent clients/sessions
  - Optional static bearer token auth (`TRAC_MCP_AUTH_TOKEN` / `server.auth_token`), checked with `secrets.compare_digest`; unauthenticated requests get `401` + `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer`
  - Bind-safety guard: refuses to bind a non-loopback host for `--transport http` unless a token is configured or `--allow-unauthenticated` is explicitly passed
  - DNS-rebinding protection via the MCP SDK's `TransportSecuritySettings` (`Host`/`Origin` allow-listing, extensible via `server.allowed_hosts`/`allowed_origins`)
  - Unauthenticated `GET /healthz` for container liveness/readiness probes
  - New `--transport`, `--host`, `--port`, `--path`, `--allow-unauthenticated` CLI flags and `TRAC_MCP_TRANSPORT`/`TRAC_MCP_HOST`/`TRAC_MCP_PORT`/`TRAC_MCP_PATH`/`TRAC_MCP_AUTH_TOKEN` env vars, plus a new `server:` YAML config section — no `--auth-token` CLI flag by design (would leak into the process list)
  - New `src/trac_mcp_server/mcp/http_app.py`: `BearerAuthMiddleware`, `build_http_app()`, `run_http()`
  - `Server("trac-mcp-server", version=__version__)` now reports the project version over HTTP too (previously only the stdio path set this, so `StreamableHTTPSessionManager`'s auto-generated init options would have reported the `mcp` SDK version)
  - New docs: [HTTP Transport Reference](docs/reference/http-transport.md); updated README, CLI, configuration, and deployment docs
- Multi-instance Trac support (Trac #18): every MCP tool and the `trac://wiki/*` resource now accept an optional `instance` argument that routes the call to another Trac project instead of the default one, with no server restart required
  - Named instances declared via a new `instances:` section in config files, or an out-of-band `TRAC_INSTANCES` file (YAML/JSON), with live reload on edit
  - Ad-hoc addressing (`instance: "/project-path"`) reaches any project on the same Trac host as the default instance using the default's credentials, restricted to the default instance's exact scheme+host so credentials can never be sent to another host
  - New `list_instances` tool (28th documented tool, 43rd registered including `ping`) surfaces configured instances plus, via a new `scrape_project_index()` web-scraping helper, other projects discoverable on the host's "Available Projects" index
  - `src/trac_mcp_server/instances.py`: `InstanceSpec`, `InstanceRegistry` (resolve/get_client/describe, with per-URL client caching and mtime-gated live reload), `UnknownInstanceError`, `load_declared_instances()`

### Changed
- **The live render-check row for a bare humped word no longer reads a wiki page: it renders the sentence itself (Trac /trac_mcp_server #225).**
  - **Why**: #225 retires the 37 April-2026 mirror and test-litter pages on `/trac_mcp_server`, and `test_project_milestones_reports_its_autolink_as_incidental` render-checked one of them, `ProjectMilestones`, only for its bare `GitHub` (#79, #106). Deleting the page would have turned `ci-live.sh` red.
  - **What**: renamed `test_bare_humped_word_renders_plain_under_ignore_missing_pages`. It renders a line taken from that page with `wiki_to_html` on the live suite's default instance and runs `build_warnings` over it, as the #115 row does. The assertions are unchanged: neither `incidental_wiki_autolink` nor `missing_local_target` fires, and no `/wiki/GitHub` anchor appears.
  - **Seeded**: the same row with the sentence's `GitHub` written as an authored `[wiki:GitHub GitHub]` link fails on the real Trac; restored, it passes.
- **`wiki_file_pull format=html` and `trac-mcp wiki-pull --format html` now rewrite the `href` of each link: an in-page anchor becomes `#frag` and `link_rewrites` / `--link-map` applies, and every other byte of Trac's render is unchanged (Trac /trac_mcp_server #219).**
  - **Why**: Trac renders an in-page anchor (a `[[PageOutline]]` table of contents, `[#Heading x]`) as the absolute `<trac>/wiki/<Page>#Heading`, so a published HTML export jumped into Trac, and `link_rewrites` was ignored for html. The Markdown export already handled both; the HTML export now decides each link the same way
  - **What**: the URL decisions moved from `html_to_markdown.py` into `converters/link_targets.py` as `LinkTargets(link_map, document_url).map(url)` (the old `_absolute_path`, `_in_page_anchor` and `_map_url`, unchanged in behaviour; the Markdown converter delegates to it and its tests are unmodified). The new `rewrite_hrefs(html, link_map, document_url)` applies it to HTML. `_handle_pull` chooses the map for `html` as for `markdown` (the call's array, validated before any Trac read, else the daemon's map, else none), reports `link_rewrites_source` for it, and runs Trac's HTML through `rewrite_hrefs` before the minimal document is built. `tracwiki` still ignores the map and reports `none`
  - **Byte for byte**: the HTML is not parsed into a tree and written back, which would normalise quotes and entities. `html.parser` locates the start tags and each tag's own text is tokenised for its attributes, so only a real `href` attribute (name in any case; double-quoted, single-quoted or unquoted) is touched: not `data-href`, not `href=` text inside another attribute's value, not text, `<pre>` content, comments or script/style content, and not `src`. The value is unescaped before it is mapped; a link whose target does not change keeps its original bytes (an `&amp;` stays as Trac wrote it), a changed one is written `html.escape`d inside its own quote (double quotes for an unquoted value), and an empty `href` is left alone
  - **Tested**: `tests/test_converter_rewrite_hrefs.py` (in-page anchor with and without a map, longest prefix, an unmapped link identical, `data-href`, `href=` inside a `title` value, single-quoted, unquoted and `HREF`, `&amp;` kept and re-escaped, `<pre>` text, comments, script and style, several links per line, non-ASCII text and CR/CRLF line ends, `img src`; for every `tests/fixtures/trac_html/*.html` the output equals the input with no map and no document URL, and with both differs only in `href` values), `TestPullHtmlLinkRewrites` in `tests/test_mcp/tools/test_wiki_file.py` (call, config and empty-call maps, `link_rewrites_source`, a bad map failing before any read, tracwiki unchanged) and a live row `test_pull_html_rewrites_anchors_and_maps_the_page_link` (PageOutline TOC and `[#Beta jump]` as `#...`, then a `WikiStart` link mapped by a prefix read off the render). Written first and seen failing on the unchanged code (`link_targets` did not exist; the handler tests and the CLI rows failed on the old html behaviour)
  - **CLI**: `wiki-pull --format html` sends `--link-map`, else the local `export.link_rewrites`, as `--format markdown` does (a broken local config is now a usage error for html too); only `--format tracwiki` warns `no effect` and sends none. The fallback for a pre-#213 server that refuses `link_rewrites` applies to an html pull unchanged; a pre-#219 server ignores the map for html and `wiki-pull` prints `the server did not confirm the link map was applied`. `--format` and `--link-map` help text and `docs/reference/{mcp-tools,cli,configuration}.md` updated
  - **Review fixes**: (1) an attribute value is unescaped the HTML5 way, so `?a=1&not=2` stays the URL it looks like (a `;`-less legacy named reference before a letter, digit or `=` is literal) instead of becoming `?a=1¬=2`; (2) attributes are read with `html.parser`'s own `attrfind_tolerant` and loop, so `<a =foo href=...>` is rewritten and `href=="..."` yields the value HTMLParser reports (a test compares both on odd tags); (3) the content of `title`, `textarea`, `xmp`, `iframe`, `noembed`, `noframes` and `plaintext` is text, so a link written there is left alone; (4) `wiki_file_pull` builds the document URL with `page_url()`, so a page named `Rate%20Card` (rendered `/wiki/Rate%2520Card#H`) gets `#H`; (5) the edits are applied in one forward pass, not one string rebuild per edit; (6) docs and tool text say an in-page anchor is written `#frag` and never mapped by `link_rewrites`, no longer "never rewritten"
- **The live rows can reach `/trac_test` on its own origin: `TRAC_TEST_URL` names it, and every row that built the URL from `TRAC_URL`'s host and port now goes through `tests/live_helpers.py` (Trac /trac_mcp_server #203).**
  - **What**: `trac_test_url()` returns `TRAC_TEST_URL` (trailing `/` stripped) when it is set and non-empty, else `/trac_test` on `TRAC_URL`'s scheme, host and port as before. A value that is not an absolute `http(s)` URL with a host, or whose path does not end with `/trac_test`, raises a `ValueError` naming the variable and the defect (never the value, which can carry userinfo), rather than letting the write guard refuse every write or a scheme-less value be joined onto `TRAC_URL`'s host. The new `trac_test_registry(default_config)` declares `/trac_test` on that URL, so a server, CLI or handler stack that resolves `instance="/trac_test"` lands on it (a declared name is looked up before the ad-hoc `/path` join); it inherits the default credentials and a per-caller identity replaces them, as on the ad-hoc path, and it keeps `/trac_test` declared when the registry reloads its instance files mid-run. 19 live rows moved off their own derivation: 13 built a client from `InstanceRegistry(config, {}).resolve("/trac_test")` (or `.get_client`; now `trac_test_registry(<the row's own config>).resolve(...)`, keeping each row's config source -- `bootstrap_config()` or the `TRAC_*` environment -- so `write_gate`, timeouts and YAML credentials are inherited as before), 2 hand-built the URL from `TRAC_URL` (now `trac_test_config()`, the same four fields), 4 handed a registry to a server, CLI or handler stack (now `trac_test_registry`). `.env.example` and the README's live-test section document the variable
  - **Why**: the destructive-test instance is moving off `TRAC_URL`'s port to its own origin (rookery #69). The old port will answer 308 to the new one, and a redirect to another origin drops the Basic credentials, so every live row would have failed on the `TRAC_URL`-derived URL
  - **No-op until set**: with `TRAC_TEST_URL` unset the URL, the credentials and each row's Config (every field, via the default config's `dataclasses.replace`) are what they were; nothing sets it yet
  - **Tested**: `tests/test_live_helpers.py` (unset derives from `TRAC_URL` with its port kept and its project path dropped; set is used verbatim and loses a trailing slash; empty counts as unset; a path not ending in `/trac_test`, or a bare origin, raises; the registry resolves `/trac_test` to `trac_test_url()` with and without the variable, with the default credentials, and with an identity's credentials when one is passed). Written first: the `TRAC_TEST_URL` and registry tests failed on the unchanged helper. A live smoke of converted rows with the variable unset passed
  - **Review fixes**: (1) 13 rows had moved to `trac_test_client()`, which builds a bare Config from `os.environ` and so dropped the inherited `write_gate`, rpc timeout and YAML credentials of the old `InstanceRegistry._synthesize` path; they are back on their original config source through `trac_test_registry(config)`, and a test checks that a non-default field (`write_gate=False`) reaches the resolved Config and equals the old empty registry's. (2) `TRAC_TEST_URL=/trac_test` or `trac.example.org:8001/trac_test` passed the path check and was joined onto `TRAC_URL`'s host, the old origin; `trac_test_url()` now requires an `http`/`https` scheme and a host. (3) The `ValueError` echoed the raw value, so userinfo (`http://user:pw@host/trac_tst`) reached conftest's log; it names the variable and the defect, and only the path. (4) `InstanceRegistry._reload_if_changed` replaces the declared set when an instance file changes mtime, which dropped the `/trac_test` spec mid-run; `trac_test_registry` returns a subclass that re-pins it after each reload (`src/` untouched). Each has a test that failed before it
- **`wiki_file_pull` and `trac-convert` now produce Markdown from Trac's own rendered HTML, through `trac_html_to_markdown`, instead of the regex TracWiki converter (Trac /trac_mcp_server #210, phase 2 of #206).**
  - **`wiki_file_pull` (`format=markdown`)**: reads `get_wiki_page_info` first, then the source and the HTML (`get_wiki_page_html`) at that version, so an edit landing between the reads cannot pair one version's HTML with another's source. The translator gets the source for its code-block languages and no `link_map` yet (#213). Its warnings come back in the text block (`Conversion warnings:`, as push does) and in `structuredContent.warnings`, which is new and always a list (`[]` for `format=tracwiki`). A `not_found` fault from any of the three reads is the existing `not_found` error. `format=tracwiki` reads no HTML and is otherwise unchanged. The input schema is unchanged; the tool description now says Markdown is Trac's rendered HTML translated, a one-way export not meant to be pushed back. `trac-mcp wiki-pull` goes through this tool, so it changes with it.
  - **`trac-convert`, TracWiki -> Markdown**: `convert_text(..., unknown_macros=)` becomes `convert_text(..., render_html=None)`, a callable from TracWiki text to Trac's HTML; with none it raises the new `TracConnectionRequired`. `main` builds the renderer lazily, so `--from auto` still resolves first and Markdown -> TracWiki and `--to-wiki` stay offline. File, stdin and clipboard input render through `wiki.wikiToHtml`; `--from-wiki PAGE` uses `get_wiki_page_html(PAGE)`, so the page's own context resolves relative links, with the page source it already fetched supplying the languages. One `TracClient` serves both reads. `convert_text` reports `source_format="tracwiki"` as before, not the translator's `html`.
  - **Exit 4 when there is no Trac**: TracWiki -> Markdown with nothing configured (`bootstrap_config` raises) exits 4 (`EXIT_TRAC`), writes `trac-convert: TracWiki -> Markdown needs a Trac connection: ...` naming what to set, and writes nothing to stdout. A configured Trac whose render fails also exits 4, with the `_classify_trac_error` message (action `wiki render`). The `main` docstring's exit-code list and the parser description say so. An unreadable `--trac-password-file` is reported once. Empty input that detects as TracWiki is refused offline like any other (no short-circuit).
  - **`--unknown-macros` is deprecated**: the flag stays, defaults to `None`, and its help says "deprecated, no effect (Trac renders macros itself); removed in #214". Passing it prints one `warning: --unknown-macros is deprecated and has no effect` line on stderr unless `-q`. `tracwiki_to_markdown.py` and `converters.common.auto_convert` are untouched (#214 removes both); `auto_convert`'s TracWiki -> Markdown branch is unreachable, its only caller, `wiki_file_push`, passes `source_format="markdown"`. `build.sh` already lists `trac_mcp_server.converters.html_to_markdown`, `markdownify` and `bs4`.
  - **Tested**: handler tests against #208's `stock_TracCgi` fixture through a fake client (output equals `trac_html_to_markdown(html, source)`; a `{{{#!python` block comes out as a fenced `python` block; the HTML read is pinned to the info version with and without `version`; info is read first; warnings in both places; `tracwiki` never reads the HTML; `not_found` from each of the three reads). CLI tests: offline TracWiki -> Markdown from a file, stdin and `--from auto` exits 4 with the exact message and an empty stdout; Markdown -> TracWiki still runs with no Trac; a file input calls `wiki_to_html` with the file's text; `--from-wiki` calls `get_wiki_page_html` and opens one client; render failures exit 4; `--unknown-macros` warns once and changes nothing, and `-q` silences it; a real two-process pipe gets exit 4 through `sys.exit`. The existing pull tests gain the HTML read. Of the TracWiki -> Markdown CLI cases, the ones about the conversion inject a fake renderer, the ones about I/O or flags moved to Markdown -> TracWiki, and of the 18 offline round-trip cases the 8 that start from Markdown keep their md -> tracwiki half, asserted on the TracWiki, while the 10 that start from TracWiki are removed (that half is the translator's goldens and the live rows). The regex converter's own tests (`tests/test_converter*.py`) are untouched.
  - **Live rows (`/trac_test`)**: `test_pull_markdown_is_trac_rendered_html_translated` pulls a per-run scratch page (`Scratch/Ticket210PullLive<hex8>`, deleted in a `finally`, swept by `live_helpers.STALE_PAGE_PREFIXES`) holding a `{{{#!python` block and a nested list through the real handler and client; `test_live_stdin_tracwiki_to_md_renders_through_trac_test` runs `trac-convert --from tracwiki --to md` on stdin through the real `wikiToHtml`. `test_live_round_trip_check_from_edit_to` now runs through the translator.
  - **Seeded defect** (`Rules/testing/SeededDefectFirst`): with the handler calling the translator with `source=None`, `test_python_block_is_a_fenced_python_block` and `test_output_is_the_translator_output` failed (the fence lost its language); both pass again once restored.
  - **Review fixes**: (1) `trac-convert --from-wiki PAGE --to md` now pins like `wiki_file_pull`: it reads `get_wiki_page_info` first and fetches the source and the HTML at that version (it was two unpinned "latest" reads), falling back to unpinned reads with a `warning:` when the version is unusable; `--to tracwiki` / `--to-wiki` still read no HTML. (2) The offline refusal for a `--from auto` input detected as TracWiki (`echo hello | trac-convert --to md`) adds a line saying so and to pass `--from md`. (3) `TracConnectionRequired` carries the `bootstrap_config` `ValueError`'s text as a `  config error:` line, on the TracWiki -> Markdown refusal and on `--from-wiki`'s "no Trac credentials found"; a bad `TRAC_URL` no longer reads as "nothing configured" (exit stays 4). (4) `wiki_file_pull` (markdown) adds a warning when `get_wiki_page_info` gives no usable version, so the unpinned reads are announced. (5) The `--from-wiki` HTML read's fault is classified with the page name (`wiki page not found: PAGE`). (6) The live pull row no longer lets a failed cleanup replace its own assertion error. (7) `trac-mcp wiki-pull` prints each returned `warnings` entry as `trac-mcp: warning: ...` on stderr after the write. Each fix has a test that failed before it
- **`ticket_check_list` fetches its candidate tickets through batched `system.multicall` instead of one `ticket.get` RPC each (Trac /trac_mcp_server #158).** `_load` made one `client.get_ticket` call per id through `gather_limited`/`run_sync_limited`, which runs two at a time. Measured live on `/auto_pm` (296 tickets) that took 2.9 s, against 0.45 s for sequential `system.multicall` batches of 50 `ticket.get` calls
  - **The ticket's "fetch each id at most once per call" point already held**: `_handle_check_list` passes one `cache` dict to every `_load`, so a ticket found in several text chunks was fetched once. The change is therefore the multicall batching plus the shared `_multicall` memory below, not a new cache
  - **New `TracClient.get_tickets(ticket_ids)`** returns `{id: <ticket.get result>}` (the value `get_ticket` returns) for each id that loaded; an id that failed is absent. Ids are de-duplicated in order, and an empty list makes no RPC. While the server has `system.multicall` they go in batches of `TICKET_FETCH_BATCH` (50). A fault on one entry leaves that id absent, with no single retry; a reply that is not a list of the right length sends that batch to single gets. A "no such method" fault is remembered and the rest is read singly; any other fault or exception sends just that batch to single gets
  - **`_wiki_multicall` is now `_multicall`**: a missing `system.multicall` is a fact about the server, not the wiki, so wiki search and ticket fetch share one memory of it (still remembered for five minutes; the constant is now `MULTICALL_ABSENT_TTL_SECONDS`, renamed from `WIKI_MULTICALL_ABSENT_TTL_SECONDS` for the same reason)
  - **`_load` takes only the ids not yet cached, splits them into chunks of `TICKET_FETCH_BATCH` (50) and runs one `get_tickets` per chunk, 2-wide through the limiter** (`gather_limited` / `run_sync_limited`), so a slot is held per chunk rather than for the whole fetch and the single-get fallback keeps the old 2-wide parallelism. It caches `ticket[3]` for each entry that is a ticket (a list of at least four items whose fourth is a dict), else `None`. A chunk whose `get_tickets` raises is logged as a warning with its traceback and caches only its own ids as `None`, so they land in `failed_ids` / `unresolved` as before, the other chunks' tickets are still found, and nothing is retried within the call
  - **Tested**: the client against a fake XML-RPC endpoint (120 ids make 3 multicalls, a per-entry fault, a missing method remembered across calls, other faults and exceptions, a malformed reply, an empty list, duplicate ids); `_load` once per text chunk with a ticket in two chunks fetched in the first call only, 120 ids making three `get_tickets` calls of at most 50, and a raising `get_tickets` failing only its own chunk; one `@pytest.mark.live` read-only test on `/trac_test` comparing `get_tickets` with `get_ticket` and an absent id, which passed against the real server
- **`ticket_update` finds the comment it just posted with one small RPC instead of reading the whole changelog (Trac /trac_mcp_server #132).** Since #124/#131 every comment write read all of `ticket.changeLog` to find its own row, and matched it only to the whole second. The plugin now names the one change set at `ticket.update`'s microsecond `_ts`
  - **New plugin method `ticket.getChangeAt(id, ts)`** in `plugins/tracrpc_comment` (on `TicketCommentRPC`; the egg goes to **1.2.0**). `ts` is the `_ts` string of integer microseconds (XML-RPC ints are 32-bit). It returns `[cnum, author, comment]` for the change stored at exactly that microsecond (`cnum` as Trac stores it, a string, dotted for a threaded reply; `comment` the stored text, unmodified), or `[]` when there is no such change or it has no comment field. `TICKET_VIEW` is checked in the method; a non-digit or out-of-range `ts` is a `TracError`. 1.1.0 was never rolled out, so every instance goes from 1.0.0 straight to 1.2.0; the distinct version makes a stale egg fail visibly with `VersionConflict` rather than load silently. **The plugin must be rolled out to every instance at once (see `plugins/README.md`) before the fast path takes effect**
  - **`TracClient.get_ticket_change_at(ticket_id, ts)`** calls it, and returns `None` when the server has no such method, remembering that per client for five minutes (`TICKET_CHANGE_AT_ABSENT_TTL_SECONDS`) exactly as #129 does for `putPageIfVersion`. Any other fault propagates
  - **`_find_posted_comment` tries it first** and uses the answer only when it is certainly this write's: a 3-item list with a flat comment number, whose author is the configured Trac user (no author check under OIDC, where the client has no username) and whose stored text equals the text sent after CR/CRLF folding. tracrpc's `ticket.update` reads `_ts` back from the database *after* saving, so a write landing in between makes `_ts` name that other write's change set; the author and text checks keep it from being reported as ours. Anything else (plugin absent, `[]`, a dotted or malformed result, another author, different text, an exception, a timeout, no `_ts`) falls through to the unchanged changelog scan, which reports a genuine mismatch itself, so behaviour on an instance without the plugin does not change and the plugin path only ever reports a match. The response shape does not change. Plugin and scan share one `_COMMENT_LOOKUP_TIMEOUT` deadline, not one each
  - **Tested**: the plugin offline against real Trac (the right `[cnum, author, text]` for an update's microsecond, `[]` for a time with no change and for a change with no comment field, a dotted cnum for a `replyToComment` reply, `TICKET_VIEW` denied, a bad `ts`); the client with `_rpc_request` mocked (list returned, missing method returns `None` and is cached for the TTL, tried again after it, another fault raises); `ticket_update` with the plugin path never calling `get_ticket_changelog`, the scan used for another author's change, a different text (a real mismatch is still reported, through the scan) and each other fallback case, and the one shared deadline. Seeded: reading the changelog on the plugin path fails the skip test, treating `[]` as a final answer fails the fallback test, and dropping the author or the text check fails its test
- **`bare_ticket_ref` is no longer emitted; `render_check` lists the tickets a document links to once instead (Trac /trac_mcp_server #151).** The code fired at `warning` on every local ticket link (`'#N' resolves to: <summary>`), 8258 emitted lines over 30 days of session transcripts against 107 for the next code, so a correct ticket read `0 error(s), 12 warning(s)` and nobody read the warnings any more, real ones included
  - **Nothing a bare `#N` can get wrong goes unreported**: a ticket that does not exist is still `missing_local_target` at `error`, `PR #N` is still `pr_number_as_ticket`, and a bare twin of `<prefix>:#N` is still `bare_ref_shadows_prefixed`; all three still refuse a write. The one case left, a mistyped number that hits an existing ticket, is served by the next point
  - **`ticket_render_check` / `wiki_render_check` text output** gets one line after `Links:`: `Ticket refs (N): #57 defect: ... (closed: fixed); #3 ...`, each non-missing local ticket listed once per document, across all sections, in the order first seen, with Trac's own title for the link. `#18`, `ticket:18` and a `comment:M:ticket:18` link count as one ticket (keyed by the href), and a `comment:` link's `Comment M for #N:` title prefix is dropped. The line lists at most 50 tickets, then `... and K more` (the rest are in `structuredContent` `links.ticket`). The line is not a finding and does not count toward the error or warning totals; a missing ticket stays off it and stays under `Missing links`. `Ticket refs: none.` when there are none
  - **Write responses**: the gate's "Also reported, not blocking" list and a successful write's advisory lines stop carrying bare refs. The gate does not show a `Ticket refs` line: its anchors stay inside `_run_checks`, and exposing them would change `GateOutcome` for every write path to add a line nobody asked the write path for
  - **No schema change**: `structuredContent` is unchanged; `links.ticket` entries already carried `text` and `title`. `bare_ticket_ref` is removed from `ADVISORY_CODES`. Downstream consumers that bucket the code will find the bucket empty
  - **Recall gate** (live corpus, before/after, per-code counts): same corpus both times, 2088 documents from both stores (`scripts/store_sweep.py`, fetched 2026-11-04): `bare_ticket_ref` 4957 → 0; every other code identical — `target_check_disabled` 502, `link_ref_in_code_span` 345, `missing_local_target` 167, `unconfigured_intertrac_prefix` 100, `broken_inline_markup_in_render` 61, `pr_number_as_ticket` 27, `literal_markup_in_render` 11, `bare_ref_shadows_prefixed` 11, `intertrac_target_captured_punctuation` 4, `escaped_link_target` 2 — and 111 documents would be refused, unchanged
  - **Tested**: the old must-warn fixtures (`convert_preview` row 14, `render_check` row 4) are silent and keep their ticket anchor and title; a ticket cited twice across description and comments is listed once, in first-seen order, and a dead ticket beside it stays the one error; seeded controls on the write gate still refuse a dead `#99999` (`missing_local_target`), `PR #99999` and the `bcs:#99999` + `#99999` shadow. Removing the `Ticket refs` line, or its de-duplication, turns the new tests red
- **Over http, the file tools refuse server-side paths by default (Trac #111).** New setting `file_access: local | off` (`--file-access`, `TRAC_MCP_FILE_ACCESS`, `server.file_access`) defaults to `off` on the http transport and `local` on stdio. With `off`, a `file_path`/`output_path` is refused with `permission_denied` -- before any Trac call, and without touching the file -- naming the inline forms and the CLI. No caller confined those paths (`validate_output_path`'s `base_dir` was never passed), so any bearer-token holder could push any file the daemon's uid could read into a wiki page, or write fetched bytes anywhere it could write
  - **Breaking for an http caller that relied on server paths**; set `file_access: local` to restore it where every token holder may read and write the server process's files. stdio is unchanged
  - **The process-wide default is `off`, not `local`**, so a server wired up without `main()` fails closed. Tests that exercise the path forms opt in with an explicit `local_file_access` fixture, never an autouse one. A seeded defect that disabled the gate failed all seven refusal tests; one that dropped `main()`'s wiring failed the wiring test
  - **A reverse proxy's body limit now matters**: nginx's default `client_max_body_size` (1 MB) caps what an inline push or upload can send
- **`check_write` no longer guesses which tool re-checks a write (Trac #87).** Its render-failed note built the recovery advice from the field name — `content` meant wiki, anything else meant ticket — which was right for all five write paths that existed and wrong the moment a sixth arrived: a milestone description would have been told to re-check with `ticket_render_check`, a tool that cannot see a milestone. `recheck_with` is now a mandatory keyword on `check_write` and `gate_or_refuse`, so a new write path cannot inherit an answer nobody chose
  - **Milestones pass `None`**, and the note then says no render-check tool covers the field instead of naming one. There is no `milestone_render_check`; an author sent to the wrong tool is worse off than one told to read the page
  - This is the path whose entire purpose is to stop an unchecked write reading as a checked one, which is why a confidently wrong instruction on it is worth a signature change. Internal only — no tool schema or response changes for a caller whose write is checked normally
- **Breaking (advisory surface): `target_check_skipped` is replaced by three codes (Trac #83).** It merged outcomes that want opposite treatment from #64's blocking gate, so no severity was right for it — which is why #64 comment 2 asked for a ruling and comment 3 recorded it unanswered. `probe_targets` already distinguished the statuses; they were collapsed one function later, at reporting time
  - **`target_check_disabled`** (`info`) — the caller passed `check_targets=false`. Nobody asked for the check; that is the caller's deliberate choice, not a finding. **This is the path the ticket did not mention and the only one that fires on real content: all 188 of the corpus's findings are this one**
  - **`target_check_capped`** (`info`) — more probeable targets than the cap. The *document* is denser than the gate handles and the author can act (raise `target_cap`, split the document), so this is the candidate for #64's error column
  - **`target_check_failed`** (`info`, and stays advisory) — the probe could not reach the instance, including a ticket-realm target whose liveness control did not answer. The *checker* could not do its job; blocking here would stop every write while a remote instance is down and charge the author for an outage they did not cause. Carries the no-result-at-all case too
  - **The cap population is no longer empty, contrary to the ticket's section 3.** That was measured when the probe saw only the wiki realm; #82 widened it to the ticket realm, and the stock `TracChangeLog` help page goes from 15 unique probeable targets to **175**. Two documents now exceed the cap of 50, both that page. Worth carrying into #64: promoting `target_check_capped` to `error` would refuse writes to them, and the remedy it prints is not available to someone editing a stock help page they did not write
  - **The merged name is deleted, not aliased**, on all three paths — leaving it anywhere puts the ambiguity straight back where #64 has to rule on it. This renames a code in `convert_preview`, `ticket_render_check` and `wiki_render_check` output, and in the `target_cap` parameter description, so **a connected session needs a reconnect to see the new schema text**
  - Recall gate over the store corpus: 188 out of the old code, 188 into `target_check_disabled`, **every other code identical to the finding** and 86 documents refused, unchanged
  - The unreachable-instance row is tested against a **real** refused port and a real blackholed TEST-NET address rather than a mocked exception — but in the *offline* suite, not `ci-live.sh` as the ticket suggested: those sockets need no credentials and no Trac, and a `live` marker on a test that does not need the substrate is a test that stops running on GitHub Actions, which is precisely what #85 cost
- **The offline suite now runs without credentials, and is asserted to (Trac #81).** Without `--run-live`, `conftest.py` disables `.env` loading process-wide (`PYTHON_DOTENV_DISABLED`), and `ci.sh` additionally scrubs `TRAC_URL`/`TRAC_USERNAME`/`TRAC_PASSWORD`/`TRAC_INSECURE` from the environment for its pytest step
  - **This is the half a live script alone would not have caught.** In #85 `@pytest.mark.live` sat on a private helper instead of `TestConvertPreviewLive`, so four live tests ran *unconditionally* — they found `TRAC_URL` in `.env` on every developer machine and passed, while `master` sat red on GitHub Actions across two merges. `ci.sh` was green throughout and a `ci-live.sh` would have been green too; only an environment without credentials could see it
  - **Process-wide, because gating conftest's own `load_dotenv()` was measured and found insufficient.** `bootstrap_config()` calls `load_dotenv()` itself — correctly, since the server reads `.env` at startup — and that is the path #85's four tests take. With #85's defect seeded back in, `ci.sh` was still green under the conftest-only gate; the seeded run is what turned a plausible design into a working one, exactly what `Rules/testing/SeededDefectFirst` is for
  - Verified in both directions on the seed: `ci.sh` goes red on a misplaced `live` marker, while `pytest tests/ --run-live` — the run that originally hid it — still passes, so the gate discriminates rather than just failing

- **Breaking: the inline ticket and wiki tools no longer convert, in either direction (Trac #69).** The `format` parameter is gone from `ticket_create`, `ticket_update`, `ticket_batch_create`, `ticket_batch_update`, `wiki_create` and `wiki_update`; the `raw` parameter is gone from `ticket_get`, `ticket_changelog`, `wiki_get`, `wiki_search` and `milestone_get`; and `?format=` is gone from the `trac://wiki/` resource. All of them now store and return TracWiki bytes exactly as given, so a read-edit-write round trip is byte-exact
  - **Why removal rather than a flipped default.** #62 added `format` defaulting to `markdown`, which aimed the *destructive* failure at hand-authored TracWiki — indentation inside a `{{{ }}}` processor block was silently stripped, invisible in the render and in the (empty) warning list, visible only in the stored bytes — and the *safe* one at Markdown, which merely renders as literal `#` and `**`. Moving the default would have left an undeclared caller still getting *something* silently; removing the alternative leaves nothing to omit
  - **A stale caller is told, once, at the call site.** `format="markdown"` or `raw=false` returns a `validation_error` naming `trac-convert`, rather than being ignored. `format="tracwiki"` and `raw=true` are accepted as no-ops, so a caller already declaring TracWiki needs no change
  - **The converters are demoted, not deleted.** They still back the standalone `trac-convert` binary (#16), the `convert_preview` tool, and `wiki_file_push`/`wiki_file_pull` — the file tools keep converting deliberately, because they have a *filename* to go on where the inline tools have only content
  - **#47's pin moved rather than being dropped.** Its symptom — marker-poor Markdown misclassified by the content heuristic and stored unconverted — is re-anchored onto the converter and `auto_convert` tests, where the heuristic still runs. The misclassification is still asserted, not relaxed
  - The CRLF question left open by auto_pm:#90 is answered rather than inherited: the inline write handlers add no normalisation of their own, but a CR cannot reach the store through them regardless — `xmlrpc.client` emits a raw CR instead of escaping it as `&#13;`, and XML 1.0 line-end normalisation collapses it to LF on the parser side, before Trac sees the value. Measured end-to-end after deploy: a CRLF body stores with zero CRs. Storing bytes verbatim therefore does *not* create the CRLF-in-an-LF-store risk that was anticipated
- `ticket_get` now returns the ticket's comments by default (Trac #60): number, author, timestamp and body, so one call is the whole ticket. Previously comments were reachable only through `ticket_changelog`, with nothing in a `ticket_get` response indicating any existed — the compliant triage path cost two calls and the one-call path *looked* complete, which silently cost correctness (an operator's answer, already recorded in a comment, re-asked)
  - New `include_comments` (default `true`) — the documented opt-out for fetching `_ts` before a write; `max_comments` (default 50) caps long threads
  - Comments are filtered out of the changelog inside the tool, so field-only changes never reach the response: a ticket whose description was edited no longer drags two full copies of that description along to deliver a few bytes of comment text (the cost that ruled out proxying `ticket_changelog` wholesale)
  - Each comment carries the number Trac assigns it — the same number `ticket_render_check`'s `comment` parameter is keyed on, closing a discoverability gap where that number could only be found by guessing
  - Truncation is loud by construction: over the cap, the head and tail are kept and the middle dropped (a long thread has its scope set early and corrected late), and the response names how many exist, how many are shown, and which comment numbers were omitted — `comment_count` is always the exact total. A failed changelog fetch still returns the ticket, and says the comments are missing
- `main()` passing the resolved server config to the tool registry is now pinned by a test: with `read_only` or `disabled_tools` set, the registry passed to `run_http` leaves those tools out (Trac /trac_mcp_server #165). Seeded: with `main()` building the registry from a default `ServerConfig()`, the read-only and disabled-tools cases fail and the defaults case passes.

### Removed
- **The regex TracWiki -> Markdown converter and its round-trip machinery are deleted (Trac /trac_mcp_server #214).**
  - **What**: `converters/tracwiki_to_markdown.py` (4,326 lines: `TracWikiParser`, `tracwiki_to_markdown`, the list-stack, continuation and blockquote-indent passes) and its export from `converters/__init__.py`; the `tracwiki-unconverted` fence protocol (`FALLBACK_FENCE_INFO`) and the write leg's unwrap of it (`_is_fallback_fence`, `_is_processor_run`, the fallback case in `_item_runs`), so a fence with that info string is now refused by the write leg (`ValueError`, one line: `wiki_file_push` and `convert_preview` report it as a `validation_error`, `trac-convert` exits 3 with `trac-convert: conversion failed: ...` and `--to-wiki` writes nothing). Markdown pulled from Trac before #210 can still carry that fence, and without the unwrap it would have become `{{{#!tracwiki-unconverted`, a processor Trac does not have: an error box on the page with no warning from the converter or from Trac's write. A refusal is consistent with #212's push guard for Markdown exported from Trac, and the message says to edit the page as TracWiki (`wiki_get`, or `trac-mcp wiki-pull PAGE FILE --format tracwiki`). Any other fence language, `foo` included, is unchanged (`{{{#!foo`); and `trac-convert --unknown-macros` with its deprecation warning (argparse now rejects the flag, exit 2).
  - **Why**: TracWiki -> Markdown has been a one-way export through Trac's rendered HTML since #206/#210, and its front matter makes the push paths refuse it (#212). Nothing called the old converter, and the round trip it served (read, edit, write back) is the loop that guard exists to stop.
  - **Moved**: `_LIST_MARKER_RE` (with `_BULLET_MARKER`, `_NUMERIC_MARKER`, `_ALPHA_MARKER`) moved to `converters/common.py`; `_starts_a_block_in_trac` in the write leg still needs Trac's list-marker grammar. `tracwiki_to_markdown_lang` stays in `common.py` (`html_to_markdown` uses it). Kept: `[[...]]` macro stashing, CamelCase escaping and TracLink passthrough, which authored Markdown needs.
  - **Changed: `auto_convert` now refuses TracWiki -> Markdown**: a TracWiki source with a Markdown target raises `ValueError("TracWiki -> Markdown needs Trac's rendered HTML: use trac_html_to_markdown")` instead of converting. Its one caller, `wiki_file_push`, only converts Markdown -> TracWiki.
  - **Tests**: collected 6,284 -> 4,909 (the pytest-ruff plugin adds two items per file). Of 120 functions that read and wrote, 38 were rewritten to start from the Markdown a person could author, with the same expected TracWiki, and 82 were deleted: 37 only checked a TracWiki -> Markdown -> TracWiki round trip, 25 took the fence protocol as input or expectation, 20 started from Markdown only the old reader produced or repeated an existing write-leg test. 384 read-only functions were deleted, with 38 more that only exercised the deleted module's internals or served those tests (a list-stack unit test, a marker-grammar drift check, a helper for a live round trip). Added: `auto_convert` refuses TracWiki -> Markdown, `--unknown-macros` is a usage error, and `trac_mcp_server.converters.tracwiki_to_markdown` has no import spec (`test_source_format.py`). `tests/test_converter_retained_capability.py` and `tests/fixtures/store_corpus/` are deleted; the two footnote-rejection tests in that file moved to `test_converter.py`. Nine converter test files, left with no test, are deleted.
  - **Fixtures**: the `tests/fixtures/trac_html/` captures keep their text, by operator decision: some of their stored TracWiki and golden Markdown still mention the removed converter.
- **`TracClient.get_wiki_page_with_metadata`**, unused anywhere in this repository; call `get_wiki_page` and `get_wiki_page_info` instead (Trac /trac_mcp_server #189).

### Fixed
- **`plugins/README.md` no longer tells anyone to install `tracrpc_comment` the pre-rookery-69 way (Trac /trac_mcp_server #221).** rookery ticket 69 moved the plugin on the maintainers' Trac host from per-instance eggs into the Trac stack release, so the README's host-specific steps had gone stale, and the "install by commit into the Trac stack venv" ones would now modify a root-owned release behind the deploy's back.
  - **Install section**: one host-neutral procedure (`pip install` from a commit into Trac's venv, enable per instance, reload; `--no-build-isolation`, `--force-reinstall --no-deps`, confirm by `system.listMethods`), plus a short pointer for the maintainers' host: the plugin ships in the Trac stack release, pinned in rookery's lock file, moved by a new pin, `stage.sh trac` and `deploy.sh trac`. The procedure itself stays in rookery `services/trac-uwsgi/README.md`, not copied here.
  - **Removed**: the per-environment egg steps, the 1.0.0 to 1.2.0 upgrade notes and the 2026-09-17 roll-out list (still in the file's history). The `VersionConflict` lesson stays as one paragraph: enabling is per instance, the version is per process.
  - **Rollback and intro**: the rollback commands and the intro no longer name the host's paths, its pidfile or the retired workstation daemon, and neither does `plugins/tracrpc_comment/setup.py`'s docstring. The review restored four notes the first cut dropped (delete leftover eggs on migration, a same-version egg is skipped silently, `pip uninstall` hits every instance, the host needs `git` and a route) and corrected `deploy.sh trac --rollback` to the whole stack release.
- **`trac_html_to_markdown` no longer lets Markdown read the text of an inline pass-through, and no longer splits a list at an uncovered item (Trac /trac_mcp_server #218).**
  - **Why**: an element no rule covers is written as raw HTML. (A) CommonMark keeps parsing the text BETWEEN inline HTML tags as Markdown, so `<span style="color:red">b *c* `f`</span>` came out as `<span ...>b *c* `f`</span>` and rendered emphasis and a code span the page never had; the same went for `**`, `__`, `[l](u)`, and a line starting `# ` or `- ` inside the text. (B) `<ul><li>a</li><li style="color:red">b</li><li>c</li></ul>` became `- a`, a bare `<li>` HTML block, `- c`: the block ended the list, so it rendered as three lists
  - **What**: (A) markup passed through anywhere but as an HTML block (an inline element, or a block tag under a heading) has its TEXT NODES escaped with the converter's own rules (`\`, `*`, `_` outside a word, backtick, `[`, `]`, `~`, the backslash only where it would form an escape); attributes are never touched. Newlines in those text nodes become spaces first (see Review fixes), so no line-start escaping is needed. `<` and `&` are left alone, because serialisation already writes `&lt;`/`&amp;` and a backslash before an entity would show it literally. Text inside a raw `<code>` is escaped too: it is not a Markdown code span. An HTML block (table, `div`, ...) is unchanged. The escape rules moved from the converter's `escape()` into `_escape_text()`, which `escape()` now calls. (B) a `ul` or `ol` with a direct-child `li` that is not covered passes through WHOLE as an HTML block, as an uncoverable table does, with the same warning (`li[style] is not covered; passed through as HTML`), once. A nested list holding such an item passes through on its own, indented into its item; the outer list stays Markdown and stays one list
  - **Tested**: in `tests/test_converter_html_to_markdown.py`, with `MarkdownIt("commonmark").enable("table")` as the oracle: rendering must make no `em`, `strong`, `a`, `s`/`del`, `h1`, `ul` or `code` the input lacked, and the visible text must equal the input's, for the span reproduction and nine variants (`**x**`, `__y__`, `[l](u)`, `~~s~~`, backslashes, `<code>*k*</code>`, `&` and `<` in the text, intraword `_`), a span in a table cell and in a heading, and `#`, `-`, `>`, `1.`, `---` after a newline inside a span; a list with one styled item renders as ONE `ul`/`ol` of 3 `li` (one carrying the style) with one warning, and the nested case keeps the outer list one list. Pinned unchanged: a covered list, an HTML block, a pass-through table and a span's attributes. The existing row `<ul><li style=...>` now expects the list as a whole, not the bare `<li>`
  - **Seeded**: written first and seen failing on the unchanged converter: 19 of the new rows (the `~~s~~` and `&amp;` span variants, the `>` line start and the pinned rows already passed)
  - **Golden files**: none of the #208 fixtures' `*.golden.md` changed (regenerated with `UPDATE_GOLDEN=1` after the fix; `git diff -- tests/fixtures/` is empty)
  - **Review fixes**: (1) in an inline pass-through the newlines of text nodes (`\r\n`, `\r`, `\n`; not inside `pre`, `textarea`, `listing`, `xmp`, `plaintext`) become single spaces before escaping, so the span stays on its opening tag's line, never forms a type-7 HTML block and no line inside it opens a heading, list or HTML block (backslashes showed literally there, and indented `# h` / `- l` lines escaped nothing); the line-start escaping of the first version is gone. (2) Every CommonMark HTML-block start name (types 1 and 6: `details`, `summary`, `iframe`, `figure`, `nav`, `script`, `style`, ...) is a block in a pass-through, wrapped in blank lines and never escaped, as `div` is. (3) An uncovered `ul`/`ol` warns for the list AND for its uncovered items, once each; only a covered list with an uncovered item takes the whole-list branch. (4) A nested list passed through inside a covered `li` is placed like a covered nested list (one newline, indented under the item), so the outer list stays tight. (5) A `dl` with an uncovered direct-child `dt`/`dd` passes through whole, as a block. `git diff -- tests/fixtures/` is still empty
- **A comment made as a reply to the ticket description is comment N, not "no number" (Trac /trac_mcp_server #207).**
  - **Why**: Trac's web UI stores a reply to the DESCRIPTION with changelog `oldvalue` `"description.N"` (seen live: `'description.2'` on /trac_test ticket 43). `TracClient.parse_comment_number` took only `"N"` and `"M.N"`, so it returned None: `ticket_get` listed the comment with `"number": null`, `ticket_update reply_to=2` answered `not_found`, and a description write containing `comment:2` was refused as `dangling_comment_ref` once a later comment existed (`known_comment_numbers` knew only parsed numbers plus max+1, which is also why a write made while the description reply was the newest comment slipped through on #206).
  - **What**: `parse_comment_number("description.5")` returns `CommentNumber(5, "description")`; `CommentNumber.reply_to` is now `int | Literal["description"] | None`. Only the lowercase token `description` is accepted as the first of two parts, the number follows the existing ASCII-digit rules; `"Description.5"`, `"description."`, `"description.0"`, `"description.5.1"`, `"foo.5"` and `"description"` alone stay None. Every caller reads through the parser, so `ticket_get` (`number` "5", `in_reply_to` "description", heading `(reply to the description)`), `reply_to` quoting, the changelog "comment N has no text" label, the write gate's `known_comment_numbers`, render-check pairing and the CLI needed no change. `_comment_cnum` and the `get_change_at` read-back still treat any non-None `reply_to` as not this tool's own flat write.
  - **Tested**: `parse_comment_number` accept/reject rows in `tests/test_client.py`; offline handler rows for `ticket_get` (number, `in_reply_to`, heading), `ticket_update reply_to=2` quoting a `description.2` row, `_comment_cnum` staying None, and `known_comment_numbers` knowing 2 for rows `"1"`, `"description.2"`, `"3"`. One live row, `TestTicketCommentWriteGateLive::test_a_reply_to_the_description_is_comment_two`, makes a REAL description reply by posting Trac's own web form (`tests/live_helpers.py` `post_description_reply`: XML-RPC cannot, `ticket.update` has no `replyto`) and asserts the stored `description.` oldvalue, `ticket_get` number "2" / `in_reply_to` "description", a `comment:2` description write passing the gate and `reply_to=2` succeeding.
  - **Seeded**: written first and seen failing on the unfixed parser: the three `description.N` accept rows in `test_parse_comment_number_accepts`, `test_update_reply_to_quotes_a_reply_to_the_description`, `test_get_description_reply_reports_its_own_number`, `test_description_reply_row_is_known_under_its_own_number`, and the live row (`number` None instead of "2").
  - **Review fixes**: the web-form helper reads the form with lxml (a regex also matched `data-value=` and cut values at the other quote), its failure messages strip userinfo from the URL, and its docstring says the `/trac_test` check is path-only.
- **An expired OIDC access token gets a 401 from the MCP endpoint (Trac /trac_mcp_server #202, GitHub PR 134).** Under `TRAC_MCP_OIDC_RPC_URL`, `OidcTokenRequiredMiddleware` reads the `exp` claim of a JWT bearer token (without checking the signature, which stays Trac's job) and answers `401` with `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer error="invalid_token"` once it has passed or is within 30 s. Before, the expired token was forwarded, Trac's `401` came back as text inside a tool result with HTTP 200, and an MCP client such as LibreChat, which refreshes its token only on a `401` from the MCP endpoint, never did. Tokens that are not JWTs or carry no numeric `exp` pass through as before
  - **Seeded**: with the expiry check disabled, `TestOidcExpiredToken::test_expired_jwt_rejected_with_invalid_token` fails (200 instead of 401); with `RecursionError` removed from the `except`, `test_deeply_nested_payload_passes_through` fails
  - **Review fixes**: the payload segment must be entirely base64url (padding `=` at the end only) before it is decoded, because `urlsafe_b64decode` silently drops other characters and decoded `x.eyJ~!*leHAiOjF9.y` to `{"exp":1}` and a 401; such a token now passes through as not a JWT. The 401 is logged at INFO with the seconds past `exp`, never the token or another claim. The 30 s early-refresh boundary is pinned by tests (`exp = now + 30` is expired, `now + 31` is not). Seeded: with the base64url check removed, `test_characters_outside_base64url_are_not_a_jwt` fails; with the log call removed, `test_rejection_is_logged_without_the_token` fails
- **A `{{{#!comment}}}` or `{{{#!table}}}` block in a list item stays in the item, and the lines after it continue it, on both legs (Trac /trac_mcp_server #192).** `_fallback_processor_blocks` stashed the block from its `{{{`, so the opener's indentation stayed outside the stash: the opening fence landed at the item's indent, the placeholder and closing fence at column 0. The list passes read the column-0 placeholder as the end of the list and put a blank line after the opening fence (#144's F3), which ends the item in CommonMark, and the column-0 closer then OPENED a fence that ran to the end of the document: ` * a` / `   {{{#!comment` / `   x` / `   }}}` / ` * d` came back with ` * d` in a literal `{{{ }}}` block, and so did every later item. A column-0 block ended the item as well, so `- item` / `{{{#!comment` ... `}}}` / `  continuation text` / `  - sub` came back with `continuation text` a top-level paragraph and `sub` a new top-level list
  - **What Trac renders** (measured on /trac_test, 2026-11-08, `convert_preview`, ticket comment 3): M1 a `{{{#!comment}}}` in an item, indented, at column 0 or with a body line at column 1, keeps the item open (the lines after it continue it, an indented marker nests under it); M2 a `{{{#!table}}}` with a nested `{{{#!td}}}`, indented or at column 0, renders inside the `<li>` and `more` continues the item; M3 a `{{{#!td}}}` / `{{{#!th}}}` closes the list (the table follows, an indented line after it is a blockquote, the next marker opens a new list); M4 a comment then a td in one item ends the list at the td
  - **Read leg** (`_fallback_processor_blocks`, where the opener is the first non-whitespace on its line): a run of `comment`/`table` blocks with a list item open is fenced at the item's text column O, exactly as #144 fences a plain block. The core of `_fence_in_list_item` moved into `_fence_at_item_column`, which both paths call (same O, same declines: a lettered marker #74 and a TAB-indented marker line; same rule of stripping the opener's indentation only if every non-blank line after the opener starts with it; placeholder line at O; the stash recorded in `_item_fence_stashes` so `_shift_nested_markers` moves it with the item, #179).
