The plain-block path keeps its bytes. Everything else takes the opener's indentation INTO the stash (a run holding a td/th, a block with no open item, a declined marker), so the fence, placeholder and closer all sit at column 0 and the Markdown ends the list there, as Trac does (M3); an indented opener with no list above it is balanced now too, where its fence was just as unbalanced. A block not first on its line, and a column-0 block with no open item, keep master's bytes
  - **The open-item lookback** reads a view of the text so far, not `_lines_since_blank(emitted)`: this pass runs before `_convert_code_blocks`, so an earlier plain block in the same item is still raw there, and its column-0 body line would pop the item. In the view a first-on-line block (a plain block, or a comment/table stash) is left out, with its indentation and closing line, because a block is transparent to the item stack (#144's F4-F6), and a td/th run stands for one column-0 line (M3). A column-0 marker (`- item`, `1. item`) is an item in this lookback and for the lines after the stash (`_list_stack_step(col0=True)`, `_items_open_above(col0=True)`): the ticket's first shape is a column-0 item, which #144's lookback has never seen
  - **The later passes read the stash as #144's block** (`_convert_indented_blocks`): a processor block fenced in an item has all three lines at O, so they are masked and counted as a block's lines (`_item_stash_triples`, also in `_stash_lines` and `_lines_inside_blocks`), and the run after the stash goes through `_continues_item_after_block`, `_starts_inside_open_item` and `_split_past_bound` as the run after a plain block does (a line past the bound still ends the list, #175). `_is_digits_ordered` also accepts a column-0 `1.` marker. A column-0 line straight after the stash, or after the continuation run, that ends a column-0 list gets one blank line, so CommonMark does not take it into the item (`_stash_ends_col0_list`; the separator pass that does this for indented items cannot see column-0 markers)
  - **Write leg** (`_item_runs`): a fallback fence whose body is exactly one coalesced run of `{{{#!comment}}}` / `{{{#!table}}}` blocks (`_is_processor_run`, with the read leg's own regex and brace matcher) is a `code` run instead of `text`, so it goes under the item at the continuation indent with no blank line before it, and a paragraph after it is adjacent (#143). The blank line that set it off ended the list in Trac even when the Markdown kept the fence in the item. Every other fallback fence (a td/th run, a spanned table, a lettered list, an indented block, a comment with text after its closer) stays in the text run, byte for byte. `find_list_item_splits` shares `_item_runs`, so it follows
  - **Round trip**: byte-identical wherever the source block sat at the item's continuation indent. A block at column 0 under `- item` comes back at the continuation indent (`   {{{#!comment`), which renders the same (M1), the trade #144 made for a plain block. A comment whose body has a line shallower than the opener keeps its indentation unstripped (F2), so its closer gains O columns on the first pass; the comment is hidden and the form is stable after it. The td shape (` * a` / `   {{{#!td` ... `   }}}` / `   more` / ` * b`) keeps the list ending at the td, and the later item stays an item; `more` comes back as a paragraph, where Trac has a blockquote, which is what a top-level `{{{#!td}}}` followed by `   more` already gave
  - **Tests** (`tests/test_converter_processor_block_in_item.py`): the ticket's two shapes and comment 1's two shapes, M2's table forms (indented and column 0), M1's comment run, a column-0 comment under a `1.` item and under a `10.` item, a body line at column 1, an earlier plain block with a column-0 body line (the lookback view), a nested item moved by #179's shift, a continuation with no nested item and prose at column 0 after the block (column-0 item), the td/th, no-item, lettered, TAB and not-first-on-line forms (each with the read-leg Markdown, the mistune structure, the round trip and its stability), and the write leg on its own, with the rows that keep the text run. One `@pytest.mark.live` test per shape renders source and round trip on /trac_test and compares the HTML (read-only, `wiki.wikiToHtml`), plus one for the td shape. Seeded: with the lookback reading the raw text (a plain block left in), the earlier-plain-block rows failed; with the masking of the stash lines removed, 16 rows failed
  - **Rows of earlier tickets that pinned master's bytes for these shapes are updated in place, each with a comment**: #182's three rows in `test_converter_quote_before_list.py` (a stashed processor block in a column-0 item, in an indented item, a stashed td in an indented item) and #190's `processor-block-stash-under-a-bullet` and `-a-lettered-item` rows in `test_converter_column_zero_continuation.py`, with the bullet half of `test_processor_block_stash_is_not_swallowed` (the fence is now legitimately the item's own, right after its text; the lettered half keeps its assertion)
  - **Review fixes**: (F1) a one-line `{{{x}}}` first on its line was dropped from the lookback as if it were a block, so a table after a column-0 `{{{x}}}` line was fenced inside an item that Trac had ended, read back as indented code and then as an inline span; only a real multi-line block (opener alone on its line, closer alone on its line) is transparent now, and master's bytes return for that shape. (F2) a block with text after its closer (`}}} tail`) is not fenced in the item; the column-0 stash takes the rest of that line, so its closing fence is alone on its line. (F3) an indented `||` row ends the list in the lookback (Trac puts the table outside the list, measured), so a block after it takes the column-0 path. (F4) any lettered or roman item in the open stack, a column-0 one included, declines the in-item fence, because its list is a verbatim fallback of its own. (F5) a run of comment/table blocks fenced in an item stops at the first blank line between its blocks, where Trac ends the list; the blocks after it decide for themselves. Round trip: the write leg still puts a blank line between a top-level block and the list after it, so ` * b` after the second table of F5's shape comes back after a blank line (same render). Also: a document with no processor block skips the lookback entirely
  - **Known cost**: the lookback walks the lines since the last blank line for each block, as #144's does for a plain block, so a list of N items with a block each and no blank line between them is quadratic (1,500 such items: 6 s here, against 14.7 s for the same list with plain blocks on master)
  - **Unchanged**: the plain-block path, a `{{{#!td}}}` / `{{{#!th}}}` run's stash bytes at column 0 with no list above, a block not first on its line, and the plain-text continuation rules (#149, #175, #176, #178)
- **An indented line under a column-0 list item continues the item, up to Trac's bound, on the read leg (Trac /trac_mcp_server #190).** `- a` / `  q` read `q` as a blockquote after the list, and `- a` / ` q` as a verbatim fence after it, where Trac has `q` in item `a`. The round trip then moved `q` out of the item. A lettered column-0 item (`a. a` / `  q`) was fenced alone and `q` became a quote after the fence
  - **What Trac renders** (measured on /trac_test, 2026-11-08, `convert_preview`; read off Trac 1.6's `_indent_formatter`): a column-0 marker is a list marker at column 0, so an indented line continues the item up to column 2 under a bullet and column 3 under a digits item (`trac_continuation_bound(0, ordered=...)`), and a line past that ends the list and is a blockquote (#175). The shapes are in the table on the ticket (comment 1): B1 to B6 for a bullet, O1 and O2 for a number
  - **The fix** (read leg only, in `_convert_indented_blocks`, after the brace, definition-list and lettered steps and before #182's cut): a run that starts on the line straight after a column-0 bullet or digits marker line (checked on the masked text) and holds no list marker is the item's continuation. Its lines up to the bound go out as written and a list stays open for what follows, which CommonMark keeps in the item (a lazy continuation left of the text column). The rest of the run, from the first line past the bound, goes through the existing quote and fence steps as its own run, so `- a` / `  q` / `   r` reads `q` in `a` and `r` in a fence after the list
  - **Lettered and roman column-0 items** (`_fallback_column_zero_lists`, #89; the operator's decision, ticket comment 2): the lines after the marker line are walked as Trac walks a list (`_lettered_item_extent`, with `_continued_item` and its bound): an indented marker line opens a nested item, any other indented line must continue one of the open items, and the lines join the verbatim fence of the list, as does a following column-0 marker line. A blank line, a column-0 non-marker line, a line past the bound, a tab, a table row or heading, a line holding `{{{` or `}}}` and a line of an earlier stash (a processor block's fence) stop it. `a. a` / `  q` round-trips byte for byte, and so does `a. a` / `  - b` / `    - c` / `    more`, which a fixed column-3 test split across two fences
  - **Declined, with master's bytes** (`_column_zero_continuation`): a tab in the indent of ANY line of the run (Trac counts a tab as one column, so a tab line may be inside the bound where the split would fence it outside; checked before the walk), a line of an earlier stash (the indented opening fence of a processor block), a head line that is Markdown-only syntax, which CommonMark would turn into a heading, quote, fence, rule, HTML block or nested list where Trac reads text (`#`, `>`, a fence, `---` and any other thematic break, `+ x`, `1) x`, `<div>`), a head line that is a Trac table row or heading (Trac ends the list at a table row), and a `>` citation (Trac puts it in the item; master reads `> > q` outside the list; a separate ticket owns it). A run holding a list marker anywhere keeps master's path (` - a` / `  q` / `   r` / `      - x`), as does a run holding a `{{{` block, as `TracPlugins` has under an item
  - **Round trip**: `- a` / `  q` comes back as ` * a` / `   q` (the write leg's own form, marker at column 1 and text at column 3): not byte-exact, the same render. Before, `q` left the list
  - **Tests** (`tests/test_converter_column_zero_continuation.py`): one row per shape (B1, B2, B4, B5, B6, O1, a bullet `*`, a number with `q` at column 2, a past-the-bound line after a continuation, `a. a` / `  q`, the same with a following `b. b`) and rows that keep master's bytes (B3, O2, a tab, a space then a tab, a `>` line, a `>` after a continuation line), each with the read-leg Markdown, the mistune structure (which `<li>` holds the text) and the round trip. Seeded: with the step off, 28 tests failed (the new file and the #182 row); with no bound or the bullet bound for a digits item, 9 and 3; with the lettered continuation off, 7 (first cut; the `>` decline is now one of F4's rules and the in-loop tab guard, unreachable, is gone: see Review fixes)
  - **Review fixes**: (F1) the head lines had no blank line after them, so a column-0 line after the whole run (`1. a` / `   q` / `P`, `- a` / `  q` / `|| x ||`) became a CommonMark lazy continuation of the item where Trac ends the list at it; one blank line now follows the head unless the next line is a column-0 marker line (or the stash lookback), a code-block delimiter or a stash line, because Trac keeps a column-0 `{{{` block in the item (T3, pinned at what the code gives). (F2) the lettered continuation is Trac's walk instead of a fixed column 3. (F3) the bullet path declines, and the lettered walk stops, at the indented opening fence of a stashed processor block, which the first cut glued to the item text or leaked as a literal fence line. (F4) the bullet path declines on a head line of Markdown-only syntax. (F7) the tab decline covers the whole run. Seeded, each on its own: with F1's blank line off, 6 tests failed; with the fixed column 3 back, 9; with the lettered stash stop off, 4; with the Markdown-syntax decline off, 30; with its table/heading part off, 3; with the whole-run tab check off, 9. The bullet path's stash decline alone shows no failure, because F4's fence rule declines the same line; with both off, 34
  - **Rows of earlier tickets that pinned master's bytes are updated in place, each with a comment**: #182's ` * x` / `- a` / `  q` (now `q` stays in `a`, round trip ` * x` / ` * a` / `   q`), and the column-0 lettered row of `test_converter_quote_before_list.py` (`P` / `a. a` / `  q` / `  - b` is now one verbatim fence, byte-exact on the way back, where master fenced `a. a` alone and read `q` as a quote)
  - **Unchanged**: the write leg, a `{{{ }}}` or processor block under a column-0 item, a line past the bound, and the plain-text continuation rules (#149, #175, #176, #178)
- **A citation line goes to the list item Trac puts it in, at that item's text column, on the read leg (Trac /trac_mcp_server #187).** `_shift_nested_markers` gave a `>` line the owner a plain text line gets (`_continued_item`) and moved it by that item's shift, so ` * a` / `  * b` / `  > q` read `q` in `a` (CommonMark does not let a quote continue `b`'s paragraph, and ends `b` at the dedent) where Trac has it in `b`, and ` * a` / ` > q` left the list altogether. The round trip then moved `q` out of `b` or out of the list
  - **What Trac renders** (measured on /trac_test, 2026-11-08, `convert_preview`; read off Trac 1.6's `Formatter.handle_quote_block`): any line whose stripped text starts with `>` is a citation line. The FIRST line of a run closes every list whose marker column is greater than its `>` column, and the run is one blockquote inside the deepest item left open. Two differences from plain text: no upper bound (#175), so a `>` past the item's text column stays in it, and a `>` AT the outermost marker column stays in that item, where plain text ends the list. A `>` at column 0 closes every list. Fourteen shapes are in the table on the ticket (comment 1)
  - **The fix** (read leg only, in `_shift_nested_markers`): a citation line with an item open (no tab in its indent, not inside a processor block walked as a unit) takes the deepest open item whose source marker column is at or left of its `>` column, cuts the stack back to it, and is written at that item's output text column with its indent stripped, where a `>` opens a quote in that item (right after a deeper item CommonMark closes that item at the dedent). The later `>` lines of the run keep that item and column. #145's separator then puts the blank line and the re-indent after the quote, so ` * a` / `  * b` / `  > q` / `  more` / ` * c` reads `more` in `b`. The single-level shapes (` * a` / `  > q`, ` * a` / ` > q`, ` 1. a` / ` > q`) are the same bug and are fixed too (the operator's decision, ticket comment 2)
  - **Declined, with master's bytes**: a `>` at column 0 or left of the root marker, an owner (or an item the cut would close) that is frozen or has a tab gap (tab-indented lists, a root at column 4 or more), a tab in the quote's indent, a `>` after a blank line (no list is open: #181/#183). Lettered and roman lists never reach this pass (#89)
  - **Round trip**: Q2, Q3, QO, Q3L, S1, S2, SO and the `more` shape come back with `q` in the right item, at the Markdown text column (` * a` / `  * b` / `  > q` gives ` * a` / `   * b` / `     > q`: not byte-exact, the same render). A quote in an item AFTER its nested list (` * a` / `  * b` / ` > q`) reads correctly now, but the write leg still writes it after a blank line, so the round trip ends the list there, as before (#145's known limit; the write leg is filed as #197)
  - **Bytes that moved without changing the structure**: a `>` past its item's text column (` * a` / `  * b` / `     > q`, ` * a` / `    > q`) was already in the right item and now reads at the text column, one column left of master's bytes. The round trip is identical
  - **The earlier passes agree** (review): the passes that walk raw lines before `_shift_nested_markers` (the in-item fence's `_open_list_item`, `_split_past_bound`, and everything on `_list_stack_step(upper_bound=True)`) closed the list at a `>` at the root marker's column, which the Markdown now keeps open. They use Trac's quote rule now (`quote_rule`: close only the items at a column strictly greater than the `>`, no upper bound), so a fence after the quote stays in the item (` * a` / ` > q` / `   {{{`), text past the bound after it is outside the list, the line after a nested quote stays in its item, and a marker after a blank line is #183's fence. A tab-indented line inside a run of `>` lines continues the run (only the first line's tab declines it), and a line whose leading whitespace holds a no-break space is not a citation here. Known gap, not fixed: a quote after a fenced code block in a nested item (` * a` / `  * b` / `    {{{` ... `}}}` / `  > q`) still ends up at column 0, as on master (pinned)
  - **Tests** (`tests/test_converter_quote_owner.py`, 82 rows): one row per shape (Q1 to Q5, Qm, QO, Q3L, Qc, S1, S2, S4, SO, the `more` shape, a three-line run) with the read-leg Markdown, the mistune structure (which `<li>` holds the quote) and the round trip, and rows that keep master's bytes (fences under a nested item at two columns, a `>` at column 0 after a list, left of the root marker, a tab-indented list, a tab in the quote's indent, a frozen root and a frozen nested one, a tab-gap root and nested one, a `>` after a blank line). Seeded defects, each on its own: the owner from `_continued_item` instead of the `>` column (3 rows fail, all of the shape with a `>` at the root marker's column over a deeper open item: the two rules agree everywhere else), the write by the item's shift instead of its text column (38), no run continuation (3)
  - **Rows of earlier tickets that pinned master's bytes for these shapes are updated in place, each with a comment**: #179's known-gap row (now `q` in `b`), #177 (a quote at column 1 and at column 2 after an item, then `c`: the quote is the item's, so `c` is switched to a new list), #175/#176 (the `interrupter` rows in `test_converter_between_column_continuation.py` and `test_converter_list_marker_column.py`)
  - **Unchanged**: the write leg, the plain-text continuation rules (#149, #175, #176, #178), #180's blank line before a nested ordered marker after a quote, a `>` after a blank line
- **A list ends at every blank line, so an indented line after one is a quote and a marker after one opens a new list, on the read leg (Trac /trac_mcp_server #183).** `_convert_indented_blocks` left a run that starts after a blank line alone whenever a list could be open above it, so CommonMark kept it in the open item: ` * a` / blank / `   c` / ` * d` moved `c` into the list on the round trip, an indented line lost its indent, and ` * a` / blank / `   * c` nested `c` in `a` and dropped the blank line
  - **What Trac renders** (measured on /trac_test, 2026-11-08, `convert_preview`): Trac ends a list at EVERY blank line, including one after a `{{{ }}}` block inside an item. After it an indented non-marker line is a blockquote at any column (1 to 4 measured) and an indented marker line opens a NEW top-level `<ul>`/`<ol>` at any column, keeping its own nesting below it. Twelve shapes are in the table on the ticket (comment 2)
  - **Paragraph after the blank line** (read leg): #182's head cut is also made when the line before the run is a blank line (a line of spaces, tabs or `\r`; a `[[BR]]` line is text at this pass and never counts), whatever the open-list flag says. The head goes through the same step 5 alone (canonical indent: `> ` lines, anything else the `tracwiki-unconverted` fence), then one blank line, then the marker starts the next run. `  q` after the blank gives `> q`. The added blank line between the quote and the next list is accepted (the operator's decision, as on #182); the render is identical
  - **Marker after the blank line** (read leg): a run that starts after one or more blank lines (the items open above are found past every consecutive blank line, one upward walk) with a list open above them, whose first line is a marker at or past the OUTERMOST open item's text column, is carried verbatim through the fallback, up to the first marker line left of that column or the end of the run. A marker indented 4 or more columns, or by a tab, is fenced whatever the item, even left of its text column (` 10. a` / blank / `    * c` / ` 11. d`: Trac gives `<ol start=10>`, `<ul>`, `<ol start=11>`; CommonMark reads the marker as code or nested), and so is one after a lettered item (` a. a` / blank / `     * c`, which was `     - c`, indented code, then a `{{{` block on the round trip). That fence runs to the first later plain marker left of the text column (the first later plain marker when the item is lettered or tab-indented, whose column is unknown). This keeps the stored indent and Trac's two lists; dedenting the run was rejected because it rewrites the indent and merges two Trac lists into one. The fence adds no blank line of its own, but the write leg puts a blank line between a code block and a list that follows it, so the round trip is NOT byte-exact: it gains that one blank line (` * a` / blank / `   * c` / ` * d` comes back with a blank line before ` * d`), and is byte-exact only when nothing follows the fence. Not changed: the write leg is out of scope
  - **A head that is not a quote** (read leg): when the cut head is a heading or a table row, it is carried unchanged, and one blank line follows it only when the next marker is an ordered one that does not start at 1 (` 1. a` / blank / `  = h =` / ` 2. d` read `  = h =` then `2. d` as its paragraph text; Trac: `<ol>`, `<h1>`, `<ol start=2>`). A bullet or `1.` interrupts a paragraph, so #182's bytes for those stay
  - **Declined, with master's bytes**: a marker left of the text column (#177's case), a marker the list's own #179 shift already moves, a plain marker (not indented 4+ or by a tab) after a lettered or tab-indented outer item (its column is unknown, and no Markdown list is open there to nest it in), a head cut before a marker indented 4 or more columns or by a tab (the added blank line would make it indented code), a blank-looking line made of any other whitespace character (not measured), and a marker with no list open above it
  - **Tests** (`tests/test_converter_list_ends_at_blank.py`, 115 rows): C2 to C9, C11 and C12 and the review's shapes (several blank lines, 4+ and tab markers, a lettered outer item, a heading head) with the read-leg Markdown, the mistune render and the round trip, rows that must not move (C1, the no-marker shapes, `P` / blank / `   * c`, the declines above, the write leg's `[[BR]][[BR]]` loose items and Markdown-authored loose, nested and ordered items). Seeded defects, each disabled on its own: the paragraph cut after a blank (24 rows fail), the whole marker rule (32), the text-column test (3), the stop at a left marker (27), the #179 deferral (1), the lettered-outer decline (1), the tab decline (1), the measured-blank-character set (1), a whitespace-only line as a blank (3). The review's conditions, each disabled on its own (failures counted over the four converter test modules this change touches): the lookback past several blank lines (9), the 4+/tab rule (17), the unknown-column decline (1), the stop at a plain marker (4), the unknown column never stopping the fence (3), the blank line after a non-quote head (8)
  - **Rows of earlier tickets that pinned master's bytes for these shapes are updated in place, each with a comment**: #144/#149 (a line after a blank, pre-149 reading), #143 (text after a blank following a block), #176 (`blank since marker`, F6), #177 (P4; the column-4 and tab-indented marker after a blank, now fenced), #178 (the heading rows of `test_converter_ordered_continuation_bound.py`: the second write now gains one blank line before the next item numbered 10 or more, which keeps it a list item; raw whitespace-only, tab-only and CRLF blank lines; the `hr_only` stability row, which the write leg writes as a blank line and an indented `----`, a quote to Trac, moved to its own test), #179 (an unshifted list), #182 (quote runs after a blank line)
  - **Corpus sweep** (offline, the cached corpus of both stores, 2,075 documents, master f5c4b88 against this branch, re-run after the review fixes): no document's read-leg output changes, so the stored pages are untouched by this change. The instrument was checked on two seeded documents (C2 and C3), which it reports as changed
  - **Unchanged**: #181's quote depth, #52/#53's loose lists (a Markdown loose item is joined with `[[BR]][[BR]]` and never reaches this pass), the write leg, and an indented `{{{#!comment}}}` block inside an item, which is garbled on the round trip with or without a blank line (#192)
- **The remaining raw-text fault classifiers go through `classify_fault` or the missing-method check, and `classify_fault` and `unwrap_tracrpc_fault` move to `core/faults.py` (Trac /trac_mcp_server #191).** Nine callers still decided what a fault meant from the raw fault text, where a page name or a ticket id the fault merely mentions can contain the token: `wiki_file_push`'s existence probe, `wiki_file_pull`, `put_wiki_page`'s version pre-check, the permission arms of `ticket_delete` and `ticket_attachment_delete`, the missing-method arms of `ticket_actions` and `get_recent_wiki_changes`, and the missing-method helpers of the `ticket_comment_*` tools and `timeline_search`. #188 and #189 did the others
  - **Callers on `classify_fault`**: `wiki_file_push` treats the page as absent, `wiki_file_pull` answers `not_found`, and `put_wiki_page`'s pre-check treats the page as missing (so `WikiPageConflict("the page does not exist")`), only when `classify_fault(e) == "not_found"`; `ticket_delete` and `ticket_attachment_delete` answer their own `permission_denied` (with the handler's corrective action) only when `classify_fault(e) == "permission_denied"`. Any other fault is re-raised, to the dispatcher for the handlers (`server_error` for a fault `classify_fault` cannot classify) and to the caller for `put_wiki_page`, which no longer reports a conflict for a code-1 `'Invalid Wiki page name 'Not Found Notes''`. `put_wiki_page`'s pre-check re-raises that fault unwrapped (`unwrap_tracrpc_fault`, fault code kept), as it does a `putPage` fault: the message reads `Invalid Wiki page name 'Not Found Notes'`, with no `while executing 'wiki.getPageInfo()'` wrapper. The old permission test also missed the real shape, `Fault(403, "TICKET_ADMIN privileges are required to perform this operation on Ticket #5")` (no `permission` or `denied` in the text): the dispatcher still answered `permission_denied`, but without the handler's TICKET_ADMIN/`tracopt.ticket.deleter` guidance, which a real 403 now gets
  - **`ticket_actions`, `get_recent_wiki_changes`, the `ticket_comment_*` tools and `timeline_search` use the missing-method check**: `is_missing_method_fault(e, "getActions")` and `is_missing_method_fault(e, "getRecentChanges")`, and in the shared `_translate_missing_method` helpers of `ticket_comment.py` (`editComment`, `deleteComment`, `replyToComment`, `getCommentHistory`, the method each tool called) and `timeline_read.py` (`getFilters` or `getEvents`, whichever was in flight), which had tested the raw text for `not found` with `method` and no method name or code at all. A fault counts for code -32601, or for text that names that method and says `not found` with `method`, or `no such method`. The methods are passed as the bare name, not `ticket.getActions`: a server whose text omits the service prefix (`Fault(1, "No such method: getRecentChanges")`) still counts, and a dotted text contains the bare name anyway. The helpers' wording and return values are unchanged, but a code -32601 fault whose text lacks `not found` is now translated too, and a resource fault that merely quotes a name containing `method` (`Comment 3 not found on ticket #5 'MethodNotes'`) is no longer swallowed as a missing plugin. A resource fault saying "not found" (`Fault(404, "Ticket 999 not found")`, `Fault(1, "Wiki page 'Not Found Notes' not found")`) no longer reads as a missing method: `ticket_actions` re-raises instead of answering `method_not_available`, and `get_recent_wiki_changes` re-raises instead of silently falling back to listing every page. TracXMLRPC's real MethodNotFound is code -32601, which matches whatever the text says
  - **`core/faults.py` (a pure move, no behaviour change)**: `classify_fault`, `fault_text` (was `_fault_text`) and its quote regexes moved from `mcp/tools/errors.py`, and `unwrap_tracrpc_fault`, `is_missing_method_fault` (was `_is_missing_method_fault`) and the wrapper regex from `core/client.py`; the module itself imports only the standard library (importing it still runs `trac_mcp_server.core/__init__.py`, which loads `core.client`). `errors.classify_fault` and `core.client.unwrap_tracrpc_fault` are the same objects as before (re-exported, asserted by identity), so no importer changes. The one visible effect: `trac-convert`'s `_classify_trac_error` imports `classify_fault` from `core.faults`, so classifying a fault no longer imports the whole `mcp.tools` package (every tool handler); `core.client` still loads, through the package `__init__`; a fresh interpreter that classifies a fault now loads none of `trac_mcp_server.mcp.tools`
  - **`_is_mid_air_collision` is left alone, deliberately**: it already needs fault code 1 AND the phrase "modified since", a pair no resource name produces on its own, and it is not a not-found/permission/missing-method reading of a fault, so `classify_fault` has nothing to say about it
  - **Tested**: for each caller a code-1 fault quoting a name (`'Not Found Notes'`, `'PermissionNotes'`) or a resource "not found" fault must not be read as absent / not_found / permission_denied / a missing method, and the real shape still is (`Fault(404, 'Wiki page "X" does not exist')`, the 403 above, -32601 naming the method); in `tests/test_mcp/tools/test_wiki_file.py`, `test_ticket.py`, `test_ticket_attachment.py`, `test_ticket_comment.py`, `test_timeline.py` and `tests/test_client.py` (which also pins the bare-name and unwrapped-pre-check cases), plus the identity tests in `test_errors.py` and a fresh-interpreter import-cost test in `tests/test_cli_convert_trac.py`. Seeded: on the unfixed callers, the two `wiki_file` code-1 tests, the two `put_wiki_page` and `get_recent_wiki_changes` resource-fault tests, the `ticket_actions` 404 test and the code-1 and real-403 tests of both delete handlers failed (nine tests), and with `cli/convert.py` importing from `mcp.tools.errors` the import-cost test failed, listing the 26 `mcp.tools` modules loaded; with the fix they pass
- **Indented quote lines directly before a list marker are a quote on the read leg: the run is cut at its first marker, the head becomes `> ` lines (or the fallback fence) and one blank line separates it from the list (Trac /trac_mcp_server #182).** `_convert_indented_blocks` (#73) left a whole run that holds a list marker untouched, so `P` / blank / `  q` / ` * c` stayed raw indented text in the Markdown and came back as the paragraph `q`, and `    q4` / ` * c` came back as a `{{{` block with the list lost
  - **The rule** (`tracwiki_to_markdown.py`, read leg only): after the definition-list, lettered-list and brace steps (unchanged, whole run), a run whose first line is not a list marker is cut at its first marker line when no list can be open above it. That is: the previous non-blank line is at column 0 or absent, or belongs to an earlier indented run that held no marker, brace, definition list or lettered list (a flag set by such a run and cleared by a column-zero line; a block's own lines leave it alone, since a block joins an open item, a stashed `#!comment`, `#!table`, `#!td` or `#!th` block's fence, placeholder and closing line included; a column-zero line that Trac reads as a list item, `- a`, `* a`, `1. a` or a stashed `a. a`, sets it, because Trac's list indent is `\s*` and the indented lines after it continue that item); the run does not start straight after a block's closing line inside an open item (`_split_past_bound`'s seed, `_starts_inside_open_item`, which also reads a stashed processor block as a block); and the first marker is not indented 4 or more columns or by a tab (the blank line would make CommonMark read it as indented code and the round trip would lose the list). The head is then classified on its own: a table row or heading keeps master's bytes, a canonical indent becomes `> ` lines, anything else (column 1, 4 or 6, a 4-space nesting under a 2-space head) goes through the `tracwiki-unconverted` fallback. The rest of the run is the next run and takes the list path as before, `_split_past_bound` included
  - **What Trac renders** (measured on /trac_test): the indented lines before the marker are always a `<blockquote>`, nested only by relative indent, and the list after them is always a top-level list after the blockquote. That held for the head at column 1, 2, 4 and 6, two head lines, `  q` / `    deeper`, a marker deeper than the quote (`  q` / `   * c`), an ordered marker (`  q` / `  1. c`) and no paragraph before. It is exactly what Trac renders with a blank line before the marker, a shape the read leg already got right
  - **The blank line and the accepted round-trip byte change**: when the head became `> ` lines or a fallback fence, exactly one blank line follows it (CommonMark otherwise reads the list marker as a lazy continuation of the quote, or the fence as part of it); a head left untouched gets none. The write leg does not change, so the round trip gains that blank line (`  q` / ` * c` comes back as `  q` / blank / ` * c`), with the same rendering in Trac. Accepted
  - **Declined, `master`'s bytes**: a quote run after a list, with or without a blank line between (#181/#183), a run whose first line is a marker, a head that is a table row or heading, a run with a definition term or lettered item (the whole run still falls back), a run after a column-zero list item (the `WikiFormatting` page's `- items can start...` / `  and they can span multiple lines` / `  - be careful...`: one `<ul>` with a nested `<ul>` on /trac_test), a run straight after a block inside an open item, a run after a stashed processor block in an open item, and a marker indented 4+ columns or by a tab (`P` / `  q` / `    * c` keeps its list; before this fix the cut wrote `    - c` after a blank line and the round trip came back as a `{{{` block). A column-zero continuation after a column-zero item that follows a list (` * x` / `- a` / `  q`) is `master`'s bytes too, wrong in Trac (`q` continues item `a`) and owned by #190
  - **Tested**: `tests/test_converter_quote_before_list.py` pins the exact Markdown, the mistune HTML (blockquote or fallback `<pre>`, then the list as a sibling) and the exact round-trip TracWiki for 15 shapes (columns 1, 2, 4, 6; two head lines; a nested head; a deeper marker; ordered, also not from 1; start of document; a longer list; an earlier quote run; a later quote run after the list; a head followed by a past-bound split), and master's exact bytes for 22 unchanged shapes (seven with a column-0 bullet, star, ordered or lettered item before the run; two with a marker indented 4 columns or by a tab and one with 5; three with a stashed processor block in a column-0 or indented item). 71 tests in all; 44 of the first 55 and the 6 review-fix rows fail on the code before the review fixes. Seeded: with the cut disabled, all 14 mistune assertions failed (44 failures in all); with a block's lines clearing the open-list flag, the two block-in-open-item cases failed; with a column-0 item not setting the flag, the six column-0 cases failed; with the cut disabled again after the review fixes, 47 of 71 failed, all 15 mistune assertions among them; restored, they pass
  - **Corpus** (all 2,075 cached documents from both stores, both legs, `master` 09f4a38 against this change, after the review fixes): 2 documents change, on both legs: `auto_pm` `comment:228:8` (a column-1 line after a table row, before a list) and `trac_mcp_server` `comment:103:2` (a column-1 line after a blank line, before a list). Trac renders a blockquote and then the list for both, and the fallback fence now restores the source bytes. The other 2,073, the `WikiFormatting` pages of both instances included, are byte-identical (a first version of the rule cut the `WikiFormatting` `  and they can span multiple lines` line after a column-0 `- ` item and wrote it back as a citation; the column-0 rule above fixed it)
- **`trac-convert`, `wiki_create` and `wiki_delete` no longer read a code-1 fault, or a page name in a fault, as a missing page (Trac /trac_mcp_server #189).** Three callers still carried their own classifiers after #188, the three #189 named: `trac-convert`'s `_classify_trac_error` treated ANY `Fault` with code 1 as "wiki page not found", and the existence pre-checks of `wiki_create` and `wiki_delete` matched `not found`/`does not exist` anywhere in the raw fault text, quoted page names included. TracXMLRPC sends code 1 for every `TracError`, so `Fault(1, "'Invalid Wiki page name 'NotFoundHandling'' while executing 'wiki.getPage()'")` was reported by `trac-convert` as a missing page, and `wiki_create` on an invalid page name containing `Not Found` went on to create it
  - **These three go through `classify_fault`** (codes first: 404/403; then the text with the wrapper and quoted names removed). `trac-convert` answers `wiki page not found: <page>` only for `not_found` (or `resource not found (fault <code>): <text>` when no page name is set, where it said a hard-coded "fault 1"), `Trac permission denied (<action>): <text>` only for `permission_denied`, and `Trac fault (<action>): <text>` for everything else, so a quoted `PermissionNotes` is no longer "permission denied" either. `wiki_create` treats the page as absent, and `wiki_delete` answers `not_found`, only when `classify_fault(e) == "not_found"`; any other fault is re-raised to the dispatcher, which translates it with `translate_xmlrpc_error` (`permission_denied` for a 403, `server_error` for a fault `classify_fault` cannot classify). Both still use `getPage`, which raises for a missing page where `getPageInfo` does not. A real `Fault(404, 'Wiki page "X" does not exist')` behaves as before on all three
  - **Other raw-text classifiers remain** (`wiki_file_push`/`wiki_file_pull`, `put_wiki_page`'s version pre-check, `ticket_read`, and the permission arms of `ticket_write` and `ticket_attachment`); they are Trac /trac_mcp_server #191
  - **`TracClient.get_wiki_page_with_metadata` was removed** as dead code: nothing in `src`, `scripts` or `docs` called it, only its two tests (deleted with it), and it read code 1 as "page not found" too
  - **Tested** in `tests/test_cli_convert_trac.py` (read and write paths with the code-1 `NotFoundHandling` fault, the read path with a code-1 `PermissionNotes` fault, the missing-page test rewritten to the real `Fault(404, 'Wiki page "NoSuchPage" does not exist')`; `test_check_trac_ping_auth_fault_classified` now uses `Fault(500, "Forbidden")` for its generic fault, and the new `test_check_trac_ping_403_fault_is_permission_denied` pins the one visible change for a code alone: `trac-convert --check-trac` on a bare `Fault(403, "Forbidden")` now says `Trac permission denied`, where it said `Trac fault`) and `tests/test_mcp/tools/test_wiki.py` (`wiki_create` and `wiki_delete`, at handler level and through the dispatcher, with a code-1 `Invalid Wiki page name 'Not Found Notes'` fault, plus the 404 shape still meaning absent / `not_found`). Seeded: on the unfixed code the three convert tests and the four code-1 `wiki_create`/`wiki_delete` tests failed; with the fix they pass
- **Fault text is read with quoted names removed, by `classify_fault`, which `translate_xmlrpc_error`, `wiki_conventions._fault_reason`, the `trac://wiki` resource and the `wiki_attachment_delete` handler use (Trac /trac_mcp_server #188).** After #186 the code arms were right, but any other fault was still classified by tokens that can come from a NAME the fault merely mentions: `Fault(1, "'Invalid Wiki page name 'PermissionNotes/../x'' while executing 'wiki.putPage()'")` came back `permission_denied`, and a milestone `Already Shipped` made `Milestone "Already Shipped" has open tickets` an `already_exists`. The `trac://wiki` resource had its own classifier (`"not found" in text or faultCode == 1`, then `"version" in text`), so a 403 on `VersionNotes` read as `invalid_version` and TracXMLRPC's real missing-page fault `Fault(404, 'Wiki page "X" does not exist')` read as `server_error`
  - **The rule, in `mcp/tools/errors.py`'s new public `classify_fault(fault, domain=None)`**: codes first (409 `version_conflict`, 404 `not_found`, 403 `permission_denied`), as before. For any other code the text is read after TracXMLRPC's `'<message>' while executing '<method>()'` wrapper is removed (`core.client.unwrap_tracrpc_fault`, public) and, if the whole message is wrapped in one pair of identical quotes (`'...'` or `"..."`), that outer pair is removed; then every quoted span is replaced by a placeholder (double-quoted `"..."`; single-quoted `'...'` only where the quote is not inside a word, so "don't" is untouched, and the span may itself contain apostrophes and ends at the first quote not followed by a word character, so `'Bob's/PermissionNotes'` is one name; typographic `‘...’` and `“...”`), then lower-cased. On that text: `not found`/`does not exist` is `not_found`, `permission`/`denied` is `permission_denied`, and in the `milestone` domain `exists`/`already` is `already_exists`; else `None`. Trac quotes names (`'X'` or `"X"`), so a quoted name is not evidence; the text arms stay because the code-1 faults for the plugin's `TracError("Comment 3 not found on ticket #5")` and Trac's `ResourceExistsError` carry their meaning only in text. `PermissionError` names its resource unquoted but is always 403, which the code arm handles
  - **Four users**: `translate_xmlrpc_error` calls `classify_fault` and dispatches to the same response builders, so its responses are byte-identical for every input that was classified the same before; `wiki_conventions._fault_reason` maps `not_found` and `permission_denied` through it and everything else to `error`; the `trac://wiki` resource uses `classify_fault(e, "wiki")`; `wiki_attachment_delete` answers `permission_denied` only when `classify_fault(e, "wiki") == "permission_denied"` (it tested the raw fault text for `permission`/`denied`, so a missing attachment on a page named `PermissionNotes` was a permission error; any other fault is re-raised to the dispatcher, where it is `not_found`). `trac-convert`'s and `wiki_create`'s own checks are #189
  - **The resource's Fault branch**: `permission_denied` returns `Error (permission_denied): <fault text>` (a 403 on `VersionNotes?version=3` is no longer an `invalid_version`); `not_found` without `?version=` is the usual not-found answer with similar-page suggestions (a 404 `does not exist` fault is no longer a `server_error`); `not_found` with `?version=N` asks `getPageInfo` without a version whether the page exists at all: a non-empty answer is `invalid_version`; an empty one, or a fault that itself classifies `not_found`, is the not-found answer; any other probe exception (a 403 fault, a transport error) returns `Error (not_found): <the original fault's text>`, which reports what Trac said to the read without claiming the page is missing and without suggestions; anything else is `server_error`. The `faultCode == 1` and `"version" in text` tests are gone, so a code-1 fault with no not-found wording is now a `server_error`, not a not-found
  - **Remaining gap**: an UNQUOTED name in a fault with a code other than 403, 404 or 409 can still match a token (`Invalid page name PermissionNotes` as code 1 is `permission_denied`). Trac quotes names in its own messages, so this needs a message that does not
  - **Tested**: in `tests/test_mcp/tools/test_errors.py`, the page-name, `"NotFoundHandling"` and `Already Shipped` faults are `server_error`; regression guards for `Comment 3 not found on ticket #5` (`not_found`), `Milestone "v1" already exists.` (`already_exists`), the wiki-domain `The page 'X' already exists.` (`server_error`, the arm is milestone-only) and `You don't have permission to do that` (`permission_denied`); direct `classify_fault` rows for each code, code over text, and `None`. In `tests/test_wiki_resources.py`: the 403 on `VersionNotes` with `?version=3` (`permission_denied`), the 404 missing-page fault without a version (`not_found`), a 404 at version 9 of a page `getPageInfo` finds (`invalid_version`), the same with `getPageInfo` raising 404 or returning `0` (`not_found`), and a code-1 fault with no not-found wording (`server_error`). In `tests/test_mcp/tools/test_wiki_conventions.py`: code-1 `Invalid page name 'PermissionNotes'` is `error`. Review fixes: in `test_errors.py`, `Invalid Wiki page name 'Bob's/PermissionNotes'` is `None` (with `You don't have permission to do that` still `permission_denied` and `Comment 3 not found on ticket #5` still `not_found`), `'Wiki page "Foo" not found'` (outer quotes, no wrapper) is `not_found`, and typographic-quoted names are `None`; in `test_wiki_resources.py`, a versioned read whose probe raises 403 or `ConnectionError` answers `Error (not_found): <original fault text>` with no suggestions; in `test_wiki_attachment.py`, a delete fault `'Attachment 'wiki:PermissionNotes: x.png' does not exist.' while executing 'wiki.deleteAttachment()'` is re-raised by the handler (`not_found` through the registry), and `Fault(403, "WIKI_DELETE privileges are required ...")` is `permission_denied`
- **A nested ordered list whose first number is not 1 now stays a nested list on the read leg: it gets a blank line after its parent's text and moves to the parent's Markdown text column (Trac /trac_mcp_server #180).** CommonMark lets only a list that starts at 1 interrupt a paragraph, so ` * a` / `   4. x` / `   5. y` came back as ` - a` / `   4. x` / `   5. y` and mistune rendered `<ul><li>a 4. x 5. y</li></ul>`: the nested list was the parent's paragraph text. ` 1. a` / `    4. x` lost it the same way, and ` 1. a` / `   4. x` (column 3, left of the parent's text column 4) read as a sibling item, so the round trip wrote ` 1. a` / ` 2. x`.
  - **Read leg, one pass** (`tracwiki_to_markdown.py`; the write leg is untouched): `_shift_nested_markers` no longer freezes such a marker (#179's review fix 3, with the `no_one` field of `_ShiftItem` and the `prev_text` bookkeeping that fed it): it moves to its parent's text column through the ordinary clamp, which is also what keeps ` 1. a` / `   4. x` from reading as a sibling item, and its subtree moves with it. The same pass writes the blank line, right before the moved marker, because it is the one place that knows per item whether it is frozen or under a tab gap (freezing propagates down its stack). It does so when ALL of these hold: the marker is an ordered number other than 1 and opens a NEW level directly under its parent (the stack is not empty and nothing was popped on arrival); neither the item nor its parent is frozen or has a tab gap; the previous line is paragraph text of that parent, i.e. its own marker line with text after the marker, or a continuation line it owns that is not a block line (a code placeholder, a fence, a `||` table row; a line starting with ONE pipe is paragraph text); and that line does not end in a hard break (two trailing spaces, what `[[BR]]` becomes). It is the nested counterpart of #144's top-level rule, which stays in `_separate_list_from_trailing_paragraph`; that pass and `_separate_adjacent_lists` read the inserted line as the blank line a Trac author may write there (` * a` / blank / `   4. x` already passed through), and the marker after it clears their blank-line flags. The first version of this change put the insertion in `_separate_list_from_trailing_paragraph` and re-inferred freezing from columns and tabs (`_nested_at_a_known_text_column`, now deleted); that checked only the immediate parent, so a subtree frozen by a grandparent's tab gap (` *<TAB>a` / `  * b` / `    4. x`) still got a blank line.
  - **Why a blank line**: it is the only CommonMark form. Indenting the list further reads as indented code, and a changed marker or delimiter does not help, since no ordered list but one starting at 1 can interrupt a paragraph. The Markdown becomes loose (`<li><p>`), the accepted cost, as for #145's blank line; the write leg reads a loose item as a tight one and already collapses the blank line (` - a` / blank / `   4. x` / `   5. y` / ` - b` writes ` * a` / `   4. x` / `   5. y` / ` * b`).
  - **Unchanged (`master`'s bytes)**: a nested `1.` or a bullet after parent text (both interrupt a paragraph already), an ordered marker that opens a sibling list after a deeper item (` * a` / `   * b` / `   4. x`: mistune already nests `<ol start="4">` in `a`), a nested list after a code block or a table row, top-level lists, and every frozen shape (tab-indented marker, tab after the parent's or a grandparent's marker, root at column 4 or more). Three more shapes are declined on purpose: an EMPTY parent item (` * `, ` 1. [[BR]]`: the blank line ended the item, and ` 1. [[BR]]` / `    4. x` / `    5. y` round-tripped into a code block instead of a nested `<ol start="4">`); a hard break at the end of the line before the marker (` * a[[BR]]` / `   4. x`: CommonMark drops a hard break at the end of a paragraph, so the blank line lost the `[[BR]]` on the round trip; the `[[BR]]` survives now and the Markdown list stays lost for that shape, the #180 defect accepted there); and a frozen subtree under a grandparent's tab gap. Known and unchanged: a `[[BR]]` at the end of an item inside a properly nested list is lost on the round trip as it is for a nested `1.`, so ` * a` / `   4. x[[BR]]` / `   5. y` no longer keeps it.
  - **Tested** in the new `tests/test_converter_nested_non_one_ordered.py`: for each of twelve shapes (bullet and ordered parents, column 3 and 4, a continuation line before the list, a sibling after it, one level deeper, a bullet under the moved item, a paragraph before the list, `10.`/`11.`, two lists) the exact Markdown, what mistune renders (an `<ol start="N">` inside the parent's `<li>`) and the round trip, byte-identical to the source except where the write leg's own column differs (column 4 reads back at column 3 as for a nested `1.`); plus the unchanged shapes, the frozen ones, a quote line before the marker, a stable second trip, and the review fixes: an empty parent (bullet and ordered), a hard break before the marker on a marker line and on a continuation line (the `[[BR]]` survives the round trip), a subtree frozen by a grandparent's tab gap, a single-pipe line before the marker (renders a nested `<ol start="4">`), a `||t||` row inside the item (the table converter pulls it out of the item, as on `master`; pinned), and the later passes' handling of what follows the inserted blank line (a deeper bullet, a continuation line, a `[[BR]]` alone on a line, a processor block). Three rows of `tests/test_converter_nested_under_ordered.py` (review fix 3's freeze) and one of `tests/test_converter_ordered_list_start.py` (`test_nested_list_from_4_under_a_bullet_keeps_its_tracwiki_bytes`, which pinned the lost list) asserted the old behaviour and changed to the new.
  - **Seeded**: with the blank-line insertion in `_shift_nested_markers` disabled, all twelve `test_the_converted_text_renders_nested` rows failed (and the Markdown and round-trip rows, the single-pipe test and the later-pass rows with them); on the unchanged code the Markdown, render and round-trip rows failed for the reasons above. The review-fix tests (empty parent, hard break, grandparent tab gap, single pipe) failed on the first version of this change and pass now.
  - **Corpus**: on the 2,075 cached documents of both stores (`~/.cache/trac-sweep`), `master` and this change give byte-identical Markdown and byte-identical round trips (0 changed on either leg).
- **`translate_xmlrpc_error` reports a `version_conflict` only for fault code 409, and classifies 404 and 403 by code too, so a fault that merely names something containing "version" is no longer called a conflict (Trac /trac_mcp_server #186).** The old `"version" in fault_text` arm fired for any fault no earlier arm matched: `Invalid Wiki page name 'VersionNotes/../x'`, a milestone named `Version 2.0`, a wiki manipulator error on page `VersionNotes`, and Trac's `ResourceNotFound("No version 3 for comment 2 on Ticket #5")` all came back as `version_conflict` with "Fetch current version ..., then retry update" advice. No real conflict reached that arm by its text: ticket conflicts are built by the ticket handlers, TracXMLRPC's own `_ts` faults do not contain "version", and wiki conflicts arrive as `WikiPageConflict` since #185
  - **409 only**: the conflict arm now matches `faultCode == 409`, the conflict code of the `wiki.putPageIfVersion` plugin (TracXMLRPC never uses 409). The action lookup and the `version_conflict` type are unchanged; the arm is a backstop, since the client already maps a 409 to `WikiPageConflict`. A fault whose text says "version conflict" under any other code is now a `server_error`
  - **Codes are checked before any text (review fix)**: the code checks sat behind the text arms, so a 409 conflict whose text says "does not exist" (the plugin's missing-page conflict, `current_version=none`) came back `not_found`, a 409 on a page named `PermissionPolicy` came back `permission_denied`, and a 403 naming a milestone `Not found bugs` came back `not_found`. `translate_xmlrpc_error` now classifies 409, 404 and 403 first, each with the response its arm always gave (the version action with the entity substituted, the not-found action and entity logic, the milestone `TICKET_ADMIN` suffix), so a 409 conflict is still a conflict whatever page it names and whatever its text says. The text arms follow in their old order (not found / does not exist, permission / denied, milestone exists / already, then `server_error`). `wiki_conventions._fault_reason` now also maps code 404 to `not_found` (and checks 404 and 403 before its text tests), so it agrees with the dispatcher. The remaining text arms, and the trac://wiki resource's own classifier, are #188
  - **404 and 403 by code**: TracXMLRPC sends every `ResourceNotFound` as code 404 and every `PermissionError` as code 403, so the `not_found` arm also matches code 404 (this is what now classifies "No version N for comment M on Ticket #K", which contains neither "not found" nor "does not exist") and the `permission_denied` arm matches code 403 in every domain, not only milestone (e.g. `WIKI_ADMIN required`). The milestone message suffix and `_DOMAIN_MESSAGES` are unchanged
  - **Tested**: in `tests/test_mcp/tools/test_errors.py`, `test_version_conflict` and `test_version_conflict_with_entity` use code 409 with the plugin's `[wiki-version-conflict current_version=7]` text; new rows assert `server_error` for the page-name and `Version 2.0` milestone faults and for "Page version conflict detected" under code 1, `not_found` for `Fault(404, "No version 3 for comment 2 on Ticket #5")`, `permission_denied` for `Fault(403, "WIKI_ADMIN required")`, and `version_conflict` for `Fault(409, "x")`; review fixes: the plugin's two real 409 texts (missing page, `current_version=none`; and `PermissionPolicy`, `current_version=4`) are `version_conflict`, `Fault(403, "WIKI_VIEW privileges are required ... on Milestone Not found bugs ...")` is `permission_denied` with the `TICKET_ADMIN` suffix, and `Fault(404, 'Wiki page "PermissionNotes" does not exist')` is `not_found`; in `tests/test_mcp/tools/test_wiki_conventions.py`, `test_style_page_404_without_not_found_wording` pins `_fault_reason` on a 404 whose text lacks both phrases. `test_xmlrpc_version_conflict_translated` (code 409, ticket) passes unchanged
- **A `wiki.putPage` fault is a version conflict only if the page's version moved, and a fault that merely names a page containing "version" is no longer turned into one by the wiki write path (Trac /trac_mcp_server #185).** `TracClient._put_wiki_page_checked_here` turned any putPage fault whose text contained "version" into a bare `ValueError("Version conflict - page was modified by another user")`, which the dispatcher reported as `validation_error` ("Check parameter values and retry") with no current version. That test misclassified in both directions: a genuine save collision (two putPage calls load the same version and both INSERT version N+1; on sqlite the loser's fault is `UNIQUE constraint failed: wiki.name, wiki.version`) lost its current version, and an unrelated fault such as `WIKI_MODIFY privileges are required ... on VersionNotes` was reported as a conflict
  - **Decided by state, not text**: for a versioned write the client now re-reads the page's version after the fault (best effort). If it is known and differs from the version the caller passed, the fault is raised as `WikiPageConflict` carrying the current version, so `wiki_update` answers `version_conflict` with "Current version is N". A re-read that is equal, fails or is not a version re-raises the original fault (unwrapped, see below); an unversioned write or a create re-raises it with no re-read. The collision's wording is backend-specific, so no message is matched. Any putPage fault means nothing was stored (Trac runs change listeners after the commit)
  - **The handler's substring branch is gone**: `_handle_update` in `wiki_write.py` no longer catches `xmlrpc.client.Fault` and tests it for "version"; every real conflict on both write paths (the plugin's 409 and the client's precheck or post-fault re-read) already arrives as `WikiPageConflict`, so that branch only misclassified. A fault now reaches the dispatcher's `translate_xmlrpc_error`, so the `VersionNotes` permission fault is `permission_denied`. A fault no earlier arm of `translate_xmlrpc_error` matches still reaches its generic `"version"` arm and reads as `version_conflict` (for example `Invalid Wiki page name 'Version...'`); that shared arm is #186
  - **Unwrapped**: a re-raised putPage fault loses TracXMLRPC's `while executing 'wiki.putPage()'` wrapper, as the plugin path's already did, so a fault reads the same with or without `putPageIfVersion`
  - **Tested**: client tests for the collision (re-read sees the next version), the page-name fault (original `Fault` raised, not a `ValueError`), a failed re-read, an unversioned fault (no getPageInfo posted), and the wrapped fault unwrapped with its faultCode kept, versioned and unversioned; handler tests for `WikiPageConflict`, a propagating `Fault`, and the same fault through `ToolRegistry.call_tool` giving `permission_denied`
- **A list nested left of its parent's Markdown text column is moved to it on the read leg, so it stays nested (Trac /trac_mcp_server #179).** Trac nests a list marker indented past its parent's marker at ANY deeper column, but CommonMark nests one only at or past the parent's text column (4 under ` 1. `, 5 under ` 10. `, 3 under ` * `), and the read leg copied the source column. So ` 1. outer` / `   * inner a` / ` 2. outer2` left the item (the bullets became a second list and the outer list restarted), ` 1. outer` / `   1. inner a` / `   2. inner b` / ` 2. outer2` came back as one flat list `1.`-`4.`, and three levels were flattened.
  - **The rule** (`TracWikiParser._shift_nested_markers`, a new pass at the top of `_convert_lists`: after `_split_past_bound`, which reads raw columns, and after `_convert_code_blocks`, so a fence moves with its item; before `_separate_list_from_trailing_paragraph` (#145/#149/#176/#178), which then read their text columns off the moved markers): a stack of the open items (source marker column, shift, output text column). A marker line pops the items at or past its column, as `_continued_item` and `_list_stack_step` do; a root marker stays; under a parent it goes to `column + parent shift`, raised to the parent's output text column when that is left of it and clamped to that column + 3 when it is past it (CommonMark reads column + 4 as indented code). Its shift is inherited by its children. A non-marker line goes to the item Trac continues it with (`_continued_item`, lower side) and moves by that item's shift, so the columns inside an item keep their distances and #149/#176/#178's bound and text-column checks hold on the moved text; a fence and a table row move with their item (a `>` line moves by its item's shift too, but CommonMark does not let a quote continue a paragraph, so after a nested item it lands in the PARENT's item: a gap already on `master`, not fixed here, #187), and the body of a block fenced at an item's text column (#144), which lives in the code stash, moves with it (`_item_fence_stashes`). A line Trac puts outside the list (after a blank line, or with no owner) is not touched. A blank line ends the list, except a whitespace-only line marked with `_BR_LINE_MARK`: the one `[[BR]]` alone makes (#178) and, since the review, the one a dropped macro leaves of its line (`unknown_macros="drop"`; see the review fixes below).
  - **Measured** on `/trac_test` with `convert_preview` (render only; ticket #179 comments 1-2): every shape renders as the nested structure, a marker at m + 1, 2, 4, 5 and 6 under ` 1. ` nests, ` 1. outer` / `   1. mid` / `     * deep` / `     deep cont` / `   2. mid2` / `  back in outer` / ` 2. outer2` is three levels with `deep cont` in `deep` and `back in outer` in `outer` after the nested list, and ` * outer` / `  * inner at m+1` nests too. A MARKER line is not held to #175's `m + 3` bound, which applies to text lines only.
  - **Bullet parents are in scope** (the operator's decision, ticket comment 2): the rule is keyed on "left of the parent's text column", so ` * outer` / `  * inner at m+1` (CommonMark reads column 2 as a sibling) is moved to column 3 too. Stock `TracEnvironment` and `TracFastCgi` in both stores and #102 comment 3 are this shape.
  - **Knock-on, #176's "between" line**: ` 1. outer` / `   * inner a` / `  back in outer` / ` 2. outer2` was refused by #176's own #179 gate (the nested list sat left of the item's text column, so moving the line would have made an indented code block). With the nested list at the text column the gate passes and `back in outer` stays in `outer`, after a blank line, at column 4. The gate is not removed; the shape now round-trips to the write leg's own form. Four rows of `tests/test_converter_ordered_continuation_bound.py` (`test_line_after_a_nested_list_that_closes_it`, `10.`/`100.` at depth 0 and 1) and two of `tests/test_converter_between_column_continuation.py` (the refused-move row `ordered outer, nested left of the text column`, now its own test, and `test_the_writers_own_ordered_outer_output_never_reads_as_code`, now exact bytes plus "no code block in the item") asserted `master`'s refused bytes and changed to the new ones.
  - **Review fixes** (all in `_shift_nested_markers` and `_convert_macros`; the write leg is untouched):
    - **A root list after a blank line stays a separate list.** ` * a` / `  * b` / blank / `   * c` is two lists in Trac, but with `b` moved the text column of `a` is 3 and `c` at column 3 was read by CommonMark as nested in `a`, next to `b`; the round trip lost the blank line and re-nested it. A root marker after a blank line at or past the previous list's output text column, when this pass moved something in that list, goes to the previous list's marker column, where #177's `_separate_adjacent_lists` (a marker left of the open list's text column after a blank line) starts a new list: ` - a` / `   - b` / blank / ` * c`, and the round trip keeps the two lists. The later items of that list (no blank line between) go to the same column, its own nested list and continuation lines move with it, and a third list after another blank line chains. An ordered list's delimiter switches (` 1)`) and a bullet after an ordered list needs no switch. The same shape after a list this pass did not change (` * a` / blank / `   * c`, or with an unmoved `b` at column 3) is #183's and keeps `master`'s bytes (pinned), and so does a marker already left of the previous text column and a list after a paragraph.
    - **Tab after the parent's marker: frozen.** `_item_text_column` counts a tab as one column, so ` 1.<TAB>a` / `   * b` gave the text "a - b". A parent whose marker-to-text gap holds a tab gives its children shift 0, `master`'s bytes for that subtree (the whole subtree, bullet parents too), like the tab-indented markers of the first round.
    - **A nested ordered list not starting at 1: frozen, #180 owns it.** CommonMark does not let such a list interrupt the parent's paragraph, so ` 1. a` / `   10. b` / `   11. c` and ` * a` / `  3. b` became paragraph text after the move (`master` kept list items). A nested ordered marker whose number is not 1 that follows the parent's text line directly (no empty line between) gets shift 0, and so do the siblings after it and its subtree: `master`'s bytes. Making these real nested lists is #180's; the rows pin `master`'s bytes and name it.
    - **A dropped macro's leftover line is item content.** With `unknown_macros="drop"` a macro alone on an indented line leaves a whitespace-only line, which Trac counts as item content (the macro was on it) and the pass took for a blank line: ` 1. a` / `   * b` / `     [[RecentChanges(x)]]` / `     more of b` moved `- b` and left `more of b` at its old column, so it fell into item `a` (and with a following `   * c` too). The macro-drop path now marks the whitespace-only remains with `_BR_LINE_MARK`, the same mark as `[[BR]]` alone (one `settle_marks` helper does both, keeping one mark on a line that ends up whitespace-only), so every walk that honours it (`_shift_nested_markers`, #178's rule in `_separate_list_from_trailing_paragraph`) reads it as non-blank. A raw whitespace-only source line is NOT marked and still ends the list (#178's tests pin that and still pass), and a macro alone on a column-0 line leaves an empty line, which stays unmarked and ends the list as before. A document that already holds the mark character is not marked at all, as for `[[BR]]`.
    - **Processor blocks inside an item.** A blank line inside an in-item `{{{#!div ...}}}` (any `{{{#!processor}}}` container still in the text, not stashed like code) emptied the stack partway: the opener and the first lines moved, the lines after the blank, the closer and the next sibling did not. Every line from the opener to its MATCHING closer, nested blocks included (`_matched_blocks`, the discipline of `_passthrough_containers`), now belongs to the item that owns the opener: no stack change, the same shift, so the next sibling moves too and the round trip keeps the closer aligned with its opener. A marker-shaped line inside the block is the block's content, not an item. An opener nobody closes keeps the per-line rules.
    - **Interrupter lines: a known gap, #187.** ` * a` / `  * b` / `  > q` puts `q` in `a`, not `b` (CommonMark does not let a quote continue a paragraph). Already wrong on `master`, not fixed here. The docstring claim that a `>` line "moves with its item" now says what happens, and a test pins the current bytes.
    - **Precheck** in `_move_line`: the placeholder regex runs only on a line that holds `\x00CODE`.
  - **Shift 0 is byte-identical to `master`**: every nesting already at or past the parent's text column (a bullet under a bullet at column 3, column 4 to 7 under ` 1. `, `10.` at 5 to 8, three levels at their text columns) and every list with no nesting keeps its bytes (pinned by 16 rows). Declined shapes keep `master`'s bytes too: a tab-indented marker line, a root marker at column 4 or more (indented code already) and everything nested under either, a tab-indented continuation line, the children of a tab-gap parent and a nested ordered marker not numbered 1 (the review freezes above).
  - **Write leg unchanged.** It writes a nested list at `depth * 2 + 1`, which Trac nests under any parent (measured). TracWiki -> Markdown -> TracWiki is stable for column-3 nesting (` 1. outer` / `   * inner a` / `   * inner b` / ` 2. outer2` comes back as it went in, and so does the ordered form), deeper source nesting comes back at column 3, which renders the same, and the "between" shape round-trips to itself.
  - **Tested** in the new `tests/test_converter_nested_under_ordered.py` (105 offline rows and one live row; the review added 34): every shape of the ticket (bullets and ordered items under ` 1. `, under ` 10. ` and ` 100. `, at m + 1, three levels, a continuation line, the "between" line, a bullet at m + 1 under a bullet) for the exact Markdown, for what CommonMark (mistune) reads out of it (one outer list, the nested list inside item 1, every line in its item) and for a stable round trip; a `{{{ }}}` block (and the same one three levels deep), a `>` quote with the line after it and a `||` table in a `{{{#!div}}}` in a nested item, each equal to the same shape written with the marker already at the text column; the clamp at column 8, children inheriting the shift, a sibling popping back to the parent, a marker after a blank line (a root, unmoved), a line outside every item, an outer item's line before the nested list; 16 pins of `master`'s bytes. Review rows: a list after a blank line (7 shapes for the exact Markdown and the structure CommonMark reads, the same 7 for a round trip that keeps two lists, the exact round trip of the repro, and pins for an unmoved list, a marker left of the text column and a list after a paragraph); the tab gap (3 rows); the non-1 ordered nest (3 pins of `master`'s bytes naming #180, plus the `1.` that still moves and the root after a blank line); a dropped macro (alone, before a sibling, a raw whitespace-only line, a column-0 macro, preserve mode); an item `{{{#!div}}}` with a blank line, nested divs, a marker-shaped line and an unterminated one; the interrupter gap (#187).
  - **Seeded defects**: with the pass removed, 50 of the new rows fail (the other 21 are the pins and the rows that already held on `master`); with the clamp removed, `test_marker_far_past_the_text_column_is_clamped_to_it_plus_three` fails; with the non-marker shift forced to 0, 13 fail (the continuation, three-level, block, quote and table rows); with the stash body not shifted, the 3 fence rows fail. Each restored. On `master` the same 50 fail. Review fixes, each reverted and restored: with the root-after-blank move disabled, 15 rows fail (7 Markdown, 7 round trip, the exact repro); with the macro drop returning `""` instead of the mark, 2 fail (the alone and the before-a-sibling rows); with the processor blocks not walked as units (`_matched_blocks` empty), 3 fail (the blank-line div, the nested divs and the marker-shaped line).
  - **Recall gate** over cached copies of both stores (2,434 documents), `master` c295d45 against the branch, for the read leg and for write(read): 32 documents change, the same 32 on both legs (15 in `/auto_pm`, 17 in `/trac_mcp_server`), and every one is expected (a nested marker left of its parent's text column: 27 under an ordered parent, 5 under a bullet; stock `TracRevisionLog` in both stores, `Permissions`, `ReferenceTool-architecture`, `ReferenceTroubleshooting`, #103, #110, #111, #118, `Rules/version_control/Mothballing`, and `/auto_pm` tickets 114, 150, 177, 198, 199, 201, 215, 224, 230, 257, 263). The other 2,402 are byte-identical on both legs, none raises. In the 32 only leading spaces change (no non-whitespace content, no blank line added or removed), and the clamp fires in none. Against the source's own nesting (a Trac stack walk compared with mistune's list-item depths) master matches in 0 of the 32 and the branch in 31, in the Markdown and in the write(read) TracWiki; the 32nd, `ReferenceTroubleshooting`, now has its bullets right and keeps one mismatch that is on `master` too (an item with a `{{{#!ini` opener glued to its text). The detector's 33rd document, `/auto_pm` ticket 216, nests bullets under a LETTERED item (the #74 verbatim fallback), so it is correctly unchanged. **Re-run after the review fixes** (same cache, `master` c295d45 from a snapshot of the commit, read leg and write(read)): the same 32 documents on both legs (15 + 17), none joined or left the set, the branch's bytes are identical to the first round's in all 2,434 documents, and still only leading spaces change in the 32 (no blank line added or removed, no exception, no mark character in any output). So none of the review fixes changes a stored document's bytes. The same sweep with `unknown_macros="drop"` changes the same 32 documents and no other. Depth check over the 32 (a Trac stack walk compared with mistune's list-item depths): `master` 0 of 32, the branch 31 of 32 (the one left is `ReferenceTroubleshooting`, as before), none regressed.
  - **Live row written, not run**: `test_live_three_level_round_trip_renders_the_nested_structure` (`@pytest.mark.live`) renders the TracWiki -> Markdown -> TracWiki output of the three-level shape on `/trac_test` through `wiki_to_html`, the path `convert_preview` uses (no writes), and asserts one `<ol>` whose item `outer` holds the nested `<ol>` (`mid` with `deep` in a `<ul>`, and `mid2`) and `back in outer`.
- **An ordered list item numbered 10 or more no longer puts its continuation past Trac's marker + 3 bound, where the list ended, and the read leg moves such a line to the item's text column (Trac /trac_mcp_server #178).** `markdown_to_tracwiki` indented every continuation line at `len(prefix) + 1`, the Markdown text column: marker column `m` + 4 for ` 10. ` (+ 5 for ` 100. `). Trac continues an ordered item only up to `m + 3`, so ` 10. j` / `     lazy cont [[BR]][[BR]]` / `     second para` ended the list at the paragraph (Markdown lists that start at 10 or later reach this from the first item since #150). The read leg had the matching hole: ` 10. j` / `    a [[BR]][[BR]]` / `    b` is one item in Trac, but `[[BR]][[BR]]` becomes a blank line and the `b` at column 4 is an indented code block outside the list in CommonMark, so it was written back as a `{{{ b }}}` block.
  - **Measured** on `/trac_test` with `convert_preview` (render only, ticket #178 comment 1): at depth 0 ` 10. j` / text at column 5 ends the list and column 4 continues; at depth 1 (`m = 3`) text at column 7 ends it and column 6 continues; a `>` line at column 5 under ` 10. j` stays inside the item, so quotes are not held to the bound.
  - **Write leg**: for an ordered marker a TEXT continuation goes at `min(len(prefix) + 1, trac_continuation_bound(...))` (`text_indent`, `m + 3`), at exactly three sites: the soft-break and loose-paragraph lines inside the item text, the plain text run after the first run, and the `rest` lines of a #176 "between" paragraph. **The cap applies only to a run with no blank line before it** (review fix): after an empty line Trac has already ended the list, so a run after a blank separator (a heading or an hr child, a paragraph after a nested list that #176 refuses, a `{{{`/`||` passthrough run), and any line after an empty line inside a run or the item text, keep `master`'s text column (`continuation_indent`, `_indent_text_lines`). The first round capped them too, which put the run at column 4 where the read leg then read it as a line of the next block: `10. j` / blank / `# h` / blank / `after` round-tripped to a `{{{` block on the second trip. These keep the item's text column (`continuation_indent`): the quote run (the `>` gap line between two quotes included), the code run (a block joins at any indent, #144) and the table `{{{#!div}}}` run. Bullets are unchanged (`len(prefix) + 1` is already `m + 2`, their bound), and so are items 1-9.
  - **Read leg, narrow on purpose** (the operator's decision, ticket comment 2): a non-marker line that continues the INNERMOST open item, deeper than its marker and no deeper than Trac's bound (`m + 3` for a digits-only ordered item, `m + 2` for a bullet), is re-indented to the item's Markdown text column when that column is PAST the bound (items 10+, or a wider gap after the marker). It needs no blank line since the marker line, and only the whitespace-only line `_convert_macros` made from a `[[BR]]` is "not blank" (it is a hard break inside the item): measured on `/trac_test` on 2026-11-08, Trac ends the list at a raw whitespace-only line exactly as at an empty one (` 10. j` / `    a` / `   ` / `    b` / ` 11. k` renders `a` in item 10 and `b` as a blockquote outside the list), so a raw whitespace-only line (spaces, a tab, the `\r` of a CRLF document) is a blank line and the line after it keeps `master`'s bytes. The converter tells the two apart by MARKING: `_convert_macros` appends `_BR_LINE_MARK` (the ASCII unit separator, which `str.strip` and `\s` count as whitespace, so every pass in between reads a marked line as it read the unmarked one and no output byte changes) to each whitespace-only line a `[[BR]]` made, and `_separate_list_from_trailing_paragraph` removes it as it walks the lines; a document that already holds the character is left unmarked and keeps `master`'s bytes for that rule; `parse` raises if a mark survives the list pass. This is not `blank_since_marker`, which counts every whitespace-only line and would have excluded the case the rule is for. The `_marker_column_rule_applies` and `_MARKDOWN_ITEM_START_RE` gates are #176's. Every other line keeps `master`'s bytes: all one-space bullets and one-space items 1-9, nested list MARKER lines (#179), a line after a real blank line, a line past the bound. The wider shape (a bullet continued at column `m + 1`, or an item 1-9 at `m + 1` or `m + 2`, after a `[[BR]][[BR]]`) is not fixed here; the converter never writes it, and it is pinned to `master`'s bytes by tests.
  - **One bound, one place** (review fix): `trac_continuation_bound(marker_column, ordered=...)` in `converters/common.py` (marker column + 3 for a digits-only ordered item, + 2 for a bullet, #175) is used by `_continued_item`, `_text_column_past_trac_bound` and the write leg's cap; behaviour is byte-identical. The rule's shared gates (`_marker_column_rule_applies`, `_MARKDOWN_ITEM_START_RE`) are evaluated once per line.
  - **Tested** in `tests/test_converter_ordered_continuation_bound.py` (207 offline rows and the live row; the first round's 126 plus 81): the write leg at depth 0 and 1 for `10.` and `100.` (a lazy line, a soft break, a loose second paragraph, a "between" paragraph after a nested list with and without a second paragraph; each at `m + 3`), a quote child, the quote gap line, a code child and a table child at the text column, bullets and item 9 byte-for-byte as on `master`; the read leg on the same shapes (a lazy line at every column `m + 1` to `m + 3`, the `[[BR]][[BR]]` second paragraph, soft breaks, a line after a nested list), a line past the bound, after a real blank line, after `[[BR]][[BR]]` and then a real blank line, and a line that would start a nested item all as on `master`, the wide-gap item, and the bullet / item 1-9 / wider-shape rows pinned to `master`'s bytes; MD -> TW -> MD -> TW stable for each 10+/100+ case at depth 0 and 1.
