Review-fix rows, each at depth 0 and 1 for 10. and 100.: the write leg after a blank separator (a heading child, an hr child, each alone and followed by a paragraph; two paragraphs after a nested list, each after a blank; a blank inside the item text and inside a run; a run after a code child and a blank; a paragraph adjacent to a code child still capped), the `10. j` / blank / `# h` / blank / `after` round trip, and heading / heading-only / hr-only round-trip shapes; the read leg after a raw whitespace-only line, a tab-only line, a CRLF document with an empty and a spaces-only blank (`b` keeps `master`'s bytes in each), a `[[BR]][[BR]]` line and a lone `[[BR]]` line (still moved), a raw whitespace-only line after `[[BR]][[BR]]` (ends the list); a raw unit-separator character in the input is kept as written; the bound helper. On `master` 66 of the first round's rows fail (the write leg, the read leg and the round trips).
  - **Seeded defects**: with the write leg's `text_indent` reverted to `continuation_indent`, 41 rows fail; with the read rule removed, 33 fail; with its blank-line gate on `blank_since_marker` instead of an empty line, 17 fail (the `[[BR]][[BR]]` rows) (first round). Review round: with the cap applied after a blank again (fix 1 reverted), 45 rows fail (the blank-separator rows and the round trips); with the whitespace-only-but-not-empty heuristic put back in place of the marking, 21 fail (the raw whitespace-only, tab-only, CRLF and raw-after-`[[BR]][[BR]]` rows and the unit-separator row); with every whitespace-only line counted as blank, 25 fail (the `[[BR]][[BR]]` and lone `[[BR]]` rows, the wide-gap row, the round trips). Each restored.
  - **Recall gate** over cached copies of both stores (2,434 documents: 309 wiki pages, 481 ticket descriptions, 1,644 comments; none faulted), `master` 052306b against the branch. The read leg: 0 documents change. The write leg fed the read leg's own output: 2 change, both expected: `/auto_pm` ticket 189 comments 12 and 14, an item 11 / 13 whose ` Rollback: ...` line sits at the marker column; the read leg moves it to the text column (col 5) on both versions, and `master` wrote it back at col 5, past the bound, where Trac ends the list, while the branch writes it at col 4, inside the item. Documents with the wider shape: 0 (only 10 documents hold a `[[BR]][[BR]]` at all). Re-run on the review-fix branch from the same cache: the same result (read leg 0, write(read) the same 2 documents, 0 raise, and its output identical to the first round's for every document).
  - **Live row written, not run**: `test_live_loose_ten_item_list_renders_as_one_list_with_item_10_whole` (`@pytest.mark.live`) renders the write leg's output for a loose ten-item list on `/trac_test` through `wiki_to_html`, the path `convert_preview` uses (no writes), and asserts one `<ol>` with ten items and the second paragraph inside item 10.
- **Three live rows no longer write to the default `TRAC_URL` store, and the live rows' fixed names are per-run (Trac /trac_mcp_server #173).** The wiki-attachment, ticket-attachment and `trac-convert` round-trip rows built their client (or ran the CLI) from the bare `TRAC_URL`, which in this repo's `.env` is `/trac_mcp_server`, a real ticket store: they attached to `WikiStart`, attached to a configured ticket, and reseeded a page there. Every live writer now targets `/trac_test`
  - **The three writers moved**: the two attachment rows build their client (the `tests/live_helpers.py` `trac_test_client()`, one place for the `/trac_test` URL) on `/trac_test` of the `TRAC_URL` host, each against its own scratch object (page `Scratch/Ticket173WikiAttachment<hex8>`; ticket `Ticket173AttachmentLive`), deleted in a `finally`. The `trac-convert` row passes `--trac-url <.../trac_test>` (the CLI's highest-precedence override; `trac-convert` has no `--instance`) on every CLI call, with `TRAC_URL` also re-pointed by `monkeypatch.setenv` as a second layer, and uses a per-run `Scratch/TracConvertLive<hex8>` page, deleted afterwards. `TRAC_TEST_WIKI_PAGE` no longer has any effect; the ticket-attachment row no longer needs `TRAC_TEST_TICKET_ID` and no longer skips, so it now runs under `--run-live`
  - **Fixed names are per-run** (`uuid4().hex[:8]`), so overlapping live runs cannot race on one object: the write-gate #105 page (`Scratch/Ticket105WriteGate<hex8>`), the #87 milestone (`Ticket87WriteGateLive<hex8>`, with its delete-before dropped since a per-run name cannot pre-exist), the wiki-conventions probe pages (`PageTemplates/Probe<hex8>`, `Probe/WikiConventions<hex8>/Style`; `wiki_conventions` lists templates by the `PageTemplates/` prefix, so a per-run name is found) and both attachment fixture filenames
  - **`WikiStart` in the conventions row is version-checked, not renamed** (the tool reads WikiStart's declaration by design): immediately before the append the row re-reads WikiStart's version and its content at that version, skips when the content differs from what the row first read (another run is mid-row), and appends with `put_wiki_page(version=<that version>)`, so a write that lands in between is refused by the server-side check (`wiki.putPageIfVersion`; where the plugin is absent the client's own precheck makes the same check, with the narrow race its docstring describes) and the row skips. The restore is made at the version the append produced and, when WikiStart changed since, reports a cleanup error ("WikiStart changed under the run; left as found"). A skip no longer swallows a cleanup error: the row cleans up first and then fails if cleanup failed, else skips. A cleanup delete that fails is now reported (the row fails, naming the object left behind) instead of swallowed, in the three rows above and the write-gate #87 and #105 fixtures
  - **A runtime guard, live-only** (it replaces an AST scan of the test source, which any `"/trac_test"` string fooled and which could not see `bootstrap_config()`, unmarked helpers or CLI calls): under `--run-live`, an autouse fixture in `tests/conftest.py` wraps `TracClient._rpc_call`, the one method that posts an XML-RPC request (`_rpc_request`, the `system.multicall` batches, `wiki.putPageIfVersion`, the attachment methods and `get_server_time` all go through it). For a `pytest.mark.live` test, a write (a method starting with put, delete, create, update, edit or reply: pages, attachments, tickets, comments, milestones, components, enums; or a `system.multicall` carrying one) from a client whose URL does not end with `/trac_test` is recorded and refused before anything is sent, and the test errors at teardown listing every recorded violation even if the body swallowed the exception. Reads are unrestricted. It is inactive offline and for unmarked tests, and does not watch HTTP requests made outside `_rpc_call` (the preview GETs). The decision function and the guard on a mocked-transport client are tested offline (`tests/test_live_write_guard.py`)
  - **A stale-leftover sweep**: per-run names removed the self-healing of the old fixed names, so once per live session, before the first live row, a session fixture deletes on `/trac_test` the pages with this branch's per-run prefixes and the `Ticket173AttachmentLive` tickets whose last modification (`lastModified`, `changetime`) is over an hour old, leaving a concurrent run's objects and the exact pre-#173 fixed names alone, and logs each deletion; a sweep error warns and never fails the session. Milestones are not swept: Trac keeps no modification time for a milestone (`ticket.milestone.get` returns name, due, completed and description), so a leftover `Ticket87WriteGateLive<hex8>` is not recognisably stale
- **A `wiki_update` or `wiki_file_push` whose content is byte-identical to the stored page now reports "unchanged" instead of a `validation_error` (Trac /trac_mcp_server #174).** Trac answers "Page not modified" and stores nothing, which is a no-op, but `put_wiki_page` raised a bare `ValueError` that no handler caught, so the dispatcher said "Check parameter values and retry" for a write that had nothing wrong with it. Separately, "not modified" was mapped to `version_conflict` in two places, which was wrong too: it names no version
  - **A typed no-op**: `WikiPageNotModified(ValueError)` (with `page_name`, `version` (the version the caller passed) and `current_version` (the page's version re-read after the no-op, best effort: `None` when the read fails or answers nothing usable, and it never replaces the no-op)) is raised from both write paths (`wiki.putPageIfVersion` and the client-side precheck then `putPage`) with the same message as before, `Page not modified (content identical)`. The match is Trac's own TracError text, `"page not modified"` compared lowercased, not the looser `"not modified"`: a false match is reported to the caller as success, so a plugin fault such as "Plugin refused: not modified by policy" is no longer a no-op (on the plugin path it goes to the usual fault re-raise, on the precheck path it re-raises the Fault). It stays a `ValueError`, so the CLI `convert` code that catches that keeps working
  - **Success-shaped results, no `isError`**: `wiki_update` answers `Wiki page '<page>' unchanged: content identical to version <n>. Trac stored nothing.`, then the write gate's lines as on a stored write. `wiki_file_push` answers `Wiki page '<page>' unchanged (version <n>): content identical to the stored page. Trac stored nothing.` with `structuredContent.action == "unchanged"` (the only place the unchanged outcome is flagged: no separate boolean) and every other key as on a stored push (conversion warnings and gate lines kept). The version `<n>` reported is the re-read current one, which removes the race caveat: identical content stored as v6 by someone else while the caller passed v5 now says 6 (push: including when its probe found no page and would have created it). If the re-read failed the version the caller passed (push: the probe's) is named, and when neither is known the text says "version unknown" (push: `version` is `null`). Both tool descriptions say so, and `docs/reference/mcp-tools.md` documents the outcome and the `unchanged` action value
  - **`wiki_create` and the identical-content race**: when the page was absent at the existence check and another writer created it with byte-identical content before `putPage`, `wiki_create` answers `already_exists` (the same type and shape as a page that exists at its check). The message says the page was created by another writer with identical content after this call's existence check, names the version (the re-read current one, or "unknown"), and says Trac stored nothing; the action says nothing needs doing, since the page already holds this content
  - **"not modified" is no longer a version conflict**: it is dropped from the `Fault` branch of `wiki_update` and from `translate_xmlrpc_error`, so a bare "Page not modified" fault now re-raises in the handler and is a `server_error` in the translator; a fault containing "version" still maps to `version_conflict` in the handler's `Fault` branch and in the translator
  - **Tested**: both client paths raise `WikiPageNotModified` (a `ValueError`, with `page_name`, `version` and `current_version`); the exact `"page not modified"` match (a "not modified by policy" fault is no no-op on either path); `current_version` set from the re-read on both paths, and left `None` by a failing or unusable re-read with the exception still raised; the `wiki_update` and `wiki_file_push` results above, including `current_version` preferred over the caller's version, the push create path reporting the re-read version, "version unknown", and a real `TracClient` with the plugin's fault; `wiki_create`'s race answer (with a known and an unknown version); the translator and the handler's `Fault` branch on a plain "Page not modified" fault. A live row on `/trac_test` (`TestIdenticalWikiWriteLive` in `tests/test_mcp/tools/test_wiki_not_modified_live.py`, per-run scratch page) pushes byte-identical content through both handlers and checks the version does not move: run against /trac_test: 2 passed. Seeded: with the precheck path's raise put back to a bare `ValueError`, its tests failed; with the re-read removed (and the handlers reading only the caller's version) the `current_version` tests failed; with `wiki_create` not catching the no-op, its race tests failed; restored, they pass
  - **Filed, not fixed here**: on the precheck path a putPage fault containing "version" becomes a bare ValueError, so it surfaces as validation_error, not version_conflict (pre-existing; filed as #185)
- **GitHub CI went red on `master` from ruff version drift (Trac /trac_mcp_server #184).** The `dev` extras asked for `ruff>=0.15.0`, so CI installed the newest release (0.17.4) while the shared `.venv` ran 0.16.5, and the two format a trailing-comment assignment in `tests/test_client_batched_multicall.py` differently. A green local `ruff format --check` therefore did not predict CI. The pin is now exact, `ruff==0.17.4` (and `constraints/deploy.txt`, which pins the dev tools too, moves from 0.15.1 to match), so raising it is a deliberate change, and the line is rewritten so that both versions agree on it
- **The wiki-search cache refresh no longer drops a whole batch of pages when a `system.multicall` reply is malformed (Trac /trac_mcp_server #172).** `get_tickets` and the wiki page fetch each carried their own copy of the batch-then-fallback loop over `system.multicall`, and the copies disagreed on one case: a reply that is not a list of the expected length. The ticket batch re-read it one call per id; the wiki batch returned `{}`, so up to 50 pages silently dropped out of the refresh (and a cached copy of each was discarded as unreadable)
  - **One loop**: `TracClient._batched_multicall(items, batch_size, calls_for, decode, fetch_singly)` now holds the rules for both callers: no items, no RPC; a malformed-length reply sends that batch to single reads; an entry fault leaves its item absent with no retry; a missing-method fault is remembered and the rest goes singly; any other fault or exception sends just that batch singly and batching goes on. `calls_for` may return a different number of entries per item (the wiki asks two, `getPageInfo` then `getPage`, in that order). `_fetch_ticket_batch` and `_fetch_wiki_batch` are removed; `get_tickets` keeps its de-duplication and contract
  - **The one behaviour change** is the wiki's malformed-length case, which now reads that batch page by page, as tickets already did. Tested against both paths with the same fakes (`tests/test_client_batched_multicall.py`); on the old code the wiki cases failed, and with the wiki's `return {}` seeded back into the helper they fail again
- **Two Trac lists that only a blank line separates stay two lists on the round trip (Trac /trac_mcp_server #177).** Trac ends a list at every blank line (#143), so ` * a` / blank / ` * c` is two `<ul>` and ` 1. a` / blank / ` 1. c` two `<ol>`, but the read leg passed the blank line through and CommonMark read ONE loose list; the write leg then wrote ` 1. a` / ` 2. c` (a single list, `c` renumbered) or ` * a` / ` * c`. Measured on /trac_test on 2026-11-07 (#177 comment 1, P1-P6): after any blank line the next marker opens a new top-level list, at any indent
  - **Read leg only**: a new `_separate_adjacent_lists` pass at the end of `_convert_lists` switches the marker character of a top-level item that follows a blank line and would be a sibling of an open list of the same kind and character (bullets alternate `-` and `*`, ordered lists `.` and `)`), and of every later top-level item of the same Trac list. CommonMark starts a new list when the character changes, so no separator is needed, and the write leg already writes two adjacent lists apart with their start numbers (#150), so `markdown_to_tracwiki.py` is untouched. ` 1. a` / ` 1. b` / blank / ` 1. c` / ` 1. d` / blank / ` 1. e` reads `.`, `)`, `.`; ` 3. a` / blank / ` 7. c` reads ` 3. a` / blank / ` 7) c` and comes back as ` 3. a` / blank / ` 7. c`
  - **Unchanged on purpose**: nested items (the one-line form ` * * x` switches only its first marker), a list after a paragraph, a bullet list followed by an ordered one (or the reverse), and a list after a line that ends the Markdown list (a non-marker line left of the open list's text column with a blank line before it, such as a paragraph or a code block at column 0, or one that interrupts a paragraph: a heading, `>`, a fence, a rule), which keep `master`'s bytes. A plain line left of the text column with no blank line before it (` * a` / `  text`, or a `||` table row) is a lazy continuation of the item's paragraph, Trac keeps it in the item, and the list stays open, so the next list after a blank line still switches (Trac starts a new list after the blank line anyway). P4 (a marker after the blank line at or past the previous item's text column, which CommonMark nests in the item) is a different defect and stays as it was (#183)
  - **What counts as a blank line, and as a marker**: only an EMPTY line separates two Trac lists for this pass. The read leg leaves a whitespace-only line for a `[[BR]]` alone on a continuation line and for a macro dropped with `unknown_macros="drop"`; that is item content, so ` * a` / `   [[BR]]` / ` * b` stays one list (it split into two before this was fixed in review). A raw whitespace-only blank line or CRLF input (`\r` is not empty) therefore keeps `master`'s merged bytes. A marker at column 4 or more, or with a tab in its indentation, is indented code to CommonMark and is neither switched nor counted as a list item (` 10. a` / blank / `    10. b` would otherwise rewrite the text inside the code block)
  - **Trade-off, accepted**: the read leg now emits `)` ordered delimiters and alternating `-`/`*` bullets in the Markdown it returns. That is CommonMark, and our own write leg (mistune) reads it, but Python-Markdown does not read `7) c` as a list item and markdownlint flags it (MD004, MD029)
  - **Tested**: exact read-leg rows for P1, P2, P3, P5, P6, tight items after the blank and the one-line nested form; unchanged rows pinned to `master`'s output (paragraph between, bullet then ordered, P4, a single loose Markdown list through the write leg); `tw -> md -> tw` rows that keep the lists apart with their start numbers; second-cycle stability; and a `--run-live` row comparing the source's and the round trip's `<ul>`/`<ol>` structure with `start` attributes on /trac_test
- **Text after a nested list, at a column between the outer item's marker and the nested one's, now stays in the OUTER item on both legs (Trac /trac_mcp_server #176).** Measured on /trac_test on 2026-11-07 (#176 comment 1, cases C1-C10, Q1, D1, N1): in ` * a\n   * b\n  text col2 between\n * c\n` the text line closes `b` and continues `a`, rendering inside `a`'s `<li>` after the nested list, with `c` in the same list; a line at a middle item's marker column in a three-level list continues the middle item. It is the only TracWiki form for "more of the outer item after its nested list". The read leg left the line in place, so CommonMark read it as a lazy continuation of `b`; the write leg wrote a paragraph after a nested list below a blank line, which ended the Trac list (the #125 split). #149 left the shape on master's bytes because only the two legs together fix it
  - **Read leg**: `_separate_list_from_trailing_paragraph` truncates the stack to the continued outer item, puts a blank line before the line and moves it to that item's Markdown text column, so Markdown keeps it in the item (the item becomes loose, #145's accepted trade-off). #149's gates all apply: no blank line since the innermost marker, `_marker_column_rule_applies` for the innermost marker line and for the target item's, and the line is not a list interrupter. Two more gates, found in review: the nested list must not sit left of the outer item's Markdown text column (an ordered outer item, #179: the moved line became an indented code block), and the line must not start like a Markdown list item (`+ x`, `1) x`, a bare `-`: it would become a nested Markdown item). When one fails the line keeps master's pop and bytes. A second line of the same paragraph stays where it is (a lazy continuation). `_list_stack_step` truncates to the continued item instead of popping: only a line AT a middle item's marker column changes (the middle item stays open)
  - **Write leg**: a new `_item_block_separator` value `"between"`: a paragraph run directly after a nested list is written with no blank line, its FIRST line at the item's marker column + 1 (always free: the nested marker is at +2; that line closes the nested list) and every later line at the item's continuation indent as any continuation line is (#75; measured C6: both paragraphs stay in the outer item), and the item is innermost again afterwards, so a code block, citation, table or paragraph that follows takes the form it takes in an item with no nested list (C10, Q1, D1, N1). Refused, keeping today's blank line, for a run with a passthrough line, a blank line of its own (a non-paragraph child) or a first line that opens a block in Trac (`1\.`, `\-`, `\>`). `find_list_item_splits` no longer reports a between paragraph and resets as the writer does, deciding on the run's RENDERED TracWiki through the writer's own renderer method and `_item_block_separator` call (a first line that opens with inline markup, such as `` `* x` c ``, is between, not a finding); a code block, quote, table or refused run directly after a nested list is still reported. The writer's run grouping is now one helper, `_item_runs`, used by the renderer and the finder
  - **Not in this change**: an ordered outer item (C2, C3, C7, N1: its nested list sits left of the Markdown text column, #179) and a wide gap (C8, #178)
  - **Why only the first line is at marker + 1**: the read leg turns ` [[BR]][[BR]]` into a blank line, and a line at marker + 1 after that blank line reads as outside the item (the same happens on master for a plain item), so a between run's second paragraph at marker + 1 split the list on a second cycle. At the continuation indent it stays in the item, and the round trip is byte-stable over two cycles (C6)
  - **Tested**: tests/test_converter_between_column_continuation.py: exact read-leg rows for C1, C4, C5, C6, C9 and C10 and the gate refusals, `tw -> md -> tw` rows for C1, C4, C5, C6, C9, C10, Q1 and D1, write-leg rows for the new form, each refusal and each block after a between run, finder rows, and a `--run-live` row on /trac_test comparing the source's and the round trip's `<li>` structure for C1, C4, C9 and C10. Rows in #112, #143, #149 and #125's tests that pinned the old blank-line form now pin the new one
- **A session close no longer logs an ERROR on MCP SDK 1.30.0 (Trac #169).** With `mcp==1.30.0` on the server side every streamable-http session close logged `Error in standalone SSE writer` at ERROR, from `mcp.server.streamable_http`, with an `anyio.ClosedResourceError` traceback. The client's DELETE terminates the transport and closes the stream the standalone GET writer is iterating; 1.30.0 moved the GET handler's cleanup into a `finally`, so the writer reports that close. The session ends normally, 1.29.1 does not log it, and a fresh install of the `<2.0.0` pin resolves to 1.30.0
  - **`build_http_app` installs a `logging.Filter` on that logger**, once however many times it runs. It matches only that logger, that message and a `ClosedResourceError` in `exc_info`, and demotes the record to DEBUG (dropped unless the logger is enabled for DEBUG); every other record from the logger, including the same message with another exception, is unchanged. Remove it once the SDK stops logging this
  - **Tested**: unit tests of the filter on synthetic records, an install test, and a real-substrate test over real uvicorn and the SDK client that closes a session and asserts no ERROR from that logger (it passes trivially on 1.29.1 and guards 1.30.0)
- **Six low-severity follow-ups from the re-review of GitHub PR 133 (Trac /trac_mcp_server #168).** Tests, one comment, one dead branch and the docs; no change to what `list_instances` or `ping` report
  - **`list_instances`'s client-first rule is pinned**: a new test gives the client and the registry opposite `write_gate` values, in both directions, and requires the text and `structuredContent` to follow the client (seeded by reading `registry.write_gate` first: both directions fail). `test_153_reads_the_daemon_setting_not_the_resolved_client`, whose name and docstring said the opposite and which only covers a call with no client, is now `test_153_without_a_client_reads_the_registry_setting`. `test_ping_and_list_instances_share_the_line` now checks `list_instances`'s text as well as ping's
  - **`_handle_list_instances` returns "Instance registry not initialized" early when there is neither a client nor a registry**, so the `else: write_gate = True` that read as fail-open (and was never reported) is gone. A client with a bearer token and no registry still gets the single-endpoint result, and a non-OIDC client with no registry still gets the error. The `server.py` comment that said `list_instances` "reads the registry, not the client" now states the rule: the client when there is one, else the registry
  - **`docs/reference/mcp-tools.md` documents both forms of the `write_gate:` line**, including the ` -- OFF on this daemon: writes are NOT link-checked` suffix `ping` and `list_instances` print with the gate off
- **Startup lines print once under http: the lifespan and the version warning go through one note helper (Trac /trac_mcp_server #167).** #164 fixed the `build_registry` lines only. `server_lifespan` still logged each startup line and printed it raw as well, and `main()` logged the version-mismatch warning and wrote it to stderr, so under http (and cli, and stdio with the log file disabled) the root logger's stderr handler showed each of them twice, once timestamped and once raw
  - **One helper, in `logger.py`**: `startup_note(logger, message, *args, level, printed, print_fn)` always logs, and prints only when `logging_reaches_stderr(logger, level)` is false (stdio with a log file). `printed` is the fallback text, a string or a list printed one per line, so stdio keeps its own wording and indentation; by default it is the message with a `Warning: ` prefix at WARNING and above. `server.py`'s `_startup_note` is now a thin wrapper over it and its private `_logging_reaches_stderr` is gone
  - **Lines that had no log line now have one**: `Parallel requests`, `Configured instances`, `Server ready` and the shutdown line (and the two error paths, whose `ERROR`-level log line now carries the credentials hint itself, `Configuration error: <e>. Ensure TRAC_URL, TRAC_USERNAME, TRAC_PASSWORD are set.` and `Failed to connect to Trac: <e>. Check TRAC_URL, TRAC_USERNAME, TRAC_PASSWORD.`, so under http the operator still sees it, once), so under http every startup line appears exactly once. Under stdio with a log file the stderr text is byte-identical to before, in the same order. The version check moved into `_report_version(is_consistent, message)` in `server.py`
  - **Tested**: under a `StreamHandler(sys.stderr)` at INFO, `server_lifespan` shows each of its nine startup lines exactly once, and the connection-failure and config-error paths show their message once; under a `FileHandler` at WARNING, stderr equals the previous sequence exactly (success, connection failure, config error); `logging_reaches_stderr` and `startup_note` on a dedicated logger (stderr handler, file handler, stdout handler, level below the handler's or the logger's, parent handler, replaced stream; `printed` as str and list, the `Warning:` default); `_report_version` warns once under an http-shaped handler and once on stderr with only a file handler. **Seeded**: against the unchanged lifespan, the three http-shaped lifespan tests failed on lines shown twice; with `_report_version` restored to the old log-plus-write, `test_http_shape_warns_once` failed
- **`tracwiki_to_markdown` ends a list at a line indented past the item's bound (Trac /trac_mcp_server #175).** Trac 1.6 continues the innermost list item only while an indented, non-marker line is at most the marker column + 2 (bullet) or + 3 (digits-only ordered item) wide; past that the list ends and the line renders as nested blockquotes (` * a` / `    col4` / ` * c` is a list, two blockquotes, a new list; measured on /trac_test on 2026-11-07). The read leg applied only the lower side of the rule (#149), so it kept such a line in the item, and the write leg put it back at the item's text column, inside the list: the round trip moved the line into the list. It now ends the list there and carries the indented run verbatim through the `tracwiki-unconverted` fallback (from the line up to the next list marker, with the fallback's warning), set off by a blank line each side. The write leg already writes a fallback fence after a list as blank line / original bytes / blank line, which closes the list and the quote and keeps Trac's tabstops, so the round trip renders as the source (` * a` / `    col4` / ` * c` comes back as ` * a`, a blank line, `    col4`, a blank line, ` * c`). The Markdown view shows a fenced block, not a `>` quote; no second depth model is needed. A line exactly at the bound still continues the item. Only indented quote text past the bound is fenced: table rows and headings past it keep master's reading, as does a run holding a `{{{`/`}}}` line. A `{{{ }}}` block at any indent stays in the open item (Trac joins a block to the innermost item, #144), so text after it continues the item (measured case K1 on /trac_test, ` * a` / `   * b` / ` {{{` / ` x` / ` }}}` / `     more` / ` * c` renders `more` inside item b, which master fenced). The bound applies to raw TracWiki only, not to text the converter has already rewritten, so an item numbered 10 or more is unaffected there. Recall gate over the cached corpus of both stores (2,075 documents, read leg and round trip compared with master's): only `trac_mcp_server` ticket 25 changes. Its items 10 to 12 (section C) now read as list items with the past-bound continuations fenced between them, and its round trip renders the same list, quote and code structure as the source (checked on `/trac_mcp_server` with `wikiToHtml`)
- **`markdown_to_tracwiki` keeps an ordered list's start number (Trac /trac_mcp_server #150).** The write leg numbered every ordered list from 1, ignoring the `start` attribute mistune puts on the list token, so ` 5. a` / ` 6. b` (Trac renders `<ol start="5">`, and the read leg keeps ` 5.`) came back as ` 1.` / ` 2.`. It now counts from `start`. Trac takes only the first item's number and counts on from it (measured on `/trac_test`: ` 5.` / ` 7.` / ` 9.` and ` 3.` / ` 1.` both count up from the first), so later numbers are emitted consecutively, as Trac shows them. A nested list keeps its own start in the TracWiki (its Markdown view is #180), and `0.` stays `0.`. Recall gate over the cached corpus of both stores (2,075 documents): 41 documents' round trips change, only in marker numbers, and each now numbers its lists as the source renders. Ticket #25's steps 5 to 8 and its section C (`10.` to `12.`) now come back byte for byte. No document's Markdown changes
- **`ticket_update`'s `reply_to` quote keeps the quoted comment's leading whitespace (Trac /trac_mcp_server #160).** The quote was built from `strip().split("\n")`, so the first line lost its indent while the rest kept theirs (a quoted ` * a` / ` * b` list became `> * a` / `>  * b`), and a CRLF comment left a `\r` on every quoted line but the last. It now drops trailing whitespace only and splits on LF or CRLF, the rule `ticket_changelog` follows since #155, so the leading blank lines of a quoted comment are kept too. The `tracrpc_comment` plugin's `ticket_comment_reply` quote is built server-side, keeps leading whitespace and was never affected; this path still differs from it in dropping trailing whitespace-only lines. Measured on /trac_test: the old quote rendered a two-item list as `b` nested under `a`, and the new one renders one flat list inside the citation. Seeded: the new test failed on the old `strip().split("\n")` before the fix
- **A line at the innermost list item's marker column now continues that item on the read leg, as Trac renders it (Trac /trac_mcp_server #149).** ` 1. a\n b\n 1. c` is one list with `b` in item `a` (measured), but the read leg's list walks popped every open item whose marker was at or past the line's column and ended the list when none was left, so `b` came out at column 0 after a blank line and the list split.
  - **Trac's rule** (measured on /trac_test 2026-11-07, and Trac 1.6's `Formatter._indent_formatter`): for an indented non-blank non-marker line `d` columns wide, with open marker columns `m_0 < ... < m_k`, `d >= m_k` continues the innermost item; `0 < d < m_k` and `d > m_0` closes the items whose marker is past `d` and continues the deepest one left; otherwise (`d <= m_0` while a deeper item is open, or `d == 0`) the list ends. It is one helper, `_continued_item`, and only its innermost answer changes bytes: `_list_stack_step` (so `_open_list_item`, #144's block placement and #146's bare-table check), `_separate_list_from_trailing_paragraph` and `_continues_item_after_block` (#143 F3) treat any other answer as before
  - **What changed**: ` 1. a\n b at marker column\n 1. c\n` reads as ` 1. a\n    b at marker column\n 1. c\n` (no blank line, the line at the item's text column); ` * a\n   * b\n   text\n * c\n` puts `text` at b's text column; ` 1. d\n{{{\nx\n}}}\n e at marker column\n 1. f\n` keeps `e` in d's item after the block. A line the separator moves is left alone when it is one of CommonMark's list-interrupting shapes, as for #145
  - **The gate** (`_marker_column_rule_applies`, used at all three sites): the marker-column rule applies only when the innermost item's marker is a bullet or a digits-only ordered marker, neither the line nor the marker line has a tab in its indentation, and the line is not one of CommonMark's list interrupters (heading, `>`, fence, rule); and, in `_separate_list_from_trailing_paragraph` only, no blank line has passed since the innermost item's marker line (that pass keeps its stack across blank lines on purpose, #52/#53, so a line two below a blank one was judged against items Trac had already closed). When any condition fails, the site pops every entry at or past the line's column, as on `master` (cf92e87), byte for byte. Code review of the first #149 branch found regressions outside it: a lettered list's code body got a blank line and a continuation left the list (` a. x\n {{{\n y\n }}}\n a. z\n` and the same with ` b` after a two-line body), a tab-indented list changed (`\t* a\n\tb\n\t* c\n`), a line after a `>` line at the marker column moved out of the list (` * a\n > q\n b\n * c\n`), and a line below a blank one was re-indented (` * a\n   * b\n\n   c\n d\n * e\n`)
  - **Unchanged on purpose**: (1) a line that continues a NON-innermost item, closing deeper ones (` * a\n    * b\n  text col2 between\n * c\n`, and a line at a middle marker's column in a three-level list), reads and round-trips byte for byte as on `master`. The helper's answer is right (the line continues `a`), but the Markdown for it is text after a nested list, which the write leg can only write below a blank line, ending the Trac list (the #125 split); `master` keeps the list whole with the text in the inner item. A recall sweep found 4 real documents in this shape, so both legs change together in #176. (2) a line deeper than the innermost marker, (3) a line at or left of the outermost marker while a deeper item is open (` * a\n   * b\n text at outer col\n * c\n`, and `   * a\n text left of marker\n   * c\n`: the list ends), (4) marker lines anywhere, `_enclosing_list_marker` (#142's div unwrap, #144), (5) a line directly after a blank line in the separator pass (Trac ends the list at the blank; the pass keeps its pre-#149 reading, which #144's non-1-marker row depends on). (6) Trac's UPPER bound (marker + 2 for a bullet, + 3 for an ordered item: a deeper line leaves the list) is not applied and is its own ticket; the gate's shapes above are unchanged on purpose too: lettered/roman lists stay on the #74/#89 fallback path, tab-indented lists are a declined shape, an interrupter line keeps #145's reading, and a line below a blank line keeps master's reading (these read and round-trip as on `master`)
  - **Tested**: unit tests of `_continued_item` for each measured case (C and the three-level middle column included) and of `_list_stack_step`'s stack; exact read-leg strings for A, B, G, J and the block case, and for the lines that must not change (D, F, C, the middle column, a line closing deeper items after a block: pinned to `master`'s bytes, C and the middle column also on the round trip); a mistune parse of the Markdown (the text is in `a`'s item for A, in `b`'s for B); round trips (the line comes back inside the continued item's marker + 1 .. text column, the list is not split, a second cycle is byte-stable) for A, B, G and J; one live row on /trac_test comparing the `<li>` nesting of the source's render and the round trip's, for A, B, G and J. A differential run of 5942 distinct random shapes (bullet, numeric and lettered markers, tabs, interrupter lines, blank lines) against `master`, read leg and round trip: 270 differed, every one with a non-marker line at an open marker's column, and none of the 1781 shapes without a space-indented bullet or numeric marker differed. No existing test's bytes changed. Added for the gate: F1, F2, F3, F5 and F6 pinned to `master`'s exact read-leg bytes and round-trip bytes (`tests/test_converter_list_marker_column.py`), and two rows that keep the improved reading inside the gate (` 1. x\n {{{\n y\n z\n }}}\n b\n` and ` * x\n {{{\n y\n }}}\n b\n * c\n`: `b` stays in the item). `tests/test_converter_code_in_list.py` is back to `master`'s bytes (the TAB-indented marker line row no longer needs a change)
  - **Seeded**: with `_continued_item` reverted to the old pop-at-or-past rule, the helper's A, B and middle-column unit tests, the `_list_stack_step` A and B rows, the A, B and block read-leg rows, the A structural test and the A and block round trips failed. With the gate predicate returning True at every site, F1, F2, F3 and F5 failed (read leg and round trip) and #144's `TAB-indented marker line` row failed; with the separator's blank-line flag ignored, F6 (read leg and round trip), the blank-line row and #144's non-1 ordered row failed
- **The write-gate live rows are parallel-safe, and the CLI live round-trip no longer fails with `Page not modified (content identical)` (Trac /trac_mcp_server #159).** Trac refuses a save that changes nothing, and both tests wrote a fixed body to the shared scratch page (`TRAC_TEST_WIKI_PAGE`), so a parallel run's identical last write failed the save. `TestWriteGateLive` now uses its own per-run page (`Scratch/Ticket159WriteGate<uuid8>`) on `/trac_test`, created before and deleted after each row, so parallel runs cannot collide on identical content, version conflicts or the read-back. The seed step of `test_live_round_trip_check_from_edit_to` appends a per-run `Run nonce <uuid> <UTC stamp>` line, which stops the "content identical" failure. That test's shared page can still race between its step 5 write and step 6 read when runs overlap; that is tracked in #173
- **Bare `||` rows under a list item no longer read back as a table inside the item, and the #143 blank line no longer ends a list (Trac /trac_mcp_server #146, #171).** Trac renders such rows outside the list, and `tracwiki_to_markdown` dropped their indentation to say so, but wrote no blank line between the item text and the table. A Markdown reader (GFM; mistune with `table_in_list`) takes the table for part of the item, and `markdown_to_tracwiki` then wrote `{{{#!div ... }}}` inside it, moving the table into the list
  - **What changed**: `_convert_tables` decides in one forward pass over the INPUT lines, with a list-item stack (`_list_stack_step`, the per-line step now shared with `_open_list_item`) per `{{{` container depth. A bare row group gets a blank line (no indent) before it when its first row follows a non-blank line and an item is open on the current stack; a bare group then ends the list (Trac does, measured in #142). `" * a\n   ||=x=||=y=||\n   ||1||2||\n * d\n"` reads as `" - a\n\n| x | y |\n|---|---|\n| 1 | 2 |\n - d\n"`, the table is outside the list in Markdown, and writing it back gives no div and unindented rows. The same holds after a continuation line, a nested item and an ordered item. Two #142 tests that pinned the old bytes were updated (bare rows in an item, bare rows after a nested item)
  - **Containers**: a `{{{` / `{{{ #!processor args` line opens one (`_passthrough_containers`, one linear stack pass; a closer with no opener is an ordinary line). An opener is a child of the item above it, or ends it by column, and starts an empty stack, so rows inside `{{{#!div class=x` under an item (with or without text before them) are not bare and keep their `master` bytes: a blank line plus the block's column-0 closer would end the list on the write leg. A block that wraps the whole list, a blank line inside an already-closed block, and `{{{ some text` (not an opener) no longer hide the item. A `{{{x}}}` on the item line and a block closed above the rows do not exempt them either
  - **Second review (R1, R2, R5)**: (R1) whitespace between `{{{` and `#!` is allowed in an opener, as on Trac's renderer (measured on `/trac_test`, 2026-11-07: ` * a\n   {{{ #!div class=x\n   ||1||\n   }}}\n * d\n` renders the div with its table inside the item and the list goes on with `d`), so that input reads back with no blank line added and the div stays under the item on the write leg. (R2) an opener that never gets a closer is a container that runs to the end of the document (Trac closes open blocks there; measured: ` * e\n   {{{#!div class=y\n   ||2||\n * f\n` renders div y inside e's item and the div swallows ` * f`), so its rows are not bare and the stack it pushes is never popped; a stray closer with no opener stays an ordinary line. (R5) the #143 rule that puts a blank line before item text right after a table now covers a bare group as well as a div group: ` * a\n   ||1||\n   more\n * d\n` reads as ` - a\n\n| 1 |\n|---|\n\n   more\n - d\n`, where GFM would otherwise take `more` for one more table row (spec example 202). The closer of a container is not text and gets no blank line, so rows that end a `{{{#!div class=x` block keep their `master` bytes. Where `more` lands on the write leg is not measured: the test only asserts that no table row contains it. `prev_div_end` and `prev_bare_end` became one `prev_group_end`, and the unreachable table-row skip in `_passthrough_containers` is gone
  - **#171**: the stack is fed input lines only, never lines appended to `result`, so the blank line `_convert_tables` puts after a div table (#143) no longer ends the list: a bare group after a div table and a line of item text gets its blank line too. The earlier backward walk re-scanned the blank-free run above every group (quadratic: 2000 `text`/row pairs took about 3 s); the forward pass is linear
  - **Also fixed**: a bare group directly followed by an unwrapped div group no longer merges with it into one Markdown table (a blank line, no indent, goes between them, as for two div groups). Where the div table belongs after the bare rows ended the list is not measured, and is unchanged
  - **Unchanged on purpose, byte for byte**: a table whose line above is already blank (rows at column 0 straight after an item already had one on `master`), a top-level table after a paragraph, after a heading or at the start of the document, a table after flush-left text that closed the list, and a table that came from a list item's `{{{#!div}}}` (#142: it stays in the item). Rows directly after an unwrapped div's rows are one group with it, so they stay in the div group
  - **Tested**: exact read-leg strings for each shape above, the negatives with their `master` output hard-coded, a mistune parse of the read-leg Markdown (the table is a top-level block, not a child of the item), the ticket's input round-tripped (no `{{{#!div`, rows unindented, the second read -> write cycle byte-stable), the round trips of the container and #171 cases (no new bare `{{{#!div` around the rows, the `y` table outside the list, no `||---||` row after the F5 merge fix), the R1, R2 and R5 inputs with exact read-leg strings (R1 and R2 as `master`'s bytes) and their round trips, and a call-counting linearity check (`_list_stack_step` monkeypatched with a counting wrapper over a blank-free run of 4000 `text`/row pairs against 1000: the count must stay within `4 * count(1000) + 16` and within one step per input line; it is 1001 and 4001, and grows about 16x for 4x the input when quadratic; no wall-clock time is read)
  - **Seeded**: with the blank-line decision disabled, 15 tests failed (every positive, the structure and round-trip checks, the inline and closed-block non-exemptions, F2 to F5, #171, and the two updated #142 rows); the negatives and the passthrough-exemption rows passed. With the old backward walk (`b026e25`) restored, the linearity test and the F2, F3, F4, F5 and #171 tests failed; everything else passed. For the second review, each fix was reverted in turn and the suite run: without R1's regex change `test_r1_space_between_the_braces_and_the_processor_is_an_opener` failed, without R2's `open_at.update(openers)` `test_r2_an_opener_with_no_closer_runs_to_the_end_of_the_document` failed, and with the #143 rule limited to div groups again `test_r5_text_right_after_a_bare_group_gets_a_blank_line` failed (each alone, nothing else); without the closer exemption the two passthrough-block rows and R1 failed; without the mark-branch blank the F5 test and the two adjacent-div tests in `test_converter_table_in_list.py` failed
- **Each startup filter line prints once under http, and a tool the registry does not serve is `unknown_tool` whatever `instance` says (Trac /trac_mcp_server #164).** Two defects in the tool filters from #112
  - **Startup lines were doubled.** `build_registry` logged each `Permissions file` / `Read-only mode` / `Disabled tools` / `Tools enabled` line and also printed it on stderr, and logged then printed the two warnings (unknown disabled names, a disabled `list_instances`) itself. Under http, cli, and stdio with the log file disabled, the root logger already writes to stderr, so every line showed twice, once formatted and once raw. The print is now only the fallback, used when no handler the logger reaches writes to the current stderr (stdio with a log file, where stderr is the only place the operator sees the line; with no handler at all, logging's `lastResort` already writes a warning there, but not an INFO line); a printed warning keeps its `Warning: ` prefix, so its wording is now `Warning: Disabled tools not found (ignored): ...`. The `_startup_note` docstring no longer says the logger writes only the log file
  - **A hidden tool answered with an instance error.** `handle_call_tool` resolved the client before looking the tool up, so a tool hidden by `--read-only`, `disabled_tools` or permissions, or a name that does not exist, called with an `instance` outside the caller's allowlist got `instance_not_allowed` (under OIDC, the `Authorization` header hint) instead of `unknown_tool`. New `ToolRegistry.has_tool(name)`; the lookup now comes first, resolves no client and carries no instance annotation, since nothing was reached. The `except ValueError` around `call_tool` is gone: `call_tool` raises `ValueError` only for a name it does not serve, which `has_tool` has already ruled out, and it handles a handler's own `ValueError` itself
  - **`docs/reference/tool-architecture.md`**'s `ToolRegistry` section said "two independent, combinable filters" and "BOTH", and its constructor examples left out `disabled_tools`. It now names the three filters, says a spec must pass all three, shows `disabled_tools=frozenset({...})` and links [Disabling Individual Tools](docs/reference/tool-architecture.md#disabling-individual-tools)
  - **Tested**: with `setup_logging(mode="http")` and with stdio, `build_registry` over read-only, one real, one unknown and the `list_instances` disabled names prints each of its five lines exactly once (stdio with a log file still prints them); a read-only-hidden tool on a foreign instance is `unknown_tool`, with no `instance` annotation and no client resolved, while a served tool there is still `instance_not_allowed`; an unknown name does not call `_resolve_client`, and under OIDC with no `Authorization` header it is `unknown_tool`. **Seeded**: against the unchanged `_startup_note`, the http and the stdio-without-file tests failed on a line printed twice; against the unchanged dispatcher, the four unknown-tool tests failed
- **`ticket_changelog` says `comment N has no text` for an empty comment entry, not `comment added`.** Trac records a numbered, empty `comment` entry for a change made without text and for a comment edited down to empty; `comment added` read as a comment whose text `ticket_get` withholds. The number comes from `TracClient.parse_comment_number`, so a reply's `2.5` reads as comment 5
- **The write gate now diagnoses a local short link whose target swallowed a suffix (`ticket:12's`, `wiki:WikiStart's`) and flags `auto_pm:#3/#4`, and its `#N` suggestions no longer point at the wrong project (Trac /trac_mcp_server #163).** Trac's short-link grammar runs a target through `'`, `-` or `/`, so `ticket:12's` renders a dead `missing ticket` anchor although ticket 12 exists. The gate refused it as `missing_local_target` ("does not exist on this instance") with no fix, so the obvious retry changed the target instead of ending the link before the suffix. Separately, `auto_pm:#3/#4` was silent (or, on a same-host prefix, misdiagnosed as a missing `wiki:` realm), and the bracket suggestion for `auto_pm:#3-#5` left the `#5` bare, which links THIS instance's ticket 5.
  - **(a) New blocking code `local_target_captured_tail`** (`error`, in `BLOCKING_CODES` with a ruling comment) replaces `missing_local_target` for a `missing` anchor whose text is exactly an unlabelled short link. `ticket:`: the text is `ticket:<digits><tail>` and the tail's first character is not alphanumeric, `_`, `#` or `?`; the suggestion is `#12's`. `wiki:`: the text is `wiki:` plus the page name from the href, and the name holds `'` followed by a letter after a non-empty stem; the suggestion is `[[WikiStart]]'s` (`WikiStart's/Sub` gives `[[WikiStart]]'s/Sub`). Evidence carries `href`, `text`, `token`, `resolved_as` and `suggestion`. Only the final `missing_local_target` append changed: the incidental-autolink and attachment budgets run first, as before
  - **(b) `_INTERTRAC_HREF_TARGET_RE` captures the whole target** after `/intertrac/` up to `?` or the end. The old pattern, `/intertrac/([^/?#]+)\Z`, matched NOTHING for `.../intertrac/%233/%234`: the group cannot span the `/` and the `\Z` then fails, so the check never ran. Dropping the `\Z` also means an href that carries a query string is now accepted. `#3/#4` now splits into `#3` and `/#4` and fires `intertrac_target_captured_punctuation`; wiki-realm and realm-less targets do not start with `#N` and are unaffected
  - **One diagnosis (realm)**: `is_missing_realm_href` returns False for a target that starts with `%23`, so `auto_pm:#3/#4` on a local prefix is the captured tail alone, no longer also `missing_intertrac_realm` ("insert `wiki:`"), which would not have fixed it
  - **Suggestion builder**: every `#N` in the tail gets the same prefix bracket. `auto_pm:#3-#5` becomes `[auto_pm:#3 #3]-[auto_pm:#5 #5]`, `auto_pm:#3/#4` becomes `[auto_pm:#3 #3]/[auto_pm:#4 #4]`; a tail with no `#N` is unchanged (`[auto_pm:#87 #87]'s`). The docstring's "verified live" claim now says what was and was not rendered. The `convert_preview` tool description names local `ticket:`/`wiki:` links
  - **Tested**: 12 live-rendered rows (`row163a`-`row163l`, captured with `TracClient.wiki_to_html` on `/trac_mcp_server`) in the table-driven suite; the exact suggestion and evidence per seeded row; the message says "if you meant ticket 12" / "page WikiStart" and never "does not exist"; shape-boundary cases (`ticket:12abc`, `12_x`, `12?x`, `12/13`, apostrophe + digit, empty stem, hyphenated page, labelled anchor, non-ticket/wiki class); `build_warnings` and `build_verify_warnings` agree on every row, including with no paired source (the new code needs none); `auto_pm:#3/#4` with `local_intertrac_bases` set gives the captured tail and NOT `missing_intertrac_realm`; unit tests for `is_missing_realm_href` and `_bracket_form_for_captured_token`; the gate inventory tests; and a live test (`TestConvertPreviewLive.test_captured_tail_suggestions_render_clean_live`) that renders each seeded row through `convert_preview`, substitutes the emitted suggestion for the resolved text and asserts no error-severity warning on the second render. `render_check` is unchanged: it lists `missing` anchors by class, so these anchors still appear under `Missing links`
  - **Seeded**: on unmodified code, 29 of the new offline test ids failed, plus the four subtests of the `%23`-led `is_missing_realm_href` case: the `row163a`-`d` cases of `test_warning_rows_produce_expected_code`; `test_row163_suggestion` for a-e (a-c: no such code; d: silent; e: the old `[auto_pm:#3 #3]-#5`); `test_local_captured_tail_replaces_missing_local_target` (a-c); the evidence, shape-only-message, blocking, `WikiStart's/Sub`, `ticket:12/13` and no-source-verify tests; `test_intertrac_slash_tail_is_captured_not_a_missing_realm`, `..._on_an_off_host_prefix` and `..._evidence`; three `test_bracket_form_for_captured_token` rows; the `%23`-led foreign-dispatcher `is_missing_realm_href` case; `test_section_4_error_column_blocks[local_target_captured_tail]`. The live test failed for rows a-d (no captured-tail finding) on unmodified code and passed for e, whose old suggestion rendered silent while linking the wrong project, which only the exact-suggestion unit test sees
  - **Recall gate** (must not move): rows 61, 62 and 72 keep `intertrac_target_captured_punctuation` and their suggestions (`[auto_pm:#87 #87]'s`, `-ish`); `auto_pm:#3-#5` keeps its code (new suggestion); `ticket:99999#comment:1`, `wiki:Foo-Bar`, `[ticket:12's label]` and `[wiki:WikiStart's]` stay `missing_local_target`; the silent controls `#12's`, `[[WikiStart]]'s` and `[auto_pm:#3 #3]/[auto_pm:#4 #4]` stay silent; row 86's realm-less slashed targets still fire `missing_intertrac_realm`; every pre-existing row keeps its set of codes
  - **Declined**: no probe of the stem (is ticket 12 or `WikiStart` real?). For part (a) severity is unchanged, error to error, so the set of refused writes is the same (given the pragma fix under **Review fixes**) and recall cannot move; only the message and the fix change, so the message says "if you meant" and never asserts the stem exists. A dead stem is caught on the retry: `[[Projects/X]]'s` reports `missing_local_target` for `Projects/X`
  - **Scope of "the set of refused writes is unchanged"**: it holds for part (a) only, and only with review fix 1 below in place. Part (b) newly refuses an off-host `prefix:#N/#M`, which is its point
  - **Review fixes**: from the code review of the first commit, nine in all.
    1. **Pragma regression**: `_allowed_codes` filters by exact code, so an existing `preview-checks: allow missing_local_target` pragma, which used to pass `ticket:12's`, no longer did once the finding was renamed (likewise `allow missing_intertrac_realm` with `auto_pm:#3/#4`). A finding that replaces an older code now carries `evidence["replaces_code"]` and `build_warnings` drops a warning when its `code` OR its `replaces_code` is allowed. `local_target_captured_tail` sets `missing_local_target`; `intertrac_target_captured_punctuation` sets `missing_intertrac_realm` when the captured tail contains `/` (before #163 that shape was `missing_intertrac_realm` on a local base and silent off-host, so the realm pragma reproduces the old pass in both). A `-` tail is not covered by the realm pragma, as before
    2. **`O'Brien`**: `_WIKI_PAGE_TAIL_RE` takes `'` followed by a LOWERCASE ASCII letter only (`'s`, `'t`, `'ll`, `'re`, `'ve`, `'d`), so a missing page `wiki:O'Brien` stays `missing_local_target`
    3. **`%23a/b`**: `is_missing_realm_href` exempts only `%23` followed by a digit (a `#<digit>` ticket token), so `.../intertrac/%23a/b` still reports `missing_intertrac_realm`
    4. **Ticket tail narrowed** to the shapes rendered live: the tail starts with `-` or `/`, or with `'` and a letter. `ticket:12:5` and `ticket:12.5` stay `missing_local_target`
    5. **Wiki fragment/query**: a missing wiki anchor whose href carries a `#` fragment or `?` query is not a captured tail (`wiki:WikiStart's#Sec` stays `missing_local_target`)
    6. **`#N` in tails**: the `#N` tokens are prefix-bracketed only when the WHOLE tail is `(?:[-/]#\d+)+` (the measured `-#5` and `/#4` shapes and chains of them); a tail with `#` in any other shape (`-x#12`, `/#5abc`, `/#x`) gets no suggestion; a tail with no `#` keeps `[token label]tail`
    7. **Wrong mechanism in the comments**: the comment on the href pattern, the `_check_captured_punctuation` docstring and this entry said the old regex "saw only the last segment, `%234`". It matched nothing (`\Z` failed after `%233`); corrected, with the note that dropping `\Z` now accepts an href carrying a query string
    8. **Duplicate regex**: `checks._INTERTRAC_HREF_TARGET_RE` was byte-identical to `targets._INTERTRAC_TARGET_RE`; it is now an import alias of it
    9. **Cleanups**: redundant `\Z` dropped from `_LOCAL_TICKET_TEXT_RE` (fullmatch only); the unquote + `/wiki/` page extraction shared by `_autolinked_page_name` and `_local_captured_tail` is one helper, `_wiki_href_page`; an over-long comment line in `gate.py` rewrapped
  - **Tested** (review fixes): both old-pragma cases pass for each of the local and realm codes, the new-code and own-code pragmas still pass, a `-` tail with only `allow missing_intertrac_realm` is still refused, `replaces_code` present on the slash and local findings and absent on the dash one; `O'Brien` and the `'t`/`'ll`/`'re`/`'ve`/`'d` tails; `%23a/b`; `ticket:12:5`, `12.5`, `12,5`, `12(x`, `12'5` stay missing and `-ish`, `/13`, `'s`, `'x` are captured; `wiki:WikiStart's#Sec` and `?action=x`; the bracket builder for chains, mixed chains and every non-chain `#` shape
  - **Seeded** (review fixes), failing on 1346dcf: `test_old_missing_local_target_pragma_still_passes_a_captured_tail` (ticket and wiki), `test_local_captured_tail_names_the_code_it_replaces`, `test_old_realm_pragma_still_passes_a_slash_tail` (local base and off-host), `test_slash_tail_names_the_code_it_replaces`, `test_wiki_surname_apostrophe_is_a_missing_page_not_a_tail`, `test_ticket_tail_outside_the_rendered_shapes_stays_missing` (four cases), `test_ticket_apostrophe_then_digit_stays_missing`, `test_wiki_fragment_or_query_is_not_a_captured_tail` (both), `test_bracket_form_for_captured_token` (the five non-chain `#` rows), `TestIsMissingRealmHref.test_percent23_page_name_is_still_flagged`
- **`--log-file` no longer has a built-in default, so `LOG_FILE` is honoured and the http daemon stops writing `/tmp/trac-mcp-server.log` (Trac /trac_mcp_server #157).** The argparse default was `/tmp/trac-mcp-server.log`, so `run()` always put `log_file` into the config overrides: on stdio the `LOG_FILE` env var was never consulted, and in http mode (the daemon, a container) a `FileHandler` on that path was always added next to stderr, with no way to turn it off
  - **What changed**: the log file resolves as `--log-file`, then the `LOG_FILE` env var if set (even to empty), then the per-mode default (`/tmp/trac-mcp-server.log` for stdio, none for http/cli); an empty value means no log file. `--log-file` now defaults to `None` and `run()` forwards it when it is not `None`, so `--log-file ""` reaches `setup_logging`. `_resolve_log_file` in `logger.py` implements the order. Stdio with the file disabled logs to stderr in the same format, never stdout (stdout carries JSON-RPC); http/cli add a file only when one resolves. The override dict is built by `config_overrides_from_args` (extracted from `run()`, unchanged otherwise). `cli.md`, `http-transport.md`, `troubleshooting.md`, `deployment.md` and `configuration.md` describe it
  - **Deploy note**: the http daemon no longer writes /tmp/trac-mcp-server.log unless LOG_FILE or --log-file is set; the jail's rc.d log (stderr) is unaffected
  - **Tested**: 13 new tests in `tests/test_logger.py`: stdio default, `LOG_FILE` read, arg beats env, `""` arg and `LOG_FILE=` both log to a single stderr `StreamHandler` with nothing on stdout and the stdio format; http default has no `FileHandler`, `LOG_FILE` adds one, `""` beats a set `LOG_FILE`; the argparse path (`[]` gives no `log_file` key, `["--log-file", ""]` gives `""`, a path gives the path); and one real-filesystem http test (no `basicConfig` mock) that the record lands in the `LOG_FILE` file. Seven fail on the old code
  - **Seeded**: with the argparse default restored to `/tmp/trac-mcp-server.log`, `test_not_given_is_not_forwarded` failed; with `_resolve_log_file` ignoring `LOG_FILE`, `test_stdio_env_var_is_read`, `test_stdio_empty_env_logs_to_stderr`, `test_http_env_var_adds_file` and `test_http_real_file_receives_records` failed; all passed again once restored
  - **Review fixes**: the module-level autouse fixture in `tests/test_logger.py` clears `LOG_FILE` for every test (cli mode now honours it, so an exported `LOG_FILE=/nonexistent/x.log` failed 7 old tests, and a writable one leaked open `FileHandler`s). `main()` turns an `OSError` from `setup_logging` into `ERROR: Cannot open log file <path>: <err>` on stderr and a `RuntimeError`, so `LOG_FILE=/nonexistent/x.log trac-mcp-server --transport http` exits cleanly instead of with a traceback. A whitespace-only `--log-file` or `LOG_FILE` counts as empty (no file); other paths are not stripped. The log format and datefmt are module constants shared by every handler built without a custom formatter, and `--log-file`'s help builds the path from `DEFAULT_MCP_LOG_FILE`. `http-transport.md` and `troubleshooting.md` no longer say stdio "logs to a file only" or that debug output always goes to the log file. Tested: whitespace env (http) and arg (stdio), the unopenable-path error with `setup_logging` patched and with a real missing directory; the four new tests fail on the previous commit
- **A blockquote in a Markdown list item now stays inside the item as Trac's citation syntax (Trac /trac_mcp_server #145).** `markdown_to_tracwiki` kept a blockquote child in the item's text run, so in a tight item its first line was glued onto the item text (`- a` / `  > q` became ` * a  q`), in a loose item the blank line before it ended the Trac list, and a table in the quote came out as ` * a  ||=x=||`
  - **Measured on `/trac_test`**: `> ` lines directly under the item text, at the item's continuation indent and with no blank line before them, render a `<blockquote class="citation">` inside the `<li>`, and the next item stays a sibling. It holds for several lines; a lone `>` line as the paragraph break (one blockquote, two paragraphs); a table (`   > ||=x=||`); a `{{{ }}}` block; a list (`   >  * l1`); a nested quote (`   > > x`); a nested item; an ordered item; item text on the next line without a `>` (it renders after the blockquote, still inside the `<li>`); a code block after the quote and a quote after a code block; and a nested list right after the quote. It fails for an item with empty text (` *` alone is not an item) and for ` * > q` (a literal `>`)
  - **Write leg**: a blockquote child that follows other item content is its own run kind, `quote`, and renders as citation lines (`> ` prefix, a bare `>` for an empty line) at the item's continuation indent, at any depth and in ordered lists. A quote nested in it is `> > x`, not `>   x` (that renders but reads back as a plain paragraph). A list in the quote restarts at depth 0, so it is `> ` plus ` * l1`. `_item_block_separator` gains the kind: a quote is adjacent to the item text, a paragraph run, a code block or a table before it, and a paragraph, code block, table or nested list after it has no blank line either. Two sibling quotes (a blank line apart in Markdown) join with a lone `>` line, which Trac shows as one blockquote with two paragraphs (a documented loss)
  - **Unchanged on purpose, byte for byte**: a quote that is the item's first child (` *   q`; no Trac form exists), a quote after a nested list (Trac attaches an adjacent block to the innermost nested item, #112/#125; `find_list_item_splits` still reports it), a quote after a run with a passed-through TracWiki line (#143 review F1) or one that already holds a blank line (raw HTML, a #63 fallback fence; the list has ended there), the quote or paragraph that follows such a quote, an empty quote, and a top-level blockquote (two-space indent). A paragraph after a citation keeps its blank line when its first line would join the quote (`>`) or open a nested item (a Trac list marker: `*`, `-`, `1.`, `a.`, `ii.`)
  - **A nested item inside a quote follows the first-child rule**: citation form applies to an item's own quote runs only, so `- a` / `  > - > x` gives ` *   x` inside the citation (` * > x` renders the literal text `> x`, measured), not a second `>`. Each quote run is rendered once, in the form its separator chose
  - **Read leg**: plain item text straight after a citation line is a lazy continuation of the quote to CommonMark, though Trac renders it after the blockquote. `tracwiki_to_markdown` now writes a blank line between them (in `_separate_list_from_trailing_paragraph`), but not before the next item, a fence or heading, another `>` line, or at the end. Only when the line sits deeper than the item's marker and no deeper than its text column (measured: Trac keeps it in the item there, and renders a deeper line outside the list); it is re-indented to the text column, the Markdown item's content column. Any other indent is left as before. The blank line makes the Markdown list loose (CommonMark renders every item with `<p>`), the accepted trade-off. The write leg takes it out again, so the round trip is byte-stable. ` * a` / `   > q` / ` * d` already read back as ` - a` / `   > q` / ` - d` and still does
  - **Known limitation, pinned and not fixed here**: a GFM table in the quote is written as `> ||=x=||` rows, and the read leg does not turn a blockquoted `||` row back into a table, so the second Markdown has the rows as literal text. The TracWiki written from it is the same as the first
  - **Tested**: write-leg cases for every shape above with exact expected TracWiki, the unchanged shapes with `master` bytes, read-leg cases for the blank line and its exceptions, and TracWiki -> Markdown -> TracWiki byte stability for the plain, multi-paragraph, table, code, list, nested-quote, nested-item, ordered, quote-then-text and quote-then-code shapes; live render checks on `/trac_test` (marked `live`, not run in this change). Three #143 tests that pinned the old blockquote output as its deferral (`test_blockquote_child_is_unchanged`, `test_paragraph_then_blockquote_keeps_its_blank_line`, `test_code_then_blockquote_keeps_its_blank_line`) were moved to the citation form. The live test finds the first top-level `<li>` by counting nesting depth (a non-greedy match stopped at an inner item's close), with a unit test of that helper on hand-made HTML. Seeded: with the citation form disabled 43 tests fail, with the adjacent separators disabled 43 fail, with the read-leg blank line disabled 7 fail; review fixes reverted in turn: the read-leg indent window 3, the nested-item citation reset 1, the `>`/list-marker check 3, the blank-line-in-previous-run check 2; all pass again once restored
- **`ticket_changelog` no longer strips a comment's leading whitespace from its text output (Trac /trac_mcp_server #155).** `_handle_changelog` built each comment from `newvalue.strip()`, and in TracWiki leading whitespace is markup (` * step one` is a list item, `* step one` is not), so an agent copying the text block back through `ticket_comment_edit` lost the list. The structured `newvalue` was already the stored bytes (#69); the text block now matches it apart from its own 4-space block indent
  - **What changed**: all trailing whitespace is stripped (`rstrip()`; leading whitespace stays) and lines are split on LF or CRLF, so a CRLF comment leaves no stray `\r`. A one-line comment that starts with whitespace takes the block form, so its indent stays visible under the block's. A whitespace-only comment prints `comment added`, like an empty one, instead of a bare `comment: ` line, and its structured `newvalue` keeps the stored bytes (`"\n"`, not `""`)
  - **Tested**: 4 new `TestHandleTicketChangelog` tests (leading whitespace kept on a multi-line comment, a one-line comment with leading whitespace in block form, trailing newlines only dropped, a whitespace-only comment). Three fail on the old code; the trailing-newline one guards existing behaviour
  - **Review fixes**: `rstrip("\r\n")` stopped at a space or tab, so `"Fixed it\n \n"` printed in block form with a trailing whitespace line; it is now `rstrip()` and prints `comment: Fixed it`. Lines were split on `\n`, which left a `\r` on every block line of a CRLF comment; they now split with `re.split(r"\r?\n", ...)` (not `splitlines()`, which also splits on `\x0b`, `\x0c` and U+2028 and so changes the stored text). The structured `newvalue` is unchanged. The blank test is now `not newvalue.isspace()` instead of `newvalue.strip()`: same result, no copy. Two new tests cover the trailing whitespace line and the CRLF comment; both failed before the fix.
  - **Out of scope**: GitHub PR 115 (#134) rewrites the wording of the same empty-comment branch and will need to merge `master`
- **A paragraph in a loose Markdown list item now stays inside the item (Trac /trac_mcp_server #143).** Trac ends a list at any blank line, and `markdown_to_tracwiki` kept a blank line before every paragraph child of an item. In `- a` / blank / `  para` / `- d` the `para` rendered as a blockquote outside the list and `d` started a new list. The same happened to text after a code or table child (the half of #143 that #144 left open)
  - **Paragraph after a paragraph**: the earlier paragraph's last line now ends ` [[BR]][[BR]]` and the next paragraph follows on a continuation line at the item's text column, with no blank line (` * a [[BR]][[BR]]` / `   para`). The leading space is always written: `Done![[BR]][[BR]]` renders as `Done[[BR]]` plus one break, because the `!` escapes the first macro, and `wiki:Foo[[BR]]` reads as one link target (#29). It applies in the item's first text run and in a later run alike. Measured on `/trac_test`: the paragraph renders inside the `<li>` with a visual gap, also nested, in an ordered list, and in a last item followed by text outside the list. A `{{{#!div}}}` around the paragraph also stays inside, but reads back as a code fence; a plain continuation line loses the paragraph break; both were rejected
  - **Text after a code or table child** goes on the next line with no blank line, so `tail` in `- a` / fence / `tail` / `- b` stays in the `<li>`, after the `<pre>` or the div-wrapped table. Not once a nested list has appeared in the item: Trac attaches a block with no blank line to the innermost open item, so the text would land on the nested item (and not round-trip); every run after a nested list keeps its blank line
  - **Passed-through TracWiki keeps its blank line**: two paragraphs are joined with ` [[BR]][[BR]]` only when neither has a line that starts (stripped) with `{{{`, `}}}`, `||`, `=` or `----`. Appended to `}}}` the separator would leave a `{{{#!html` block open and swallow the rest of the page (measured on Trac); a heading or a `||` row would take it as content. The same test keeps the blank line before a text run after a code/table child that starts with such a line
  - **Unchanged on purpose**: a paragraph after a nested list keeps its blank line (a Trac limit, #112; the `list_item_split` advisory, #125), as do a blockquote child (#145), a #63 fallback fence and any other non-paragraph block. Only paragraph-to-paragraph and code/table-to-paragraph boundaries change
  - **Read leg**: `tracwiki_to_markdown` writes a blank line between a table released from a list item's `{{{#!div}}}` and the item text that follows it, but only before plain item text (not before a fence opener, heading, quote or rule, and not before a list marker or a blank line). Without it a GFM reader takes the text for one more table row (spec example 202). The write leg still writes the text with no blank line after the closing `}}}`, so the round trip is byte-stable. A line at the item's continuation indent straight after a block's closing `}}}` is now read as item text also when no list item follows (end of input, or a blank line and a paragraph outside the list); it used to come back as a `tracwiki-unconverted` fence
  - **Tested**: write-leg cases for every shape, including a paragraph ending in `!` and one ending in `wiki:Foo`; the nested-list and blockquote shapes pinned unchanged; TracWiki -> Markdown -> TracWiki byte stability for the ` [[BR]][[BR]]` paragraph, text after a code block, text after a div-wrapped table and code + two paragraphs, each plain, nested and ordered; live render checks on `/trac_test` (marked `live`). Two earlier tests that pinned the old blank line were updated (`test_loose_item_paragraph_child_is_still_open_in_143`, now `test_loose_item_paragraph_after_the_block_stays_in_the_item`, and `test_two_trailing_paragraphs_keep_their_order`). Review fixes add write-leg cases for passed-through lines and for text after a nested list, byte-stable round trips for the last-item code and table shapes, and a read-leg case for the fence after a released table; the live test compares words on word boundaries and has a last-item case. Seeded: with the `[[BR]][[BR]]` join disabled 20 tests fail, with the no-blank-line rule after code/table disabled 16 fail; each review fix reverted in turn turns its own tests red (12, 2, 12 and 1); all pass again once restored
- **`ticket_get` and `ticket_search` show a field that was never set as empty, not as the text `None`; `structuredContent` carries `""`, not `null` (Trac /trac_mcp_server #141).** Trac's `ticket.get` returns the key with value `None` for an unset field, and the handlers read it as `attrs.get(name, "")`, whose default applies only when the key is absent. A ticket with no milestone or keywords printed `Milestone: None` and `Keywords: None | Cc: None`, and the structured result held `null`. Only `description` had been guarded
  - **`ticket_get`** reads `summary`, `status`, `owner`, `reporter`, `type`, `priority`, `component`, `milestone`, `keywords`, `cc` and `resolution` (as well as `description`) with `or ""`; **`ticket_search`** does the same for `summary`, `status` and `owner`. The `_ts` change token is unchanged
  - **Callers that tested those fields for `null` must treat `""` as unset**
  - **Tested**: a mocked `get_ticket` with every one of those fields `None` gives no `None` in the text and `""` for each field in `structuredContent`, with `_ts` unchanged; the search handler likewise. Seeded: reverting either handler fails its test
- **`wiki_search` is now a ranked keyword search; `prefix` filters before the ranking and `limit`/`cursor` page the whole result (Trac /trac_mcp_server #154).** It did not do what its schema said. `TracClient.search_wiki_pages_by_content` lowercased the whole query and kept a page only if that exact string occurred in it, in `getAllPages` order, unranked, and stopped at the first 10; `_handle_search` applied `prefix` after that cap. So `Source:` with `prefix=Rules/version_control/` found nothing although every page there holds it, `limit=50` returned 10 and no second page existed, and a question such as `why was my status change ignored` matched nothing. Each call also read the wiki page by page until 10 matched
  - **Ranking**: the BM25 that auto_pm:#257 measured (recall@5 0.967 against 0.233 for the old tool on its 30-question set), ported into the new stdlib-only `core/wiki_search.py` with its settings unchanged: k1 1.2, b 0.75, pages cut at every heading (Trac's rules, below) into sections of at most 4000 characters with the page name and heading put in front of each, tokens of two or more characters, stopwords dropped, and compound and CamelCase names also split into their parts (`CloseViaWorkflowAction` and `ticket_update` are found by `close`, `workflow`, `update`). A page scores as its best section. The query is words, not a phrase
  - **`prefix` before ranking, no 10-hit cap**: only the pages whose name starts with `prefix` are indexed for the query, so idf and the average length are those of the pages in scope (the arrangement that was measured). Every page with a score above zero is returned, ordered by (-score, page name) so a cursor's second page continues the first. Empty results keep the two "No wiki pages found" messages, with and without a prefix
  - **Output**: each hit shows its score to two decimals next to its snippet. The snippet is cut from the best section's body around the first query word it contains (about 50 characters either side, `...` on a cut end), or from the start of the section when no query word occurs literally, and is a byte-for-byte slice of the stored page: no line-ending or whitespace change, and never a piece of the synthetic "page > heading" line. The tool description says what it does (no longer "relevance ranking" for a substring match) and that it is not a phrase match. **No exact-substring mode** (the operator ruled it out)
  - **A per-client page cache replaces the page-by-page read**: `TracClient` keeps `{page: (version, lastModified, tokenized sections)}` (the instance pool keeps one client per instance and identity, so a cache never holds pages its caller cannot read), and a lock around refresh and index build. The first search reads every page with `system.multicall` batches of 50 (`wiki.getPageInfo` and `wiki.getPage` per page), or one call per page when the server has no multicall (remembered five minutes, like the plugin methods). Every search makes one listing call, `wiki.getRecentChanges` from the epoch (through `get_recent_wiki_changes`, which falls back to `getAllPages` + `getPageInfo` on a server without it), which Trac answers with every readable page's latest version and `lastModified`. That listing is the authority: a cached page that is not listed is dropped, and a listed page is re-read when it is not cached or when its (version, `lastModified`) pair differs from the pair `getPageInfo` returned when it was read. No time watermark is kept, so an edit made while the cache is being filled, a page deleted and recreated at the same version, a deleted latest version (the page falls back to an older, lower version) and a page with no version are all seen on the next search. The per-prefix indexes are rebuilt only when something changed. A page that cannot be read, or that faults inside a multicall, is skipped and tried again on the next search
