  - **`get_recent_wiki_changes(0)` now asks from the epoch**: `xmlrpc.client.DateTime(0)` is the current time (a falsy value means "now"), so the documented default of 0, "all changes", sent a range that held nothing. The wiki search lists every page with it
  - **API**: `TracClient.search_wiki_pages(query, prefix=None)` (dicts `name`, `score`, `snippet`) replaces `search_wiki_pages_by_content(query, max_results=10)`, which is removed; `_handle_search` was its only caller
  - **Tested offline**: the tokenizer, the section cut (a section's body and every long-section part are slices of the stored text, CRLF included), BM25 order and the exact formula, the best-section page score and ties by name; against a fake wiki behind a real `TracClient` and the real handler, the three replications from the ticket (a 30-page wiki whose `prefix` pages come after the 10th match; `limit=50` returns 30 and the cursor's pages are disjoint and concatenate to the full list; a multi-word query matches non-adjacent words), and the cache (an unchanged page is not fetched again and a repeat search is one call; an edited page is re-fetched and its old text is gone; a deleted page is dropped; a new page is added; an edit carrying the newest page's own `lastModified` is still seen; an edit made between two multicall batches to a page already read is caught; delete-and-recreate at the same version, a deleted latest version and a missing version are each re-read; the listing falls back without `getRecentChanges`; the index is rebuilt only on a change; multicall missing falls back and is remembered; one failed page is skipped, with and without multicall; 120 pages are read in three batches; concurrent first searches fill the cache once). Seeded: with the version comparison disabled the edit tests fail. The live test (`TestWikiSearchLive`) creates three `Scratch/` pages on `/trac_test`, searches, edits one (the new text is found, the old is not), deletes one and cleans up; it is left to `ci-live.sh`
  - **Splitter follows Trac's own rules (auto_pm:#268)**: the section cut skips every line inside a `{{{ }}}` block (`{{{` or `{{{#!processor`, nesting counted, closed by a line that is `}}}`), so a shell transcript or config inside a block is not cut into fake sections, and a heading is Trac's pattern (one to six `=`, then whitespace, then the text; the closing run of `=` and an `#anchor` are not part of the name), so a bare `======` line, `=foo`, `==NoSpace==` or seven or more `=` is not a heading. The measured arm used a looser pattern with neither rule; on the corpus that was measured this changes one page, so the shipped tool stays equivalent to it
  - **Review fixes**: (1) the cache is refreshed from a full metadata snapshot, described above, instead of a time watermark; (2) a `system.multicall` batch that fails for any reason but a missing multicall (a timeout, a dropped connection, another fault, an unparseable reply) is read page by page and the search goes on, with pages from other batches kept; (3) `_is_missing_method_fault` also accepts "no such method", on the same terms as "not found" (the text must name the method), and the search's own copy of that check is gone; (4) words are letters and digits of any script, so Cyrillic and accented words are indexed and `café` is one token (CamelCase parts are still cut by ASCII case, and ASCII text tokenizes exactly as before), and a query of nothing but stopwords and one-character words answers with a validation error saying it has no searchable words, not "No wiki pages found"; (5) the per-prefix index memo is a least-recently-used cache of 16; (6) the snippet anchors on the first whole-word occurrence of a query word (`ci` on `ci.sh`, not inside `decision`), then on the first raw occurrence, then on the start of the section

- **A threaded comment is now one comment number everywhere: `ticket_get` reports its own number plus `in_reply_to`, and `reply_to`, the write gate, `ticket_render_check` and the CLI accept it (Trac /trac_mcp_server #131).** In a `ticket.changeLog` comment row the `oldvalue` is `"5"` for a plain comment and `"2.5"` for comment 5 threaded as a reply to comment 2 (a web-UI reply). Trac takes the last dotted part as the number (`trac-change-5-` ids, `comment:5` links), but each site here parsed `oldvalue` by its own rule, so they disagreed on threaded rows
  - **One parser**: `TracClient.parse_comment_number(oldvalue)` returns `CommentNumber(cnum, reply_to)` or `None` (not a number, more than two dotted parts or an empty part, non-positive, float or bool). It parses with `int()`, never `isdigit()`
  - **`ticket_get`**: a threaded comment's `number` is `"5"`, not `"2.5"`, the key `ticket_render_check`'s `comment` parameter matches, and the comment gains `in_reply_to: "2"` (and `(reply to 2)` in the `### comment:5` heading). Flat comments are unchanged, with no `in_reply_to` key
  - **`ticket_update` `reply_to`**: `reply_to=5` now quotes a threaded comment 5 (it found nothing before); `"2.5"` is read as 5 too, and the quote links `[comment:5 author]`. An unparseable `reply_to` is `not_found`
  - **Write gate**: a threaded row now counts as an existing comment and feeds the "next number", so a `comment:5` link to it is no longer flagged `dangling_comment_ref` (rows like this were skipped)
  - **`ticket_render_check`**: a threaded comment's source now pairs with its `trac-change-5-` section instead of reporting `source_not_paired`
  - **CLI**: `ticket-pull --comment 5` finds a threaded comment 5, and `ticket-comment` reports a threaded new comment by its own number (it printed `2.5`)
  - **Tested**: the parser table, then each site on a flat row, a threaded row, an attachment pseudo-row (`oldvalue` `None`) and a non-numeric `oldvalue`. Seeded: against the old code the write gate's, `ticket_render_check`'s, `ticket_get`'s and `reply_to`'s threaded-row tests fail

- **`ci-live.sh`'s credential probe now walks up for `.env` as `tests/conftest.py` does, so the script runs in a git worktree nested in the main checkout (Trac /trac_mcp_server #128).**
  - **Cause**: the probe is a `python -` heredoc that called `load_dotenv(".env")`, which reads only the current directory. A worktree under `.claude/worktrees/*` has no `.env` (it is gitignored), so the probe exited 1 before pytest, although `conftest.py`'s bare `load_dotenv()` under `--run-live` walks up and finds the main checkout's `.env`
  - **Fix**: `load_dotenv(find_dotenv(usecwd=True))`. The heredoc has no file of its own, so `usecwd=True` starts the search at the repo root the script has already `cd`'d to and walks up from there: the same upward walk `conftest.py` makes, which starts one level down in `tests/`. The checkout's own `.env` still wins over one further up
  - **No more workaround**: the manual `.env` symlink into a nested worktree is no longer needed
  - **Tested** offline (`tests/test_ci_live_probe.py`) by running a copy of the real script at `parent/child/ci-live.sh` with a stub `pytest` first on PATH: a `.env` only in `parent/` is found (exit 0, stub ran); no `.env` anywhere still refuses (exit 1, "NOT run", stub did not run; skipped if an ancestor of the temp dir holds a `.env`); a `.env` in both takes the child's `TRAC_URL`. `PYTHON_DOTENV_DISABLED`, which `ci.sh` sets, is removed from the child's environment. Seeded: with `load_dotenv(".env")` restored, the first case fails and the other two pass
  - **Review fixes**: the precedence test now gives both `.env` files a complete credential set with distinct usernames and asserts the child's URL and username appear and the parent's do not, so a probe that merged the two files fails it (seeded and confirmed); the test's `python` is now a `#!/bin/sh` wrapper that `exec`s the interpreter rather than a symlink to it, so the interpreter runs from its real path and venv detection (`pyvenv.cfg` next to the executable) works as usual
- **A `{{{ }}}` block inside a list item now survives a Markdown round trip (Trac /trac_mcp_server #144).** `tracwiki_to_markdown` emitted the fence with its body and closer at column 0 and a blank line after the opener, so the block fell out of the item. `markdown_to_tracwiki` then glued the opener onto the item text (` * a{{{`) and turned the next item into code. The read leg now fences such a block at the item's text column. It follows Trac's own rule, measured on `/trac_test`: the block joins the innermost open list item at any indent, column 0 included, unless a blank line or flush-left text closed the list first. The body keeps exactly what Trac shows, which strips the opener's indentation only when every non-blank line carries it. The write leg puts a code child of a list item under the item text with no blank line before it, in tight and loose items alike (the code-block half of #143)
  - **A guard this exposed**: a numbered list starting at a number other than 1, directly after paragraph text, now gets a blank line before it. CommonMark does not let such a list interrupt a paragraph. Without the blank line, the code indented under its items became paragraph text, and writing back turned each block into a one-line inline code span
  - **Declined on purpose**: blocks under lettered or roman items, and under TAB-indented markers, convert as before. Markdown cannot express those lists. A code block written after a nested sub-list attaches to the sub-item, which is a Trac limit like #112's
  - **Recall gate**: a TracWiki -> Markdown -> TracWiki round trip over both stores (2,075 documents), on `master` and on the branch, with each write-back judged by Trac's render against the source's. 52 documents change and none loses a code block or a list item. 15 now match their source's structure, where they did not before. Live tests render the written-back shapes on `/trac_test` and assert the `<pre>` sits inside the `<li>`
- **A bracketed InterTrac realm link glued on behind another one (`]/[`) is now checked for an unconfigured prefix (Trac /trac_mcp_server #148).** In `[auto_pm:wiki:A]/[auto-pm:wiki:B]` the realm regex's `[^\s`]+` read past the first `]`, and `finditer` never overlaps matches, so both links became one token and the second was never checked on its own: its typo'd prefix rendered as plain text with no warning. This predates #147. A token that starts just after `[` now ends at `]`, since a bracketed Trac link target never contains one. An empty target before `]` (`[p:wiki:]`) also stops there, rather than falling through to the unbracketed form. The unbracketed form is unchanged, because Trac's unbracketed target can carry a `]` mid-token
  - **Dead code removed**: with the token stopping before `]`, #147's cut at the first `]`, the no-label branch's `]` strip and `_bracket_label`'s `raw.endswith("]")` shortcut became unreachable. A label-less bracketed realm token now takes the ticket form's path: the forward search finds `]` at the token's end. `_bracket_label` lost its now-unused `raw` parameter. The full suite was run with each of the three replaced by a `raise`, and none fired
  - **Tested** on two fixtures rendered live on `/trac_mcp_server`: two resolved glued links stay silent (and join `SILENT_ROWS`), and a typo'd second link warns exactly once, naming `auto-pm:wiki:Rules/testing/RecallGateEveryTightening`. That assertion failed on `master` and again with the regex change reverted. A regex-level test pins the token boundaries, including the empty-target shape
- **A label-less bracketed InterTrac realm link followed by `).` no longer draws a false `unconfigured_intertrac_prefix` (Trac /trac_mcp_server #147).** `([auto_pm:wiki:Rules/testing/SeededDefectFirst]).` resolves, but the realm regex's `[^\s`]+` read the token as `auto_pm:wiki:Rules/testing/SeededDefectFirst]).`: it no longer ended in `]`, so the no-label branch (#57) missed it and the check fell through to "unconfigured". A bracketed token is now cut at its first `]`, the bracket's close, since Trac's bracketed target never contains one
  - **Tested in both directions** on fixtures rendered live on `/trac_mcp_server`: the resolved link is silent, and the same shape with a typo'd prefix (`auto-pm`) still warns, its token now stopping at the bracket instead of carrying `]).` into the message. Both tests fail on the previous code
- **A fault after a successful `putPage` no longer triggers a second write, or reports a stored page as a failure (Trac /trac_mcp_server #140).** `wiki_file_push` wrapped its existence probe and its `put_wiki_page` call in one `try`, whose "not found" fallback created the page. `put_wiki_page` read the page back after `putPage` returned `True` inside the same `try` as the write, so a read-back fault escaped as if nothing had been written. An update whose read-back said "not found" (the page deleted in between) was therefore posted again as a create, recreating the page and reporting `action: created`. Any other read-back fault came back as an error for a page that was stored, so a caller that verifies its writes could not tell "nothing stored" from "stored, read-back failed". Found by reading the code; not reproduced live
  - **`put_wiki_page` separates the write from the read-back** (the "distinct exception" option, not "return what is known"). Once `putPage` has returned `True`, any read-back failure (a fault, "not found" included; a transport error; or a non-dict reply) raises the new `WikiPageStoredReadbackFailed`. It carries `page_name`, `url` and the original error as `cause` (also chained as `__cause__`). It is neither a `Fault` nor a `ValueError`, so no existing "not found -> create" or "write rejected" handler can match it, and a caller that does not know about it still sees an error rather than a success with an invented version. Every other exception from `put_wiki_page` still means nothing was stored. A read-back fault containing "version" or "not modified" is no longer translated into "Version conflict" / "Page not modified" for a page that was stored
  - **`wiki_file_push` keeps the not-found fallback to its probe**: the write sits outside that `try`, posts `putPage` exactly once, and never falls back to a create. A stored write whose read-back failed is a non-error result with `readback_failed: true`, `version: null` and `readback_error`, and text saying the page WAS stored and not to push again. Every push result now carries `readback_failed`
  - **`wiki_create` and `wiki_update`** return the same "WAS stored, version unknown" text as a non-error result. The capability probe (`detection/processor_utils.py`) treats a stored test page as stored: it renders it and deletes it instead of leaving it behind
  - **CLIs**: `trac-mcp wiki-push` exits **4** (`EXIT_STORED_INCOMPLETE`, as `ticket-comment` already does) when the server reports `readback_failed`; exit 1 still means nothing was stored. `trac-convert --to-wiki` exits with the new code **5** (`EXIT_TRAC_STORED`) with a "WAS stored ... do not retry" message; exit 4 still means nothing was stored. Both are documented in the CLI exit-code docstrings/comments, `docs/reference/cli.md` and the README
  - **Tested**: a real `TracClient` with `_rpc_request` mocked so `putPage` returns `True` and the following `getPageInfo` raises. With `Fault(1, "Page 'X' not found")`, `putPage` is posted exactly once, with the existing page's version 3. With `Fault(1, "Internal error")`, the result says the page was stored. Both tests failed on `master` (two `putPage` calls, the second a create without a version; and `server_error`). Each also fails with only the client half reverted (read-back re-raising its own fault). The client, `wiki_create`/`wiki_update`, both CLIs and the processor probe each have a test that failed with its change removed
  - **Review fixes**: the tool registry maps an escaping `WikiPageStoredReadbackFailed` to a `stored_readback_failed` error that says the page WAS stored and not to retry, never to `server_error`'s "retry later" (defence in depth: every current caller catches it). `trac-convert --to-wiki` prints the conversion warnings on exit 5 too, since the page was stored
  - **Left for later**: #129 (a server-side `putPage` version precondition) and #130 (flagging a post-write version that isn't version + 1). The read-back is now its own step after the write, which is where #130's check belongs

- **`wiki_get_history` on a page that does not exist now returns `not_found`, not `server_error: 'int' object has no attribute 'get'` (Trac #121).** Trac's `getPageInfo` answers a missing page with `0`, not a fault, and the handler read it as a dict. It now gives `wiki_get`'s answer, `Wiki page "NAME" does not exist`, the same check `wiki_diff` already made
- `ticket_get` failed with `sequence item 9: expected str instance, NoneType found` for a ticket whose description was never set: Trac returns `None` there and the response lines are joined as strings. `milestone_get` read its description the same way; both now treat `None` as empty
- **The write gate no longer refuses a valid same-page `attachment:` link, and no longer warns about an InterTrac prefix on a qualified one (Trac #113).** The pre-write render is `wiki.wikiToHtml`, which has no page or ticket to resolve against. Measured on the live server, every `attachment:` form renders there as `<a class="missing attachment">LABEL</a>` with no href -- `[attachment:x.jpg label]` and bare `attachment:x.jpg` alike -- so `missing_local_target` (an error) blocked the write. Separately, `_PREFIX_REALM_RE` read the `x.jpg:wiki:Page` tail of Trac's own `attachment:x.jpg:wiki:Page` as an InterTrac `prefix:realm:target`
  - **`gate_or_refuse` takes `wiki_page`, and looks up the target's attachments** (`list_wiki_attachments`, or `list_ticket_attachments` for `ticket_id`) only when a field contains `attachment:`. The names thread through `check_write` and `build_warnings` as `attachment_names`; `None` (every caller with no target, and any list call that raises) is no context and behaves exactly as before. `ticket_batch` checks each ticket's own list; a new ticket or page has nothing attached
  - **Cleared by count, not by name**: each bare `attachment:FILE` / `[attachment:FILE label]` token in the source (code spans and fences blanked) whose file is attached clears ONE `missing attachment` anchor, the technique `incidental_wiki_autolink` uses. A token whose file is not attached, a qualified form that is genuinely missing, a quoted filename and a `missing wiki` anchor all stay errors
  - **The InterTrac fix blanks the whole `attachment:` token rather than adding a lookbehind**: a lookbehind alone blocks the match at `x` of `attachment:x.jpg:wiki:P` and the engine restarts at `jpg`, so every dotted filename still warned. A real `foo:wiki:Page` beside an attachment link still warns
  - **Seeded defects** (budget never decremented; token blanking off; qualified form counted; list failure falling back to a permissive set; the `!` escape guard removed) each fail their tests
  - **Review fixes to the two regexes**: one shared filename class stops at the delimiters Trac stops at (`<>,;()`), a lookahead keeps `attachment:ab:wiki:Page` from backtracking into the bare token `a`, an escaped `!attachment:x` spends nothing, trailing `.!?` is dropped from a bare link's name, the InterTrac blanking no longer swallows a prefix glued on after a comma, and a failed attachment lookup is logged as a warning

- **The deploy pins are tracked, as `constraints/deploy.txt` (Trac #109).** `deploy-constraints.txt` was never committed. Its presence was also `deploy.sh`'s "am I the deploy clone" guard, so the only copy of the pins was an untracked file in `debian`'s deploy clone, and a fresh clone (kpoxa's, 2026-10-03) could not install. The pins are now tracked, byte-identical to that copy plus a header. `deploy.sh` has its own two guards instead, checked before anything is pulled or installed. The host must have the `trac-mcp-server-http.service` user unit, which turns kpoxa's rc.d deploy away to its own procedure on the store card wherever its clone is; `systemctl`'s own error stays visible, so a missing user session bus does not read as a missing unit. Then the directory must be `~/srv/trac-mcp-server-live`
  - **Renamed rather than tracked under the old name:** a tracked `deploy-constraints.txt` would make the next `git pull` in both deploy clones abort with "untracked working tree files would be overwritten", because each already holds an untracked copy at that path. The old untracked file is now unused and can be deleted after the first deploy
  - `tests/test_deploy_pins.py` fails if a runtime dependency, direct or transitive (walked through installed metadata, following extras such as `mcp[cli]`), has no pin, or if a pin falls outside its `pyproject.toml` specifier. CI never installs with the constraints, so that mismatch would otherwise surface only on the deploy host, after the pull. It also runs a copy of `deploy.sh` with stub `git`/`systemctl` to show that a checkout carrying the pins is refused on a debian-shaped host and a kpoxa-shaped one. Each check was seen failing on a seeded defect: an unpinned direct dependency (`python-dotenv`), an unpinned transitive one (`httpx-sse`), `mcp` pinned below its specifier, a guard keyed on the pins file again, no unit guard, and the guards in the opposite order
  - **Not pinned by the file, and now said so in its header:** dependencies only another platform or Python version pulls in, and the build backend, which pip's build isolation installs from `[build-system]` regardless of `-c`. Both were unpinned before this change too
- **`ticket_update`'s description no longer says comments can't be edited (Trac #107).** The sentence went stale when the `tracrpc_comment` plugin added `ticket_comment_edit`/`ticket_comment_delete`, and agents that read it posted a second correcting comment instead. The description now points at those tools; two code comments, a module docstring and a test docstring that repeated the claim are corrected, and `tests/test_mcp/tools/test_tool_descriptions.py` pins it. A running server shows the new text only after a reinstall and restart.
- **A lettered or roman list no longer reaches the Markdown as prose (Trac #74).** Trac's ordered-list marker is not only `1.`: measured on its own renderer, ` a. x` is `<ol class="loweralpha">`, ` A. x` `upperalpha`, ` i. x` `lowerroman`, ` I. x` `upperroman`. CommonMark's is digits only, so `tracwiki_to_markdown` left those lines untouched — and untouched means a **paragraph** to the next reader. Confirmed against the write leg's own parser rather than the spec: mistune reads `" a. first\n b. second"` as one `paragraph`, and the same input with `1.`/`2.` as a `list`. `wiki_file_pull` was therefore handing callers a file in which a list had become prose, with an **empty warning list**, and any edit-and-push cycle then reasoned about prose
  - **Ticket #63's verbatim fallback, the third construct to take it** after processor blocks and #73's indent widths: the run goes out unchanged in a `tracwiki-unconverted` fence, comes back byte-for-byte, and **warns**. The stored bytes were already nearly clean before this — only the leading space was lost, which is render-neutral because Trac accepts a list at column zero — so the warning is the half of this that genuinely failed first, and the tests assert it separately rather than as a detail of the byte comparison
  - **Renumbering to `1./2./3.` was rejected explicitly and is pinned as rejected.** It would change what the page says, and the write leg could not tell a rewritten list from one that was numbered all along, so one edit-and-push cycle would make the change permanent
  - **One definition of the marker grammar, not two.** #73's `_LIST_MARKER_RE` and the new `_ALPHA_LIST_MARKER_RE` are built from the same fragments, so the subset relation holds by construction; a test asserts it in both directions, because a quote scanner and a fallback reading the same grammar out of two places is how they drift
  - **A run holding both kinds falls back whole**, the operator decision from #63 applied unchanged — a numbered list with a lettered item nested inside it (Trac's own `WikiFormatting` page, and 8 of the 22 marker lines in the store corpus) is one `<ol>` containing another. The cost is that a representable numbered list is carried verbatim too; it still beats the old behaviour, where the nested item lost its indent and came back a sibling rather than a child
  - **Scope measured, not assumed.** Across 1020 documents on both stores: 22 marker lines in 4 documents, **all indented, none at column zero**. The column-zero form is lost the same way and is **Trac #89**, split out deliberately — its grammar differs (a marker line opens a list mid-paragraph, a column-zero continuation line ends it) and this fix removes the mechanism that used to manufacture it, since the indented form now comes back indented
  - One pinned assertion from #73 is deliberately reversed: `test_no_list_is_buried_in_a_verbatim_fence` no longer covers the ` a. a` / ` iv. a` rows, because for them the fence is the fix rather than the damage. The exclusion names those two rows, so a scanner that started fencing ` * a` still fails there
- **A cross-instance ticket reference is now probed at all (Trac #82).** `is_probeable_wiki_href` matched `/intertrac/wiki%3A` and nothing else, so a `prefix:#N` short link was never fetched — not capped, not skipped, simply invisible, and `missing_cross_instance_target` was structurally incapable of firing for a dead one. Measured across 1020 documents on two stores: **695 cross-instance ticket references in 124 documents, none checked**, against 305 wiki-realm references in 105 documents that were
  - **Both dispatcher shapes, because the second was found by rendering rather than by reading.** `prefix:#N` becomes `/intertrac/%23N` and the realm form `prefix:ticket:N` becomes `/intertrac/ticket%3AN`; 10 of the corpus's references take the second, and a fix keyed on `%23` alone would have left every one of them invisible — the same false negative one door over, which is the #70 → #77 → #79 pattern this belongs to
  - **The ticket realm classifies by the opposite rule from the wiki realm.** A wiki page returns HTTP 200 whether or not it exists, so that check reads the body; a ticket that exists returns 200 and redirects to `/ticket/N`, while one that does not returns a bare 500. So existence is cheap and certain, and *absence* is not: a 500 is indistinguishable from the remote instance being down, misconfigured or unreachable
  - **Hence a control probe, without which this should not ship into a blocking column at all.** Before any 500 is called missing, the same instance is asked for a *wiki*-realm dispatcher target, which answers 200 whether or not that page exists. Control 200 → the instance is up and the credentials work, so the 500 is evidence; anything else → the candidate degrades to `target_check_skipped`, reported as unchecked, never as a broken link. Without it, one unwell instance would turn every cross-instance ticket link in a document into an error and, under #64, refuse the write while telling the author to fix links that are fine
  - **The control is deliberately not `prefix:#1` on the remote instance**, which the ticket suggested: a deleted ticket 1 fails the control permanently and silently disables the whole check, and a gate that always reports uncertainty looks exactly like one that always passes
  - **Classified on status, not body**, because that is the part that is about Trac rather than about one install: this host answers a missing ticket with an empty 500, `trac.edgewall.org` answers the same 500 with a 9 KB error page. One control request per distinct instance per call, and only when something on it came back 500 — a document whose cross-instance ticket links are all live costs nothing extra
  - **Measured before choosing a severity, per #79's precedent.** All 247 unique cross-instance ticket targets in the corpus resolve; zero are dead. So this ships at `error` alongside the wiki realm with no new blocking findings — and, because the live corpus is clean, the check is trusted only on the strength of seeded defects (`Rules/testing/SeededDefectFirst`), not on a green run
  - **Recall gate, both parts reported.** Same corpus, before and after: every other code identical to the finding — `bare_ticket_ref` 2525, `missing_local_target` 174, `incidental_wiki_autolink` 110, `unconfigured_intertrac_prefix` 106 — and 86 documents would be refused, unchanged. `target_check_skipped` rises 105 → 188 by construction, because 83 more documents are now known to carry cross-instance targets that a `check_targets=false` run did not verify. That code is `info` and non-blocking, so the refusal count is untouched
  - Known boundary, documented rather than assumed away: this host enforces authentication at the web layer (401, which takes the control down with it), so a permission denial cannot reach the 500 path. A Trac using fine-grained `TICKET_VIEW` restrictions might answer 500 for a ticket that exists but is invisible to the probing account, which would read as missing
  - `scripts/store_sweep.py` gained two harness fixes found while running the gate: it loads `.env` and reports missing credentials by name instead of dying on `KeyError: 'TRAC_URL'`, and its `--json` `examples` keys are now emitted in sorted order — they followed set-iteration order, so two runs of the *same* code diffed as if something had changed, in the one script whose whole purpose is a mechanical diff
- `probe_targets` no longer leaves the END of a document unchecked (Trac #80). The cross-instance target probe capped at 10 fetches and kept the **first** 10 in document order, so whatever it dropped was at the bottom of the page — which is exactly where an agent appends. A gate whose blind spot is the content just written is inverted relative to what a write-time gate is for, and Trac #64 is about to make these checks blocking. Measured live: a document with 12 distinct dead cross-instance targets reported only 10 of them; the last two came back `target_check_skipped` at `info` severity, which does not block
  - **The cap was 10 because the probe was sequential, not because probing is expensive** — 50 targets at the 5s timeout is 250s of wall clock in a `for` loop. Probes now run concurrently (8 workers), so the default cap rises to **50**, above anything measured in real content: across 998 documents on two stores the densest was 15 unique probeable targets, and that was a stock Trac help page
  - It stays a cap rather than becoming unbounded, and `target_check_skipped` still fires beyond it and still names the hrefs — a raised bound is precisely where a bound quietly becomes no bound
  - **New `target_cap` parameter** on `convert_preview`, `ticket_render_check` and `wiki_render_check`, defaulting to 50, for a deliberate audit of an unusually link-dense page
  - `client.session` is now read inside each worker rather than hoisted. `TracClient` hands out a *thread-local* session, so hoisting — harmless while the probe was sequential — would have given every worker one thread's session and quietly defeated that design
  - `stats.targets_checked` now counts targets actually fetched rather than entries in the probes dict, which included skipped ones. A capped run reporting "12 targets checked" is the same shape the `SKIPPED` status exists to prevent
  - Still open, and deliberately not fixed here: `target_check_skipped` remains `info` and therefore non-blocking, which under Trac #64's gate is the same "no errors means certified clean" shape that ticket's section 3 refuses to ship. Raising the cap shrinks the exposure; it does not close it
- `missing_local_target` no longer reports a CamelCase word Trac auto-linked out of ordinary prose as a broken link (Trac #79). Two entirely different things render as `<a class="missing wiki">` — an authored link to a page that does not exist, and any humped word in a sentence, which Trac's WikiFormatting linkifies whether or not anybody meant it as a reference — and the render carries no signal separating them. Measured across both stores, 110 of 291 findings were the second kind, and 38 correct documents would have been refused outright once Trac #64 promotes this code to a blocking error. One of them is a comment whose offending text is `"WiFi"/"LoRa"` inside a sentence quoting Trac #27: the gate would have blocked a write for describing the defect it exists to catch
  - **The incidental population keeps its signal, at `warning`.** New code `incidental_wiki_autolink`, advisory rather than blocking, carrying the `!` escape for the exact word as a fix suggestion. `missing_local_target` keeps its name, its `error` severity and the 181 authored findings
  - **The discriminating information is in the source, so the check now reads it.** An anchor is incidental only if its text equals the page name in its href (a label was typed by a person), the name is one Trac would actually auto-link, and it still occurs bare once code spans, fenced blocks, `[[...]]`, `[target label]` and realm references are blanked out
  - **Three gates, not one, because the obvious single test fails silent.** `See [[Page]]. The Page is missing.` renders exactly one anchor — from the authored, dead `[[Page]]` — yet `Page` does occur bare afterwards, so "does the text still occur bare" alone would downgrade a genuinely broken link. The shape gate is what keeps that an error
  - **Counted per target rather than decided per document.** A document carrying both `[[TracClient]]` and the prose word `TracClient` reports one error *and* one advisory; a boolean would answer "yes, it occurs bare" for the whole document and lose the dead link — Trac #70's residual through a new door
  - **The auto-link shape was measured, not reasoned about.** Twenty probes against the live daemon are pinned as `MEASURED_AUTOLINK_SHAPES`; `PyVISA` is among them because Trac #37 is this project's evidence that a hand-reasoned CamelCase rule goes wrong on an acronym tail. Where the measurement is ambiguous the gate stays narrow, since too narrow leaves today's false positive while too wide would hide a real dead link
  - **No source means no downgrade.** `build_verify_warnings` passes an empty source when `render_check` cannot pair one with the render; every anchor then stays an error rather than being downgraded for lack of evidence
  - New `scripts/store_sweep.py` replays `build_warnings` over a whole store offline, which is how the recall gate was run: 291 findings before, 181 + 110 after, every other code unchanged to the finding. The corpus it reads is cached locally and deliberately not checked in — this repository is public
- `unconfigured_intertrac_prefix` no longer false-warns on a configured prefix inside an inline code span (Trac #61): `_PREFIX_REALM_RE`'s trailing `\S+` did not stop at a backtick, so a token whose code span was followed immediately by non-whitespace — a table cell delimiter being the common case, since `||` never has a space before it — ran past the closing backtick and swallowed the next cell. The suppression path then compared a token longer than any rendered code span, found no containment, and reported a correct, configured prefix as unconfigured. Same class as #59's fenced-block gap, at the other delimiter; `_PREFIX_TICKET_RE` was never affected because it ends at `\b`
- `install.sh` now installs both `dist/` binaries, not just `trac-mcp-server` (Trac #54): it previously hard-coded a single binary name, so `trac-convert` on `PATH` was never refreshed by the normal build-and-install cycle — a copy placed there by hand once could silently drift 15+ days and a behavior change (the #37 fix) behind `dist/trac-convert`, with `--version` reporting the same string for both and unable to tell them apart
- `markdown_to_tracwiki` converter sweep (Trac #19, #20, #27, #29): four defects found and fixed together since they're all in the same `TracWikiRenderer`
  - **#19** — `[MACRO: Name(args)]`, the placeholder `tracwiki_to_markdown` emits for a macro it can't otherwise represent, now converts back to `[[Name(args)]]` instead of passing through as dead literal text; a `wiki_get` → edit → `wiki_update` round trip no longer permanently flattens a page's macros (this also affected `[[Page]]`-shaped links before #28's fix). `[[...]]` syntax typed directly in Markdown source now survives unchanged too. Both had to be shielded as sentinel placeholders on the *raw* Markdown source before mistune parses it — mistune splits an unresolvable `[...]` span into separate text fragments internally, so shielding from inside the leaf `text()` renderer isn't reliable
  - **#20** — An empty table cell (the usual shape for a row-label column) now renders as a lone space instead of a bare `||`; two adjacent `||` (`||||`) is TracWiki's colspan-2 marker, not two empty cells, and was shifting every following header/cell left by one column
  - **#27** — Plain-prose CamelCase-shaped words (`WiFi`, `LoRa`, ...) are now defensively `!`-prefixed, since Trac's WikiFormatting auto-links any such word into a broken missing-page link and Markdown has no equivalent auto-link concept to signal intent either way. Scoped to genuine prose only — code spans/blocks, a link's own display text, and macro/link names inside `[[...]]` are all unaffected
  - **#29** — A hard line break (`[[BR]]`) immediately after a colon-valued token (e.g. `substrate:trac`) now gets a leading space; Trac's wiki-link grammar otherwise greedily consumes `[[BR]]` into a failed `wikiname:target` TracLink parse instead of recognizing it as the line-break macro
- `tracwiki_to_markdown` no longer corrupts `[[Page]]` WikiLinks read via `wiki_get`/`wiki_search` (Trac #28, critical): plain double-bracket links were being routed through the unknown-macro placeholder mechanism, which silently discarded single links and, when two or more placeholders landed near each other, merged them into one unterminated construct with a leaked raw `\x00` byte
  - `[[Page]]` / `[[Page|Label]]` are now recognized as WikiLinks (matching Trac's own macro-name-registry-first resolution) and convert to real `[text](wiki:Page)` Markdown links; only a small allowlist of genuine Trac macro names, or any `[[Name(args)]]` carrying explicit arguments, still go through the macro path
  - Fixed two latent greedy-regex bugs this exposed: the macro-placeholder restore pass and the single-bracket `[url text]` link pass could each span past their own closing token into a neighboring link/macro when several appeared close together

## [2.2.0] - 2026-07-26

### Added
- New `trac-convert` CLI binary (second `[project.scripts]` entry point) — standalone TracWiki ↔ Markdown converter usable outside the MCP server
- `--from {md,tracwiki,auto}` and `--to {md,tracwiki}` format flags with `auto` detection via `converters.common.detect_format`
- stdin → stdout as default I/O mode (Unix-pipe friendly, binary-safe, no spurious trailing newlines)
- Positional `FILE` argument and `-o/--output FILE` flag for file I/O with clear errors on missing/unwritable paths
- `--from-clipboard` and `--to-clipboard` flags via `pyperclip` for cross-platform clipboard I/O (Linux/macOS/Windows/Wayland)
- `--heading-anchors {strip,preserve}` and `--unknown-macros {bracket,preserve}` flags exposing converter kwargs (keyword-only so 7 MCP tool call sites keep working unmodified; wrong-direction flags silently ignored)
- `--verbose/-v` and `--quiet/-q` mutually exclusive flags: `-v` emits three `info:` lines to stderr (source format, bytes read, bytes written); `-q` suppresses warnings
- Standardized exit codes: `EXIT_OK=0`, `EXIT_RUNTIME_ERROR=1` (I/O, clipboard, mutex), `EXIT_USAGE_ERROR=2` (argparse), `EXIT_CONVERSION_ERROR=3` (exceptions inside `convert_text()`)
- Full integration test suite for `trac-convert`: 9-cell source×destination I/O matrix (stdin/file/clipboard × stdout/file/clipboard), md↔tw roundtrip fixtures with expected-divergence assertions, cross-mode warning routing, UTF-8/CRLF/empty-input edge cases, 10 subprocess entry-point smoke tests exercising real OS pipe fd separation and console_scripts wiring (988 passing, up from 741 in v2.1.0)

### Changed
- `pyperclip>=1.8.2` added as a **required** dependency (not optional) — enables clipboard I/O out-of-the-box on all platforms
- Tool count remains 27 MCP tools (unchanged from v2.1.3); `trac-convert` is a separate binary, not an MCP tool

## [2.1.3] - 2026-07-25

### Added
- Batch ticket operations: `ticket_batch_create`, `ticket_batch_delete`, `ticket_batch_update` -- best-effort processing with per-item results and bounded parallelism via `gather_limited`
- `TRAC_MAX_BATCH_SIZE` environment variable (default: 500, range: 1-10000) for controlling maximum items per batch operation
- Config path resolution (`resolve_config_path()`) and bootstrapping (`ensure_config()`) utilities in config_loader.py
- YAML config file support via `config_loader` and `config_schema` integration in server lifespan -- discovers `.trac_mcp/config.yaml` with hierarchical loading
- Three-source configuration precedence: CLI arguments > environment variables > config file
- 5 new tests for YAML config loading path in `test_lifespan.py`
- Package version display in test script output
- Shared error translation utility (`translate_xmlrpc_error`) consolidating 5 duplicate implementations
- Shared timestamp formatting utility (`format_timestamp`) with timezone-aware UTC
- Shared constants module (`constants.py`) for tool handlers
- Network timeout (10s connect, 60s read) on XML-RPC requests
- 79 new tests covering TracClient methods, error handlers, auto_convert, and logger (781 -> 860 -> 641 after sync removal)
- `CHANGELOG.md` version history extracted from planning documents
- `severity` parameter now supported on `ticket_create` and `ticket_update` (previously accepted but dropped)
- `ticket_update` now forwards `summary`, `description` (with Markdown→TracWiki conversion), and `type` ticket attributes (previously accepted but dropped)
- `ticket_update` now forwards Trac workflow actions: `action` (e.g. `accept`, `resolve`, `reopen`) plus `action_<action>_<action>_<field>` workflow input fields (e.g. `action_resolve_resolve_resolution`). Pattern-based forwarding for `action_*` keys, since action names are workflow-config-dependent. Enables clients (e.g. auto-pm) to drive Trac workflow transitions through the MCP `ticket_update` tool instead of being silently dropped
- `core/client` now parses XML-RPC `<base64>` values, decoding them to Python `bytes` instead of dropping or mishandling them (#12)
- MCP tool wrappers for ticket attachments: `ticket_attachment_put`, `ticket_attachment_get`, `ticket_attachment_list`, `ticket_attachment_delete` — push, fetch (with byte-identical retrieval), enumerate, and remove ticket attachments via the MCP layer, with `TICKET_ADMIN`-aware permission diagnostics on delete (#10)
- MCP tool wrappers for wiki attachments: `wiki_attachment_put`, `wiki_attachment_get`, `wiki_attachment_list`, `wiki_attachment_delete` — push, fetch (with byte-identical retrieval), enumerate, and remove wiki page attachments via the MCP layer, with `WIKI_DELETE`-aware permission diagnostics on delete (#11)

### Changed
- Modernized typing across all source files to Python 3.10+ style (`X | None` instead of `Optional[X]`, built-in generics)
- Renamed `TRAC_ASSIST_CONFIG` env var to `TRAC_MCP_CONFIG` (backward compatible with deprecation warning)
- Updated copyright from OpenCode to nerpa.tech in LICENSE and pyproject.toml
- Added PyPI project URLs (Homepage, Repository, Issues, Changelog) to pyproject.toml
- Updated all documentation (configuration, deployment, CLI, troubleshooting, README) to reflect YAML config file support
- Unified `max_parallel_requests` default to 5 (was 2 in TracConfig)
- Moved `max_batch_size` from standalone function to TracConfig field with Pydantic validation
- Consolidated redundant wiki tool tests (457 -> 158 lines, 65% reduction)
- Reorganized test files into consistent `tests/test_mcp/tools/` directory structure
- Migrated 2 test files from unittest.TestCase to pytest style

### Fixed
- `ticket_get` now includes keywords, cc, reporter, and resolution fields in both text and structured JSON output (were previously omitted)
- Lazy logger formatting (`%s` instead of f-strings) in lifespan and system modules
- `ConversionResult` type mismatch in wiki resources (was passing object as string)
- `set_client()` signature to accept `TracClient | None` (removed type: ignore)
- Removed License classifier conflicting with PEP 639 (`setuptools >= 77.0` derives it from `license = "MIT"`)
- Dead code removal: duplicate `get_version()`, unused validator loop, stale imports
- Live test configuration: `.env` now loaded in conftest for `TRAC_URL` availability
- Markdown -> TracWiki converter no longer mangles sentinel-shaped markers like `[auto-pm: state NEEDS_CODE]`; non-URL-shaped links are emitted verbatim instead of being wrapped as broken wiki links (#8)

### Removed
- Sync subsystem: `doc_sync` and `doc_sync_status` MCP tools, `src/trac_mcp_server/sync/` module, and all sync-related tests (213 tests)
- `scripts/test_trac.py` live test script and associated report
- Sync config schema models (`SyncMappingRule`, `SyncProfileConfig`) and sync profile support in `UnifiedConfig`

## [2.1.0] - 2026-02-15

Post-extraction cleanup release. Hardens the standalone package after splitting from trac_assist v1.3.2.

### Changed
- Rewrote all documentation for standalone usage (README, configuration, deployment, troubleshooting, tool reference)
- Removed orphaned trac_assist references from source, comments, and docstrings
- Cleaned import paths and removed unused dependencies (cssselect, anyio from direct deps)
- Promoted pydantic and charset-normalizer to direct dependencies
- Standardized all error returns to MCP CallToolResult format (fixed isError boolean bug)
- Bumped setuptools requirement to >=77.0 for PEP 639 support

### Added
- Config validation: URL structure, max_parallel bounds (1-20), whitespace rejection
- .env.example with all supported environment variables
- MIT LICENSE file with PEP 639 SPDX metadata
- PyInstaller build.sh and install.sh scripts for standalone binary distribution
- GitHub Actions CI workflow (lint, test, live-test, build jobs)
- Local ci.sh script mirroring CI checks
- Ruff linter (E4/E7/E9/F/B/I rules) with zero-violation baseline
- Comprehensive test coverage: 741 tests (up from ~200 extracted tests)

### Fixed
- isError field in error responses now correctly set to True (was always False)
- Detection tests relying on trac_assist fixtures
- Stale monolith references in test_trac.py integration script

## [2.0.0] - 2026-02-14

Initial standalone release. Extracted from trac_assist v1.3.2 as independent MCP server package.

### Added
- 24 MCP tools (tickets, wiki, milestones, system)
- Wiki page resources via MCP resource protocol
- Markdown to TracWiki bidirectional format conversion
- stdio transport for MCP client integration
- Environment variable configuration (TRAC_URL, TRAC_USERNAME, TRAC_PASSWORD)
- `trac-mcp-server` CLI entry point
- Independent test suite
